# Sluice v1 Conformance Ledger

**Target authority:** [v1 Architecture and Contract Reference](../explicit-io-v1-final-decision.md), revision v1-r3.
**Starting implementation baseline:** `c64f005e6e59e791f26a7ab4a594c33954f096dd`.
**Purpose:** track implementation and evidence, not add or relax contracts.

This ledger starts a new assessment. Previous architecture CLOSED/CONFORMANT
labels do not transfer. PR #389 changes documentation only; it does not implement
RequestCore, new Request APIs, progress integration, or shutdown behavior.

Formal evidence is governed by the
[v1 formal evidence map](v1-formal-evidence-map.md) (issue #391): existing
Lean/TLA+ assets are HISTORICAL / ADAPT / RETIRE_FROM_V1_EVIDENCE and none of
them is v1 evidence; future protocol and liveness closures must record their
evidence in the map's closure format. No row below changes status because an
old proof exists.

## Status vocabulary

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| NOT_ASSESSED | No complete assessment against these target requirements recorded |
| GAP | A specific missing/nonconforming behavior has been identified with source evidence |
| IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED | A candidate implementation exists; required evidence is incomplete |
| VERIFIED | Named requirements passed the recorded evidence for specified commits/configurations |
| DEFERRED_OUT_OF_SCOPE | Not a required v1 capability; no supported-product claim |

VERIFIED never means universal correctness. A required v1 item may not use
DEFERRED_OUT_OF_SCOPE. Split a row if only part has evidence. Keep safety,
conditional liveness, memory visibility and kernel evidence separately visible.

Per-request physical retirement: consumption reclaims the slot and the backend reports zero live cookies/workers. | Shutdown and destruction | SHUT | VERIFIED | **[E2 candidate, #401/#452 — candidate stage; merged ≠ VERIFIED until the recorded review]** The five-state lifecycle (Open → AdmissionClosed → Settling → ExecutionClosed → Destroyable) is implemented as the context settlement driver (`AsyncIoContext::drive_settlement_`, ADR-0004): `close_admission`/`request_stop`/`shutdown` on the context, the `AsyncBackend` settlement seam (`internal_work_retired` pure; `stop_execution`; `retire_execution_resources`), core occupancy extended with execution/control/episode/publication facts, `collect_outstanding` + `retire_delivered_episodes`, notification retirement, and destructor inversion — internal retirement obligations (D4: the consumed zero-op control pin, the late cancel control) are owned by the settling driver instead of being misclassified as caller violations, while external caller preconditions (live bindings D1/D2, live progress/host registrations D3) remain always-on fail-fasts, and the binding diagnostic names its violation. Contract: ExecutionClosed ≠ Destroyable — retained published results stay observable and consumable after execution close, and re-shutdown is idempotent (V20); shutdown reports completed / health_unresolved / health_failed and refuses under live host notification interest or a non-owner thread — the owner check is unconditional, including the idempotent execution-closed return (THREAD-01 × SHUT-03 composition, ADR-0004 §6); the shutdown entry performs the external-interest check and the drive-domain acquisition in one `access_mtx_` critical section, and `progress_notification_fd()` refuses a new borrow while a drive is active or execution is closed, so a borrowing host can never observe the notification fd this same shutdown retires (V23; mutant M11 killed by the parked-drive discriminator); a `request_stop` racing an in-flight drive upgrades the running drain into cancellation before the next pass (THREAD-01/PROG-04/SHUT-02 step 2; the driver re-reads `stop_policy_` every pass); the destruction paths observe zero public bindings and close admission in one critical section under the context mutex, so a binding landing mid-settlement fail-fasts instead of being frozen into the settled set; poison with full local retirement converges through real failure terminals while poison with a stuck in-flight borrow reports unresolved health and keeps execution open (V22/C4); the notification retires only after episode retirement, and a descriptor that reuses the retired number receives no stale wake (V23/C5). Oracles: `shutdown_lifecycle_test` — 9 ThreadPool / 17 io_uring named scenarios covering V20/V21/V22/V23/V26, the D-matrix rows, the C3 admission-close dual winner at the core seam, the mid-drive stop-upgrade oracle (real-kernel pipe read that can never complete; killed the pre-fix driver, alarm-bounded), the parked-drive borrow-linearization discriminator `shutdown_blocks_new_notification_interest_after_drive_linearization`, the observer settlement path, and the post-close owner-authority oracle `shutdown_from_non_owner_after_execution_close_is_refused` (owner completes and its repeat returns the recorded outcome; a non-owner post-close call is invalid_state — killed by mutant M12, which restores the execution-closed fast path ahead of the owner check); formal `formal/tla/ShutdownCore.tla` (safety invariants including `InvCanceledRequiresTargeting` — a canceled terminal is reachable only through a real cancel action — plus four reachability certificates, the M4 holding-form certs, and the conditional liveness pair LiveOpsSettle/LiveShutdownCloses — LiveShutdownCloses is the full SHUT-03 convergence statement (admission closed and not destroyed ~> execution-closed) proved under the explicit owner/backend fairness conjunction, with the one-shot `episode_claimed_once` observer abstraction (OBS-01: one registration per request; SHUT-02: new attachment closes during shutdown) so no fair ClaimEpisode/RetireEpisode alternation can evade the close — and each property is separately violated on the fairness-free Spec by its own negative-control configuration (ShutdownCoreLiveOpsNoFair.cfg, ShutdownCoreLiveCloseNoFair.cfg), so neither liveness claim rides on the other's fairness; mutants M1/M4/M6/M7/M8 mirrored) run by the E2 stage of `scripts/verify_tla.sh` (pool-parallel TLC, `SLUICE_TLA_JOBS`×`SLUICE_TLA_WORKERS`); the twelve-mutation campaign M1–M12 killed — nine by named C++ assertions or named-diagnostic aborts (`shutdown_mut_*` builds), three by named model invariants. NOT_ASSESSED does not imply the baseline has no useful implementation or evidence.
It prevents partial or older evidence from silently becoming certification of
the new target. The implementing PR records the precise source/evidence.

A row moves off NOT_ASSESSED only through a recorded review below. A GAP row
means a specific missing/nonconforming behavior was identified with source evidence;
it does not mean the whole slice is unimplemented.

## Evidence record template

For each reviewed slice, record:

| Field | Required content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | Exact IDs and accepted workload; explicit exclusions |
| Implementation | Commit SHA, code paths and public API/configuration |
| Change | Current behavior, target behavior and compatibility impact |
| Semantic/regression evidence | Test names/commands, result and output artifact or durable CI link |
| Protocol/model evidence | Model-to-C++ mapping, explored bounds, safety/liveness properties and assumptions |
| Publication/thread evidence | Synchronization argument and relevant instrumentation/configuration |
| Backend/kernel evidence | Backend, compiler, kernel/liburing, filesystem, injected faults and limitations |
| Status | New status and remaining gaps; never close a required slice with skipped tests |

## Phase gates

Use MIG-01 for the normative sequence and VERIFY-04 for named counterexamples.
Implement evidence with the changed protocol. Phase B already includes shutdown
and observer-reference obligations even though full integration closes later.

1. A: semantic oracle and direct path.
2. B: core, handles, publication/reclaim and teardown skeleton.
3. C: host-neutral observation and persistent progress integration.
4. D: bounded scope and optional host cleanup.
5. E: required operations, cancellation, both backend profiles and shutdown closure.
6. F: compatibility/public-surface retirement after consumer migration.
7. G: measured optimization preserving verified obligations.

Release evidence must enumerate actual supported configurations and pass W-01,
W-02, W-03, and W-04 for any supported optional host. This file is intentionally
not a claim that those gates have already passed.

## A1 review record — Issue #392, shared File semantic oracle

This record uses two axes, and they must not be conflated:

- The **assessment table** above uses the ledger status vocabulary.
- The **gap map** below uses the A1 slice's own coverage vocabulary:
  `CONFORMING` (this slice verified that rule on that path with the cited
  evidence), `GAP` (a specific divergence, with an owner), `IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED`
  (a candidate or reference exists, evidence incomplete), `OUT_OF_SCOPE`.

`OUT_OF_SCOPE` in the gap map means *this slice did not own that requirement*, not
that v1 does not require it. A required capability is never recorded as
DEFERRED_OUT_OF_SCOPE anywhere in this file; where a required capability is simply
not assessed yet, the gap map points at its assessment-table row instead.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | SEM-02 open rules; SEM-03 access matrix and validation precedence steps 1–4; SEM-04 offset/length/range; SEM-05 primitive and composition rules; SEM-06 durability rules; ERR-01 canonical mapping; ERR-02 effect representation. Excludes SEM-01 operation matrix closure, SEM-07 metadata/identity, direct composition helpers, and every request-side responsibility |
| Implementation | `include/sluice/detail/file_semantics.hpp` is the single semantic authority. `src/file_resource.cpp`, `src/blocking_file.cpp`, `src/async/file.cpp`, `src/async/async_io_context.cpp`, `src/async/threadpool_backend.cpp` and `src/async/uring_backend.cpp` call the validation rules and the error mapping; none of them re-derives File legality. The composition, effect and durability rules have no production consumer in this commit |
| Change | `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR` now map to `not_found` (was `permission_denied`). Embedded NUL is rejected before the native open (was silently truncated). An unrepresentable offset is `invalid_argument` (was `invalid_state` for the legacy reader). The last addressed byte is validated without overflow and a transfer above the native count is an invalid range (io_uring had no such check). `detail/io_validation.hpp` became `detail/uring_submit.hpp` because its remaining content is io_uring mechanism, not File validation. Review corrections: ECANCELED falls back to `backend_error` per ERR-01, since the root assigns it no canonical category and the semantic `canceled` outcome comes only from CANCEL-01 dispositions; buffer presence is no longer claimed as an SEM-03 rule and each raw-pointer surface keeps its own null-buffer fail-fast; effect certainty is derived from dispatch evidence rather than read/write direction; durability supersession requires strict ordering plus a conflicting mutation |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `semantic_errno_mapping_test` (14 table cases, table consistency, real `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR`/`EACCES` failures), `semantic_open_test` (18-combination legality table, oracle-versus-real-open cross-check, NUL rejected pre-open), `semantic_range_test` (13 range cases cross-checked against wide-arithmetic restatement), `semantic_short_io_reference_test`, `semantic_effect_outcome_test`, `semantic_durability_reference_test`, `semantic_validation_precedence_test` (21 scenarios), `semantic_reference_case_test` (V01–V03, V15–V17, V27) |
| Protocol/model evidence | None claimed. A1 is a table/property slice; the request lifecycle model belongs to Phase B |
| Publication/thread evidence | None claimed. This slice does not change publication or threading |
| Backend/kernel evidence | Direct, ThreadPool and real-kernel io_uring (`--liburing=y`, 8-case smoke passing) are compared against the same scenario table. io_uring unavailability is reported NOT RUN, never as a pass. `/dev/full` is a fault-injection device outside the PROD-02 regular-file domain and is used only to produce a deterministic write failure |
| Status | Shared File semantic oracle: IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED. Validation rules and the error mapping are called by every execution path; the composition, effect and durability rules are a reference model with no production consumer yet, and their tests prove properties of that model rather than of a path. Direct path: CONFORMING for the tested rules and scenarios below, except the zero-op no-OS-call half of SEM-03 step 3, which stays IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED (inspection-only evidence) until #393; the direct scope as a whole is therefore not CONFORMING. Request paths: GAP, see the map. **[A2 update, later commit]** the composition rules now have a production consumer and the zero-op no-OS-call half is CONFORMING; see the A2 review record below. The effect and durability rules remain a reference model |

### A1 open items recorded rather than decided

- SEM-05 requires composition to stop with a "no-progress failure" without naming
  a canonical category. The oracle reuses `invalid_state` as the closest existing
  category and adds no new category. The root does not specify this mapping, so
  it is recorded here as an ambiguity rather than treated as settled.
  **[A2 update, later commit]** that reuse is a projection of the canonical stop
  state rather than the composition authority; the public direct outcome does not
  adopt it. See the A2 review record.
- Buffer presence is not an SEM-03 rule: SEM-03 treats caller memory validity as
  not generally dynamically detectable. Each raw-pointer surface keeps a
  null-buffer-with-nonzero-length fail-fast as its own implementation
  precondition, outside the shared contract; a surface whose interface takes
  spans does not need one, and native-to-semantic refinement of such cases
  belongs to #400.
- An allocation failure (`std::bad_alloc`) reaching the host boundary has no
  canonical category. `no_space` is used as the closest existing one, but ERR-01
  describes `no_space` for the filesystem and no requirement assigns this case,
  so the mapping is recorded rather than treated as settled.
- The oracle rejects only what is not natively representable. A filesystem may
  still refuse an accepted offset near the native maximum; that refusal is an
  operation result with native detail, not a validation rejection.
- V16 and the request-side half of V15 and V27 need request-path capability
  (an unobserved write followed by a sync, and a terminal that can carry an
  unaccounted remainder). Their rules are asserted; their production evidence is
  owned by #400 and is not claimed here.

### A2 limitations and recorded boundaries

- **No-progress representation and the composition authority.** The authority
  for a stop is the shared state itself — `CompositionStop`, the accumulated
  `confirmed_bytes` and the primitive's own error — decided once by
  `detail::compose_progress`/`compose_error` and published by each path as its
  own representation. `detail::composition_error` is one such representation: a
  reference `IoError` projection with test consumers and no production consumer,
  not a second authority. The direct outcome carries a structured
  `write_no_progress` stop instead of adopting that projection's `invalid_state`,
  so the A1-recorded SEM-05 ambiguity is not canonized in the public contract.
  The projection keeps its reference-model choice, and a root decision on the
  category would move the projection without moving the canonical stop state.
  Because the two representations differ over two stop reasons, they are driven
  side by side from the same injected primitive sequence by
  `every_stop_reason_is_pinned_against_the_oracle_error_rule`, which pins the
  canonical stop state on both sides and records the split: `complete`,
  `primitive_error` (reason and native detail identical) and `impossible_count`
  agree, while `eof_before_full` and `write_no_progress` differ only in
  representation, the direct side reporting no `IoError` where the reference
  projection names one.
- **Effect certainty (ERR-02; SEM-05's OS-error row).** `CompositionOutcome`
  carries `remaining`, and `detail::composition_effect_certainty` decides it from
  the shared state: a completed, EOF-before-full or no-progress stop accounts for
  its remainder, while a primitive error and an impossible count report
  `unknown`, in both directions. Direction is deliberately not the
  discriminator, for the reason `failed_dispatched_attempt` already records — a
  failing read may have filled part of the caller's borrowed buffer (LIFE-01),
  and a failing write may already have reached the file. The write side has a
  concrete Linux counterexample: `write(2)` documents an error reported for a
  writeback failure, and the NFS write path (`nfs_file_write`, `fs/nfs/file.c`)
  returns a negative result after `generic_perform_write()` accepted bytes —
  either through `generic_write_sync` or through the writeback-error check that
  overwrites a positive result. The earlier claim recorded here, that a `-1`
  return from a direct regular-file primitive proves the attempt transferred
  nothing, is therefore withdrawn as too broad: v1 does not exclude network
  filesystems from the regular-file domain. The accounted side is limited to
  stops whose primitive reported a successful count. A direction-sensitive
  refinement (a failing read accounting for itself) is deliberately not claimed,
  because it would need a per-platform primitive contract with evidence rather
  than another assumption. With the field present the direct outcome is no longer
  a prefix-only terminal in the sense of `detail::prefix_only_terminal_can_carry`;
  the narrower shape that predicate names stays the request-side terminal. The
  field is pinned at every stop, and in both directions, by
  `direct_composition_fault_test`.
- **Cursor-surface range scenarios.** A shared-cursor call supplies no offset, so
  the offset-range step has no operand there: five scenarios whose verdict
  depends on the caller's offset (two range cases, two closed/access cases and
  one accepted-past-EOF case) are reported as not drivable on that surface. The
  length half of the range rule is covered on both surfaces.
- **Durability.** The direct integration sequence (completed write, resize grow
  and resize shrink, each followed by `sync_data`/`sync_all`) is evidence of the
  observable half only. It is not a power-loss durability proof, and the
  request-side half of the V27 row keeps its #400 owner.
- **Durability coverage of a completed resize (A1 row).** Its direct half is now
  CONFORMING; the row keeps its `IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED` status for the
  request-path evidence owned by #400.
- **Open A1 items untouched.** The `bad_alloc` category (#399), the request-side
  effect/cancel representation, and the undisclosed io_uring transfer limit
  remain as recorded; this slice does not change root, error categories or
  request paths.

## B1-1 review record — Issue #394, standalone RequestCore protocol substrate

This record covers the standalone substrate only. The assessment-table row
above stays NOT_ASSESSED for the production surface: no production request
path, backend or public handle changed in this slice, and #394 remains open
for the B1-2+ integration slices. A fresh-context adversarial review returned
`B1_1_FAIL` on the first revision; Corrective-1 fixed the four blockers
(publication-target last access, settled-work control acquisition, health's
absent acceptance effect, unconstrained E capability chain) and re-ran all
evidence; the numbers below are the post-correction state. Detailed evidence,
the happens-before argument, the mutation matrix and the section 13
correspondence tables are in
[b1-1-request-core-evidence.md](b1-1-request-core-evidence.md).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | REQ-01 (identity = context+slot+generation, generation advance and exhaustion retire, copied-ID lookup/cancel token stale after public release even while internal pins live); REQ-02 (one acceptance linearization point serialized with admission close, reservation is not acceptance, rollback leaves no residue, descriptor/borrow facts copied into core-owned bounded storage, accepted-but-undispatched stays discoverable); REQ-03 substrate halves (orthogonal lifecycle facts rather than one enum, at most one immutable terminal, publication gated on retirement of every borrow-touching reference, control records may outlive publication while incapable of touching borrowed state); REQ-04 substrate halves (publication as a release boundary: payload write then ready release-store as the publisher's last target access, consumer acquire pair, no result revision after publication); REQ-05 (reclaim predicate over the substrate's public-binding/E/C/publication facts, handle release before internal retirement pins without blocking the releaser, slot reuse reinitializes under the generation discipline; the observer-delivery conjunct joins at #396 integration); REQ-06 core-local settlement liveness; BOUND-01/02 (bounded slot table, transient capacity vs permanent exhaustion); the identity-validity basis of HANDLE-01; VERIFY-03 L1/L5 core-local halves; VERIFY-04 V04 and V05 substrate halves, V09, V24 (request-id generation facet) and V26. Excludes the public Request/handle API, observers, progress, backends, shutdown and every production migration obligation (B1-2 onward, #395–#401) |
| Implementation | `include/sluice/async/detail/request_core.hpp` + `src/async/detail/request_core.cpp`: a bounded, address-stable, mutex-serialized `RequestCore` (detail namespace) with reservation/acceptance/rollback, admission close and admission health (a health failure closes the same admission gate `admission_open()` reports; both refuse new reservations and re-refuse acceptance at the commit point), cancel arbitration, terminal selection with the admissibility floor, a frozen E capability chain (acceptance installs the first E, the last E retires only once a terminal holds the outcome, no new E after a chosen terminal), C pin acquisition refused on settled work, publication begin/complete with an outside-lock caller-target window, synchronous final-release reclaim, and generation advance to permanent retire. Canonical terminal representation is `sluice::detail::IoOutcome` (success with confirmed bytes, failure with confirmed prefix and effect certainty, `canceled_before_dispatch`). Tests: `tests/request_core_test_driver.hpp` (PublicationTarget with seal-before-ready ordering, the ready release-store contract, and post-ready/post-destroy/misordered publisher-access audits; FakePhysicalDriver deterministic physical-event injection), `request_core_protocol_test.cpp` (34), `request_core_publication_test.cpp` (11), `request_core_consumer_probe.cpp` (2, incomplete-type host-neutrality asserts plus a public-surface lifecycle). Build: three additive targets in `xmake/tests.lua`, each compiling the substrate TU and linking only `sluice_core`. No AsyncIoContext/ThreadPool/io_uring/Scheduler/Fiber/WaiterToken/RoutingLease/ProgressSource wiring exists |
| Change | Additive only. No production file's behavior changed; production admission, `Completion` authority and `RequestHandle` are untouched and remain the old authority until the B1-2+ migration slices. No requirement, error category or public contract was amended |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `request_core_protocol_test` 34/34 and `request_core_publication_test` 11/11 (debug and ASan+UBSan via the clang toolchain; the project-wide `asanubsan` mode cannot use gcc on this host because libasan is absent), `request_core_consumer_probe` 2/2, full suite 37/37 in this configuration, `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Load-bearing case names are enumerated in the evidence note (admission/close race and health-gated admission incl. the between-reserve-and-accept refusal, generation aliasing and exhaustion, terminal floor incl. pre-claim cancel and health flag, the E-chain capability rules incl. last-ref premature-release rejection and post-terminal acquisition freeze, settled-work control refusal, pin-gated reclaim with B/C/P-last variants, publication ordering, release racing the publication epilogue with the target destroyed mid-window, the last-publisher-access order/destroy audits, effect-certainty representation for post-accept and pre-claim failures, zero-op) |
| Protocol/model evidence | `formal/tla/RequestCore.tla` (first liveness-carrying model; `FairSpec` with six `WF_vars` conjuncts on named service actions; Corrective-1 adds `NoteHealth`, `AcquireExec`, the RetireExec last-ref guard, and the explicit AcquireCtl settled-work guard) + 20 cfgs gated by `scripts/verify_tla.sh` stage B1-1: clean safety (6,165,549 states generated / 996,436 distinct / 0 on queue, 16 invariants incl. the new `InvNoAcceptAfterHealth`, `InvExecHeldUntilTerminal`, `InvNoExecRevival`), clean liveness (`PROPERTY L1 L5` checked, no violation), 14 invariant-killed mutants, 3 temporal-killed mutants (`MutStrandPostAccept`, `MutLazyReclaim`, and Corrective-1's `MutCtlAfterSettled`, the formal reproduction of the settled-control blocker), and 1 clean separation witness (`MutReadyBeforePayload`, subsumed by the E-chain rule), each kill matched against the exact violated invariant line. Bounds: 2 slots, GenMax 2, MaxCtl 1. Full correspondence tables in the evidence note's section 11 |

Cross-thread instrumentation and TSan are explicitly not claimed (deterministic single-thread tests only); multi-threaded publication evidence belongs to the public-surface/threading slice |
| Backend/kernel evidence | None claimed. The substrate is host-neutral and involves no backend, kernel or filesystem; physical events are injected by the fake driver |
| Status | Production admission and slot lifecycle: NOT_ASSESSED, unchanged (this record adds substrate evidence only; ThreadPool/io_uring request paths remain the current authority until their migration slices). The standalone substrate's protocol obligations — V04/V05 substrate halves, V09, V24 request-id facet, V26, L1/L5 core-local under declared fairness — carry the recorded deterministic-test and TLC evidence above for this branch. ## B1-A review record — Issue #394, context-owned RequestCore and context identity domain

This record covers ownership, identity provenance and the migration seam only.
The assessment-table row stays NOT_ASSESSED: no production request lifecycle
moved, so no production request is RequestCore-owned after this slice.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | REQ-01 (one context identity domain with explicit no-wrap/exhaustion behavior; context identities must not alias while live; a raw address is not identity across destruction/recreation), ARCH-01/ARCH-02 (`IoContext` owns RequestCore; the backend must not own it, and must not allocate public request identity), BOUND-01 (`request_capacity` is a context budget; exhaustion semantics) and HANDLE-03 (a context ID prevents cross-context identity confusion; no registry and no safe-handle promise). Excludes every production cutover obligation (B1-B ThreadPool, B1-C io_uring), the public `Request<T>`/handle API, observers, progress, cancellation, shutdown, and the A1/A2 direct-path rules |
| Implementation | `AsyncIoContext` (the root's `IoContext` before the public rename) gains `std::unique_ptr<detail::RequestCore> core_`, allocated in its constructor and transferred untouched by both move operations, so the core object's address, slot table, generation state and identity survive a legal context move. `include/sluice/async/detail/context_identity.hpp` + `src/async/detail/context_identity.cpp` (listed in `xmake/libraries.lua` because the `src/async/*.cpp` glob misses `detail/`) hold the single identity domain: `claim_context_identity(cursor)` is the domain algorithm and `allocate_context_identity()` is its fail-fast production entry point. `src/async/detail/request_core.cpp` joins the library for the same reason, since the context owns the core in production. Backend seam `AsyncBackend::adopt_context_identity(detail::ContextIdentity) -> std::size_t` is a **pure** private virtual (`friend AsyncIoContext`, mirroring `resolve_identity_state`): every backend states which slot table carries the context identity and its capacity, or explicitly returns 0 for "no slot table". `RequestArena` no longer receives an identity at construction and gains `adopt_context_identity`; `ThreadPoolBackend` and `UringAsyncBackend` (including its no-liburing stub) implement the seam. `arena_context_identity()` and `context_core_for_test()` are `SLUICE_ASYNC_INTERNAL_TESTING` surface. Evidence target: `tests/request_core_ownership_test.cpp` |
| Change | Context construction now allocates one identity from the single domain, installs it into the backend slot table, and constructs the context-owned core with that identity and the reported capacity; a context move or move assignment transfers the same core instead of re-homing state. The two backend-local identity namespaces (`next_backend_id()` with the magic bases `0x54500000` / `0x55720000`) are deleted; disjointness between backends is now structural (one counter) instead of conventional. The identity cursor latches exhaustion before handing out its last value, so no claim can follow a wrapped counter. `RequestHandle`, `resolve_identity_state`, `identity_of`, `supports_request_identity()`, `Completion` authority, the arena slot lifecycle and all publication behavior are unchanged. Production ThreadPool and io_uring requests still route exclusively through `RequestArena`: the core is idle for them (evidence below) |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `request_core_ownership_test` 8/8 (ThreadPool) and `request_core_ownership_uring_test` 9/9 (real ring), full suite 42/42 with `--liburing=y` in debug and release, 38/38 in debug without liburing, targeted ASan+UBSan with leak detection over both ownership binaries (8/8 and 9/9; the project-wide `asanubsan` mode still cannot link gcc on this host because libasan is absent, so the sanitizer run uses the clang toolchain), `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Load-bearing cases: `move_transfers_the_same_core` and `move_assignment_transfers_the_same_core` (same core object, identity, capacity, accepted slot and generation across a context move and across a move assignment; the moved-from/move-assigned source holds no second core; the moved-in slot table and core carry the same identity, and a request submitted afterwards resolves through it), `backend_slot_table_carries_the_context_identity` (two backends constructed without a context share one unadopted slot-table identity; each adopted slot table carries its own context's identity; distinct contexts hold distinct identities), `foreign_and_released_identities_do_not_resolve` (own context resolves, foreign context rejects, empty handle rejects, identity survives a context move, a released identity stops resolving), `production_request_is_not_request_core_owned` (backend slot in use and terminal ready while every core fact stays zero: no mirrored reservation, acceptance, terminal, publication or binding; the core admission gate is untouched), `adoption_seam_carries_no_lifecycle_authority` (a recording backend adopts nothing at construction, adopts exactly once at context construction, the core carries the identity the backend received plus the capacity it reported, and neither adoption nor the move changes a canonical core fact), `domain_identities_are_not_reused_across_contexts` (eight live contexts, all nonzero, pairwise distinct), `identity_domain_terminates_at_exhaustion` (a fresh cursor hands out 1 then 2 and never repeats; a cursor seeded at the last value hands that value out once and then reports exhaustion on every further claim instead of wrapping), `uring_context_carries_the_context_identity` (same identity, capacity and no-mirror facts through the real io_uring backend; the case reports a skip, counted separately from passes, when the ring is unavailable) |
| Mutation evidence | Eight production mutations were applied, force-rebuilt (`xmake build -r <target>`) and run against both ownership binaries; each was killed by a named assertion or by the compiler, and the unmutated baseline passed after restore. The force-rebuild is per-target and that target compiles its own async translation units and links only `sluice_core`, so it never links `sluice_async` and the stub-archive hazard above does not apply to these runs. Move (constructor) re-homes a fresh core → `move_transfers_the_same_core`. Move assignment re-homes a fresh core → `move_assignment_transfers_the_same_core`. Slot table ignores the adopted identity → `backend_slot_table_carries_the_context_identity` (and the move-assignment identity pairing). Identity domain returns a constant → `backend_slot_table_carries_the_context_identity`, `domain_identities_are_not_reused_across_contexts`, `move_assignment_transfers_the_same_core`. Identity claim falls back to a plain `fetch_add` → `identity_domain_terminates_at_exhaustion` (the domain re-hands-out 1 after the boundary). Core budget hardcoded instead of the reported capacity → the three capacity assertions. Production acceptance mirrored into the core → `production_request_is_not_request_core_owned` (named failures, then a normal failing exit: the case drains the core it observed so the violation is reported as a FAIL rather than masked by a destruction terminate). A backend omits the seam → rejected at compile time, since the seam is pure virtual |
| Protocol/model evidence | No formal state added, and no TLC configuration re-run. B1-A does not touch `src/async/detail/request_core.cpp` or its header, so the B1-1 model's actions and invariants still correspond to the same C++ transitions. The context identity domain is outside the modeled protocol: `RequestCore.tla` carries `Identity(s) == [slot, gen]` for a single implicit core, and a foreign or unadopted context is rejected by an identity comparison before any modeled action runs — the same class of pre-state rejection as an out-of-range slot index. Modeling context identity would add a state dimension with no request-lifecycle transition to constrain, so it is deliberately not invented here |
| Publication/thread evidence | No new lock and no changed happens-before edge. The identity domain adds one relaxed atomic read-modify-write whose only cross-thread property is unique-value allocation: two contexts constructed on different threads receive distinct identities, and the exhaustion marker is latched with a compare-exchange, so no ordering carries any other state. The identity is written into the backend slot table inside the context constructor, before the context is reachable by any other thread, and it is immutable thereafter: `RequestArena::context()` and `adopt_context_identity` are the only accessors, adoption has exactly one call site (the context constructor, on the calling thread) and no worker, poller or delivery path reads the field, so no legal interleaving pairs an adoption with a read and no mutex is added for one. The context move remains the pre-existing `unique_ptr` transfer under the caller's normal external synchronization. Adoption returns no owner, borrows nothing, and the backend never receives the core, so no delivered interleaving is affected. Cross-thread instrumentation and TSan are not claimed; deterministic single-thread evidence only, as in B1-1 |
| Backend/kernel evidence | ThreadPool evidence is a real backend with a live worker. io_uring evidence uses the real kernel ring (`UringAsyncBackend::available()` true on this host, liburing 2.13); when no ring is available the io_uring case is reported as skipped and counted separately from the passing cases, never as a pass. The io_uring case reads a real file; no filesystem-behavior claim is added and the A1-recorded io_uring gaps stay with #400. Both backend headers and both no-liburing stubs are compiled by the configurations above. Reproduction note: the local `xmake -r` rare rebuild can leave `libsluice_async.a` built from the no-liburing stub objects; that reproduces on the base commit, is unrelated to this slice, and is avoided by an ordinary build or `xmake clean -a` |
| Authority-contraction audit | Removed as a direct consequence of the identity move: both backend-local identity namespaces (`ThreadPoolBackend::next_backend_id()`, `UringAsyncBackend::next_backend_id()` with their magic bases) and the arena's construction-time identity parameter. **No production request authority moved.** `RequestArena` remains the only accepted-set authority for production requests, and every legacy backend lifecycle mechanism — arena slot states, ready ring, dispatch queue, worker lifecycle, cancel intent, waiter/routing leases, publication bindings — stays required, because the same requests still run through it. `ContextIdentity::for_testing` stays: it is test-only construction for the standalone B1-1 suite, not production authority |
| Status | Context-owned RequestCore, the single context identity domain and the migration seam: VERIFIED for the requirement scope named above at the head of this record's PR, under the configurations listed in the evidence rows (deterministic tests, mutation kills, ASan+UBSan, real-kernel io_uring). Production admission and slot lifecycle: NOT_ASSESSED, unchanged — ThreadPool and io_uring requests remain on `RequestArena`, verified mechanically by the idle-core assertion, not merely by inspection. Open and explicitly not claimed here: ThreadPool cutover (**B1-B**), io_uring cutover (**B1-C**), the core handoff half of the seam (no consumer exists yet; B1-B extends this seam when its cutover needs the core), the public `IoContext` rename, and formal coverage of context identity |

### B1-A mechanism justification (AGENTS.md gate)

Every runtime item this slice adds, against the gate's questions: what fact it
represents and which authority owns that fact; what requirement or failure
justifies it; whether it is derivable; its classification; and which mechanism it
replaces, with the condition that permits removal.

| Mechanism | Fact / owning authority | Justification | Derived? | Classification | Replaces; removal condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AsyncIoContext::core_` (`unique_ptr<RequestCore>`) | This context owns that RequestCore (owner: `IoContext`) | ARCH-01/ARCH-02; REQ-01 stable identity across legal moves. Indirect because `RequestCore` is non-movable (address stability plus a mutex), which would otherwise make the context non-movable | No | Authority (ownership) | Replaces "no core in production"; removed only if the core ceases to be context-owned, which no v1 requirement allows |
| `claim_context_identity` + the domain cursor | The single context identity domain and its cursor value (owner: the domain) | REQ-01: one domain, no aliasing while live, exhaustion fails before wrap can make an old identity valid; HANDLE-03 | No | Authority | Replaces both backend-local `next_backend_id()` counters; no removal condition (the domain is the v1 authority) |
| `kContextIdentityExhausted` (0 as the sticky marker) | "This domain has terminated" (owner: the same cursor) | REQ-01's no-wrap clause: without the latch, any state that survives an exhaustion would resume at 1 and alias a live context | No; it is cursor state, not a summary | Authority | None; it is the same counter, not a second mechanism |
| `adopt_context_identity` seam + the three implementations (incl. the no-liburing stub's explicit `return 0`) | Which slot table carries that identity and what its capacity is (owner: the backend slot table; the identity value belongs to the domain) | ARCH-02 (the backend must not allocate public identity) and BOUND-01 (the core must be sized by the table it will serve); pure virtual so no backend can silently keep an unadopted table | No; the slot table is backend-owned state the context cannot compute | Migration seam (temporary) | Replaces the arena's construction-time identity argument; removable once B1-B/B1-C move the request lifecycle into the core and the seam has no consumer |
| `RequestArena::adopt_context_identity` + `ContextIdentity context_{}` | The identity the arena validates its requests against (owner: the context, installed into the arena) | REQ-01/HANDLE-03 cross-context rejection; the arena must reject a foreign context's handle | No; but no new state — the same field, installed instead of constructed | Compatibility state | Replaces the constructor parameter; removed with the arena itself under B1-B/B1-C |
| `context_identity_exhausted_fail_fast()` | The domain's exhaustion diagnostic (owner: the domain cursor) | REQ-01 requires explicit exhaustion behavior rather than silent reuse | Yes: it reports the cursor's marker and adds no independent fact | Derived diagnostic | None; same family shape as `request_arena_generation_exhausted_fail_fast` |
| `RequestCore::context_` / `capacity_` (B1-1 state, reused) | Summaries of the once-installed identity and the reported capacity (owner: the domain and the slot table) | Lets the core reject foreign identities and bound its own table | Yes | Derived summary (non-authoritative; kept, not a second source of truth) | None in this slice |
| `context_core_for_test()` / `arena_context_identity()` (macro-guarded) | Observation of this slice's ownership and identity facts (owner: the production objects) | This slice's evidence need: stable ownership and provenance must be asserted mechanically, not by inspection | Yes | Test-only evidence | None; withdrawn with the B1-A/B1-1 test targets |
| `CoreCaseCleanup` in the evidence target | Drains the core a failed case observed (owner: the test harness) | Keeps a named assertion failure the reported evidence: without it, destroying a non-quiescent core terminates and masks the violated assertion | No; test-harness tooling outside the production binary | Test-only evidence | None; test tooling |




## B1-B review record — Issue #394, ThreadPool atomic RequestCore authority cutover

This record covers the first production authority move. After this slice every
ThreadPool request's acceptance, identity, accepted-set membership, execution
responsibility, terminal arbitration, canonical result, publication,
public-binding release and reclaim belong to the context-owned `RequestCore`;
ThreadPool owns only physical dispatch/execution facts. io_uring is untouched
and remains on `RequestArena` until B1-C.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | REQ-01 (identity = context+slot+generation allocated by the core; public lookup/cancel token stale immediately after public binding release even while a publication pin holds the slot), REQ-02 (single acceptance linearization under the core's serialized authority; validation before reservation; reservation is not acceptance; post-accept dispatch failure converges through the terminal path; accepted-but-undispatched work keeps an owner), REQ-03/REQ-04/REQ-05 production halves for ThreadPool (publication gated on borrow-touching retirement; the `Completion<T>` ready release/acquire pair as the compatibility visibility edge; reclaim under the core's canonical predicate with synchronous final-pin reclaim), SEM-03 steps 3–7 precedence and the zero-op immediate-publication rule on the request path, INV-02 (bounded ThreadPool submit/cancel initiation), ARCH-02/BACKEND-01 (backend owns physical execution only), BOUND-01/02 (capacity and queue bounds), HANDLE-01/03 basis (public binding semantics without the final `Request<T>` API), VERIFY-03 L1/L5 ThreadPool-mapped environment assumptions, VERIFY-04 V04/V05/V09/V24/V26 production ThreadPool halves. Excludes io_uring cutover (B1-C), the public `Request<T>` API (#395), Observer (#396), ProgressSource/no-lost-wake (#397), the full cancel/effect matrix (#400), shutdown state machine (#401) and any queue/lock-free/performance redesign |
| Implementation | `ThreadPoolBackend` drops its `RequestArena` entirely and adopts the context-owned core through the new `AsyncBackend::adopt_request_core(detail::RequestCore*)` seam (default false; the context constructor calls it after B1-A's `adopt_context_identity`); the submit transaction becomes validate → `core_->reserve()` → scratch/delivery-record write → tentative Completion binding → `core_->accept()` (the linearization point; installs the public binding and the first execution ref) → either inline zero-op publication or physical dispatch. The zero-op path acquires a core control ref before publication begins — the deferred ready-event obligation is pinned for its whole life — and `poll()` discharges the owed event and releases that ref, whose final retirement reclaims the slot synchronously (the review-corrective delivery pin; the first production consumer of the B1-1 `acquire_control`/`release_control` pair). The worker pops the dispatch ring and takes over execution under `work_mtx_` via `core_->claim_execution()` (late/stale claims skip cancelled work), runs the blocking syscall, offers the physical outcome via `core_->offer_terminal`, retires the borrow-touching ref via `core_->release_execution`, and queues the publication drive. `poll()`/`wait_one()` drive publication: `begin_publication` (granted only after terminal + E retirement) → compatibility write to the caller's `Completion` outside all locks → `complete_publication` → ready-sink event. `Completion` gains a core binding (`install_core_binding_for_backend`/`core_binding_for_backend`; reset/destructor call `release_public_binding`); the arena binding remains for io_uring. Cancel resolves the request key from the binding, removes queued work and arbitrates in the core: pre-claim cancels win a zero-effect terminal, retire the execution ref and queue publication; post-claim cancels record intent only. `AsyncIoContext::~AsyncIoContext` now retires the backend before the core (workers call into the core); `outstanding()` is core-derived. Evidence target: `tests/threadpool_core_cutover_test.cpp` with reworked deterministic pause gates (submit entry, pre-accept commit, accepted-pre-dispatch, worker dequeue/claimed/outcome-pre-terminal, publication epilogue) and fault injections (dispatch failure, three submit-stage failures) |
| Change | Behavior changes: (1) SEM-03 precedence for ThreadPool — validation precedes reservation, so invalid range outranks capacity and closed admission ranks between them (two A1 GAP rows close for ThreadPool; io_uring unchanged); (2) zero-length data requests are accepted, published at acceptance (`ready()` true on submit return) and never dispatched (A1 zero-op GAP row closes for ThreadPool; the recorded ThreadPool divergence set in `semantic_validation_precedence_test` becomes empty), and their ready event stays owed — under a core control ref — until the next poll discharges it, so a context destroyed with the event undelivered is non-quiescent destruction instead of a silent drop; (3) `request_state()` no longer reports `backend_ready` for ThreadPool — a request is publicly outstanding until published, then completion-ready (an internal fact stops being a public state; the root's HANDLE-01 table has no such state); (4) cancel racing a zero-op now reports already-terminal instead of racing; (5) the backend's `outstanding()` and destruction quiescence checks read core facts instead of arena counters. No public API was added, removed or retyped; `Completion`, `RequestHandle`, waiter registration and the ready-sink events keep their shapes |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `threadpool_core_cutover_test` 14/14 (asanubsan, debug, release, TSan): pre-accept rejection leaves no core residue; range precedes capacity under saturation; the accepted-undispatched window holds a live core owner (E=1, binding, no terminal) with an empty dispatch queue; cancel in that window wins pre-execution and converges through the terminal with zero syscalls, and the stale dispatch entry the losing submitter still enqueues is drained by its worker before teardown; injected post-accept dispatch failure returns an accepted request whose terminal is `backend_error` (V05) while no worker ran; worker claim/running cancel keeps `cancel_intent` with E=1 and the physical outcome still wins (cancel never retires a running borrow); the last physical access (syscall count 1, terminal not yet chosen, E=1) strictly precedes ref retirement and no publication is queued while E lives; the epilogue race (release between result write and `complete_publication`) invalidates the public identity immediately, pins the slot on the publication inflight flag, and reclaims synchronously on the final pin with no unrelated new I/O (V26), after which the slot is reusable; zero-op publishes at acceptance with zero syscalls and discharges its owed delivery event on the next poll; the corrective's capacity=1 case pins the whole reset-before-poll sequence — after the Completion is reset before any poll the slot stays accepted (published, binding released, C=1) and the owed event survives, a new submit is refused `would_block` with the owed record untouched, the next poll delivers exactly one ready event carrying the old request key, the final pin retirement reclaims synchronously with zero new syscalls, and the slot then serves a new request at generation+1; admission close between reserve and accept rejects with `invalid_state` and rolls the reservation back (V04 production shape); waiter registration/delivery rides the publication with the registered token and lease; a stale identity does not resolve after slot reuse (V09); four concurrent submissions across three workers all publish, reclaim and run every syscall on workers (INV-02). `request_core_ownership_test` 8/8 with the flipped premise (`production_request_is_request_core_owned`: live core facts during flight, core publication/binding ownership, reclaim on release; the arena idle-core case is gone with the arena). Full suite: 39/39 asanubsan without liburing, 39/39 debug, 39/39 release, 43/43 debug `--liburing=y`, 43/43 release `--liburing=y` (real-kernel io_uring smoke intact proves B1-C remains unmigrated); `semantic_validation_precedence_test` now reports 0 recorded ThreadPool divergences; the direct zero-op precedence cases (`file_access_precedence_test`) drive the progress service at least once after a zero-length submit so the deferred event is discharged before teardown; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean |
| Protocol/model evidence | `formal/tla/RequestCore.tla` is unchanged: the B1-B production mapping introduces no semantic transition outside the accepted B1-1 protocol. Production correspondence: submit validate+reserve ≙ Reserve; rollback ≙ Rollback; `core_->accept` ≙ Accept (the zero-op path is the model chain Accept∘PhysicalOutcome(ok)∘RetireExec compressed into one call, matching the B1-1 substrate); the zero-op's deferred compatibility delivery ≙ `acquire_control` at obligation creation and `release_control` after the sink event, whose final retirement triggers the modeled synchronous Reclaim even when the public binding was released first; `close_admission` ≙ CloseAdmission; worker pop+claim ≙ ClaimExec (a cancelled-in-window queue entry leaves the claim disabled — modeled as cancel-then-no-claim); syscall→`offer_terminal` ≙ PhysicalOutcome; pre-claim cancel win ≙ CancelRequest cancelwin; post-claim cancel ≙ CancelRequest intent; final `release_execution` ≙ RetireExec; `begin/complete_publication` ≙ BeginPublish/CompletePublish; `Completion` reset/destructor ≙ ReleaseBind (including the pub=inflight epilogue interleaving); synchronous `try_reclaim` ≙ Reclaim. Stage B1-1 rerun after the cutover and again after the delivery-pin corrective (same jar v1.7.4, same 20 cfgs, exact `verify_tla.sh` command list): safety clean, `PROPERTY L1 L5` clean, 14 safety mutants killed on their named invariants, 3 liveness mutants killed, the MutReadyBeforePayload subsumption witness clean. ThreadPool environment assumptions under which L1/L5 cover the production path: accepted dispatchable work is eventually serviced (workers wait on `work_cv_` and every dispatch push notifies — OS scheduling of a runnable worker is outside Sluice authority); worker physical execution eventually returns (a stalled blocking syscall invalidates the premise, as the root's REQ-06 caveat records); enabled internal publication/reclaim work is serviced (the driver's poll is the service; the ready-wait wake chain carries it, and the fiber runtime's park loop polls through `wait_one`, so a zero-op's pinned event is discharged before quiescence). L2 (the owner observes the wake) remains #397's obligation and is assumed here, not proved |
| Mutation evidence | The corrective's delivery-pin mutations were applied to `src/async/threadpool_backend.cpp`, force-rebuilt (`xmake -r threadpool_core_cutover_test`) and run against the cutover binary; the unmutated baseline passed after restore. Removing only the zero-op `acquire_control` dies on the protocol diagnostic in `poll()` (`release_control` underflow → `threadpool_core_handoff_fail_fast`). Removing the pin acquisition and the `release_control` call together dies on nine named assertions of `zero_op_delivery_pins_the_slot_until_the_event_is_delivered` (pin observation, `would_block` reuse refusal, owed-record survival, exactly-once delivery, old-key identity, synchronous reclaim, reuse). Removing only the `release_control` call dies on `the zero-op slot reclaims after release` plus the non-quiescent destruction terminate |
| Publication/thread evidence | The ThreadPool C++ happens-before chain: the worker's syscall and outcome construction happen-before its `offer_terminal` (same thread, sequenced; the outcome write lands under the core mutex); `release_execution`, the FIFO push (under `work_mtx_`) and the ready-wait signal are sequenced after it. The driver that observes the signal (condition-variable acquire) or polls unprompted pops the FIFO under `work_mtx_` (acquiring the push) and calls `begin_publication`, whose core-mutex acquire establishes visibility of the outcome, returning a by-value payload copy. The delivery thunk then writes the caller's `Completion` storage and executes `state_.store(ready, release)` — the pre-existing compatibility visibility edge, with the storage write strictly sequenced before it. A consumer's acquire load of `ready` therefore synchronizes with the entire worker→core→publisher chain before it reads the result or reuses the buffer (REQ-04; S4/S5). The borrow boundary: the thunk's ready store is the publisher's last access to that `Completion`; `deliver_event` afterwards touches only delivery-record bookkeeping (waiter token/lease), never the completion or caller memory. The epilogue race is closed by the core mutex alone: a release between the thunk and `complete_publication` finds `publication_inflight` (visible ⇒ released), drops `binding_live` and skips reclaim; the publisher's `complete_publication` then publishes and reclaims synchronously. The delivery pin adds no new lock or atomic: both of its transitions run under the core mutex already held by `publish_zero_op_inline`/`poll`, and the zero-op publication completes on the submit thread before submit returns, so no cross-thread delivery interleaving exists in the pinned window. `active_workers_--` is never used as borrow proof — the proof point is offer+release under the core's authority after the last physical access. TSan: the cutover suite and the ownership suite pass under `-m tsan` (real submitter/worker/publisher/consumer interleavings); the Fiber-runtime tests do not compile under gcc TSan because of the pre-existing `disable_sanitizer_instrumentation` attribute directive in `src/async/fiber_ctx.cpp` (same class as the A2-recorded asanubsan blocker), so no TSan claim is made for them |
| Backend/kernel evidence | ThreadPool evidence runs against real regular files on tmpfs/ext4 (pread/pwrite/fdatasync/fsync, real EINVAL on `/dev/null` preserved through `IoOutcome::failure` with native detail). Real-kernel io_uring (`--liburing=y`, liburing 2.13) passes its full smoke and ownership suites on the same binaries, demonstrating the unmigrated path is intact. Locking: context `access_mtx_` → backend `work_mtx_` → core mutex, acquired only in that order (the core never calls out; delivery runs outside `work_mtx_` and outside the core mutex; host hooks are never run under either); delivery records are guarded by `access_mtx_` and never touched by workers |
| Authority-contraction audit | Removed with the cutover: ThreadPool's entire `RequestArena` ownership (slot state machine, generation table, reserve/prepare/commit transaction, `enqueue_in_flight_pin_`, ready ring, terminal arbitration and storage, publication binding, `release_completed_binding`→free, cancel arbitration, waiter storage, admission-closed flag, `accepted_outstanding_`/`slot_in_use_`/`high_water_mark_`/`capacity_rejections_` counters), the `submit_transaction` usage, the `admission_mtx_` admission serialization (the core mutex is now the linearization authority) and the `TerminalResult`-based syscall result path. Retained compatibility mechanisms: the `Completion` dual binding (core flavor for ThreadPool, arena flavor for io_uring until B1-C), waiter/routing records and `ReadyEvent` delivery (#396 owns the successor), `RequestHandleState` mapping. Retained physical mechanisms that look duplicated but are independently required: `prepared_ops_` (physical syscall parameters the core does not store), the dispatch ring (bounded dispatch storage), `active_workers_` (physical worker-busy count for teardown quiescence — a physical fact, not a borrow fact), the publication-pending ring (the progress driver's work list — derived from core eligibility facts, bounded by capacity because every entry pins an unpublished accepted slot; `begin_publication` remains the authority). Corrective delivery pin: `DeliveryRecord.event_owed`/`owed_key` had outlived publication without entering the core's reclaim accounting; the fix materializes no new runtime fact — the obligation is carried by the existing B1-1 `control_refs` (classification: physical/compatibility obligation of the deferred compatibility delivery; replaces `None`; removable only with the compatibility ready-event delivery itself under #396). Temporary: `adopt_request_core` (removed when B1-C migrates io_uring and the seam collapses into the backend interface), the Completion arena flavor (removed with B1-C/#395/#402 consumer retirement) |
| Status | ThreadPool production admission/lifecycle: the B1-B scope above is VERIFIED for this record's PR head under the recorded configurations (deterministic interleaving tests, TSan on the cutover suites, full-suite matrix, real-kernel io_uring regression, stage-B1-1 TLC rerun with mutations). The assessment row stays NOT_ASSESSED as a whole: io_uring remains on `RequestArena` until B1-C, the public `Request<T>` API is #395, observer/progress/shutdown/effect obligations are #396/#397/#400/#401, and the `RequestHandleState::backend_ready` removal is a compatibility-surface change consumed by the B1-C ledger update. **[B1-D update, later commit]** B1-C migrated io_uring without removing the enum value: `backend_ready` now has zero producers and stays with #395/#402 compatibility retirement, and the `adopt_request_core` seam this record marks temporary collapsed into the backend interface contract at B1-D |

## B1-C review record — Issue #394, io_uring atomic RequestCore authority cutover

This record covers the io_uring production request path only. With it, both
production backends run under the context-owned `RequestCore`; there is no
mergeable half-migrated state: `UringAsyncBackend` no longer holds a
`RequestArena` at all, so no second lifecycle authority exists beside the core
on any production path. #394 remains open only for the closure slices it
already names (#395–#401). **[B1-D update, later commit]** #394's migration
scope itself closed at B1-D (cross-backend evidence convergence plus seam
contraction; see the B1-D review record); the #395–#401 obligations are
untouched and keep their owners.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | REQ-01 (io_uring request identity = core-allocated context+slot+generation; public lookup/cancel stale immediately after public binding release even while a transport pin holds the slot), REQ-02 (single acceptance linearization under the core's serialized authority: validate → reserve → transport preparation that can roll back → `core_->accept` → post-accept transport work; pre-accept failures leave no reservation, no borrow, no public identity, no cookie/router/ledger entry; post-accept failures converge through the core terminal path), REQ-03/REQ-04/REQ-05 production halves for io_uring (publication gated on borrow-touching retirement at the original-operation CQE; the `Completion` ready release/acquire pair as the compatibility visibility edge; reclaim under the core's canonical predicate with synchronous final-pin retirement), SEM-03 steps 3–7 precedence and the zero-op immediate-publication rule on the io_uring request path (the previously recorded io_uring halves of the precedence and zero-op GAP rows are closed), INV-02 (bounded io_uring submit/cancel initiation; the submission-mode blocking risk remains a #400 profile item), ARCH-02/BACKEND-01 (backend owns SQE/CQE transport only), CANCEL-01 floors (pre-dispatch cancel wins zero-effect; a control CQE is neither the original result nor proof of original retirement; a racing cancel never erases a confirmed count), BOUND-01/02 (cookies, ledger and control records bounded by capacity/ring depth; no stranded accepted work), VERIFY-03 L1/L5 io_uring-mapped environment assumptions, VERIFY-04 V04/V05/V09/V13/V14/V26 io_uring halves and the V24 request-id facet. Excludes the public `Request<T>` API (#395), Observer (#396), ProgressSource/no-lost-wake (#397), RequestScope (#398), host redesign (#399), the full cancel/effect matrix (#400), the shutdown/poison state machine (#401), and any performance/queue redesign |
| Implementation | `UringAsyncBackend` drops its `RequestArena` and adopts the context-owned core through `adopt_request_core`. The submit transaction becomes validate (SEM-03 1–4, zero-op range skip with offset normalization) → `core_->reserve()` (admission/health then capacity) → prepared-SQE-descriptor and delivery-record write → tentative `Completion` binding → `core_->accept()` (the linearization point; installs the public binding and the first execution ref; a zero-op is terminal at acceptance) → either inline zero-op publication (a core control ref pins the owed ready event; the next `poll()` discharges it and the final pin reclaims synchronously) or dispatch: the dispatch ring holds accepted-undispatched handles; `dispatch_one_locked` acquires an SQE slot, claims execution in the core (a lost claim returns the unused SQE slot and drops the entry), then allocates the monotonic cookie, prepares the SQE, creates the router entry and appends the transport-ledger entry. CQE reaping maps each class to its true role: an original-operation CQE offers the physical outcome to the core (first admissible winner), retires the borrow-touching ref (`borrow_touch_fully_retired`), queues publication, and retires the router entry unless a control CQE is still owed; a control CQE (tagged cookie) releases only the core control ref acquired when the cancel SQE was prepared, never touching the borrow or the outcome. The router entry keeps `control_state` and `terminal_delivered` so it survives until both the original and the control CQE have retired. Poison maps onto the core: `note_health_failure` closes admission, kernel-invisible work (dispatch entries and prepared ledger entries) converges to error terminals through the core, and already-submitted operations keep their real completion path so no fabricated terminal publishes a borrow that the kernel may still touch. Cancel resolves the request key from the `Completion` core binding, removes queued work, and arbitrates in the core: pre-claim wins retire E and queue publication; post-claim cancels record intent and issue at most one cancel SQE per request (coalescing derived from the live router entry's `control_state`). `outstanding()` is core-derived; identity resolution and waiter registration gate on core lookup; the context destructor retires the backend before the core. Evidence target: `tests/uring_core_cutover_test.cpp` (20 cases) with deterministic pause gates (submit entry, pre-accept commit, accepted-pre-dispatch, publication epilogue), submit-stage and dispatch-failure injections, a no-kernel fake submit that advances the SQ cursors so CQEs arrive only through `inject_cqe_for_test` (fully deterministic ordering), and real-kernel cases on the live ring |
| Change | `UringAsyncBackend` loses the `RequestArena`, the arena submit transaction, the per-slot cancel scratch vector, the `RouterEntry::deferred_terminal` pair (value + mirror bool) and the backend admission-closed mirror; `Completion`'s arena binding flavor and `install_binding`/`submit_transaction` compatibility surface remain but now have zero production consumers (removal condition below). SEM-03 precedence and zero-op behavior change on the io_uring request path as the ledger rows record; no requirement, error category or public contract was amended |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `uring_core_cutover_test` 20/20 (debug, release, asanubsan, TSan, real kernel liburing 2.14/kernel 6.18): pre-accept rejection leaves no core residue; range precedes closure and capacity; the accepted-pre-submit window holds a live core owner (E=1, binding, no claim, no SQE/cookie/ledger) and cancel in that window wins pre-execution with zero transport facts while the losing submitter's drain drops its own queue entry; original-CQE-first defers nothing semantically — the physical outcome is chosen and E retired at the original CQE while the control obligation still pins the slot, publication proceeds, the released binding stops resolving, and the control CQE reclaims synchronously with no new I/O; control-CQE-first retires only the control obligation (E stays held, no publication) and the late original CQE canonicalizes the real outcome on the same generation; a racing cancel never erases the confirmed short count; a stale cookie's CQE cannot affect the reused slot and cookies never reuse across generations; the stale public identity stops resolving after slot reuse; the zero-op publishes at acceptance with zero transport facts and its owed event survives a reset-before-poll at capacity 1 (refuses new admission `would_block`, delivers exactly one event, reclaims synchronously, then serves the next generation); submit-stage failures at reserve/prepare/commit all roll back with no transport leak; injected dispatch failure and sequenced submit failure (`-EIO` poison) converge accepted requests through the terminal path — the never-visible one to an error terminal, the kernel-visible one to its real physical outcome — with the poisoned backend refusing new submissions with its health failure and nothing ownerless; a running cancel across the poison keeps the router entry routable so the real original CQE still converges request A to its success terminal while the cancel obligation's ref retires in the recovery walk; the publication-epilogue release race pins the slot on the publication-inflight flag and reclaims synchronously; waiter registration rides publication with the registered token and lease; four concurrent submissions all publish and reclaim with no cookie leak; real-kernel read converges through the core publication and reclaim; the real-kernel cancel target converges to one terminal whose disposition is platform-specific (recorded: this kernel completes the target with native `-EALREADY` → `backend_error` with native detail) and reclaims. `semantic_validation_precedence_test` now drives the io_uring request path against the oracle with 0 recorded divergences (19 compared, 2 not drivable, same as ThreadPool); `semantic_backend_conformance_test` reports 0 recorded uring divergences (zero-op divergences closed); `uring_backend_smoke_test` 8/8 on the real ring; `request_core_ownership_uring_test` 9/9 with the flipped premise (the io_uring request is core-owned; the B1-A idle-core assertion is gone). Full matrix: debug 44/44 and release 44/44 with `--liburing=y`; asanubsan 10/10 on the self-contained seam targets (the project-wide asanubsan sluice_async build remains blocked by the pre-existing gcc/fiber_ctx attribute error recorded in B1-B, unrelated to this slice); TSan passes `uring_core_cutover_test`, `threadpool_core_cutover_test` and `request_core_ownership_uring_test` at the final head; `--liburing=n` builds and passes 39/39 with the honest stub. `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean |
| Protocol/model evidence | `formal/tla/RequestCore.tla` is unchanged: the io_uring production mapping introduces no semantic transition outside the accepted B1-1 protocol — the transport state space (cookies, SQE stages, ledger, router) lives in the C++/backend correspondence and never changes a core action's precondition or effect. Production correspondence: submit validate+reserve ≙ Reserve; rollback ≙ Rollback; `core_->accept` ≙ Accept (the zero-op path is the model chain Accept∘PhysicalOutcome(ok)∘RetireExec compressed into one call, as in B1-B); dispatch pop+claim ≙ ClaimExec (a cancelled-in-window entry leaves the claim disabled; the lost-claim path also returns the unused SQE slot); original-CQE `offer_terminal` ≙ PhysicalOutcome; pre-claim cancel win ≙ CancelRequest cancelwin; post-claim cancel ≙ CancelRequest intent; the control SQE's `acquire_control` ≙ AcquireCtl and the control CQE's `release_control` ≙ ReleaseCtl (the modeled obligation whose retirement pins the slot against reclaim); post-original-CQE `release_execution` ≙ RetireExec; `begin/complete_publication` ≙ BeginPublish/CompletePublish; `Completion` reset/destructor ≙ ReleaseBind (including the pub-inflight epilogue interleaving); synchronous reclaim on the final pin ≙ Reclaim; poison `note_health_failure` ≙ NoteHealth; `close_admission` ≙ CloseAdmission. Stage B1-1 rerun on this branch (same jar v1.7.4, same 20 cfgs, the exact `verify_tla.sh` stage-B1-1 command list): safety clean (6,165,549 states generated / 996,436 distinct / 0 on queue, `Model checking completed. No error has been found`), 14 invariant mutants killed on their named invariants, 3 temporal mutants killed (`Temporal properties were violated`), the MutReadyBeforePayload subsumption witness clean; and the `PROPERTY L1 L5` liveness configuration completed clean on this branch (6,165,549 states generated / 996,436 distinct / 0 on queue, `Model checking completed. No error has been found`). io_uring environment assumptions under which L1/L5 cover the production path: submitted kernel work eventually produces the required retirement evidence (a CQE for every submitted SQE — a stalled filesystem operation invalidates the premise exactly as REQ-06 records); progress service eventually reaps available CQEs (the driver's poll/wait protocol); enabled internal publication/reclaim work is serviced (the same poll drive discharges publications and owed zero-op events). L2 remains #397's obligation and is assumed, not proved |
| Mutation evidence | Eight named C++ mutants (`SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_*` compile definitions, one xmake target each, run manually and recorded; the clean suite passes after restore). `COOKIE_REUSE` (cookie allocator returns a fixed value) dies on `stale_cookie_cannot_reach_a_reused_slot` — monotonic-consumption, never-reused, and stale-CQE-ineffective assertions all fail, and the stale CQE then corrupts the reused request. `CANCEL_CQE_AS_ORIGINAL_TERMINAL` (the control CQE fabricates a canceled outcome, retires E and retires the entry) dies on `cancel_cqe_first_never_becomes_the_terminal` — the control obligation is observed to retire the borrow, the late original outcome is lost, and the published bytes are wrong. `PUBLISH_BEFORE_BORROW_RETIREMENT` (publication queued before `release_execution`) dies on `uring_core_handoff_fail_fast` — `begin_publication` refuses while an execution ref is live (the core's InvNoPubWhileExec correspondence). `DROP_ORIGINAL_OUTCOME` (negative byte-op CQEs are dropped) dies on the Completion L9 assertion after bounded polling — the stranded request never publishes. `REMOVE_CONTROL_PIN` (no `acquire_control` when the cancel SQE is prepared) dies on `original_cqe_first_defers_until_control_retires` — the slot reclaims before the control CQE and the late release lands on a stale slot (`uring_core_handoff_fail_fast`). `RECLAIM_BEFORE_CONTROL_RETIREMENT` (the router entry retires while the original outcome is still owed) dies on `cancel_cqe_first_never_becomes_the_terminal` — the late original CQE misses the router and the outcome is lost. `STRAND_POST_ACCEPT_SUBMIT_FAILURE` (poison recovery skips the Class-A ledger walk) dies on `post_accept_submit_failure_poison_converges` — the never-visible request never converges. `PREMATURE_ZERO_OP_DISPATCH` (zero-ops forced through transport) dies on `zero_op_publishes_at_acceptance_without_dispatch`. Structural exclusion: "leave Arena terminal/reclaim authority active beside RequestCore" is not expressible post-cutover — the backend no longer contains an arena to mutate; the idle-second-authority proof is the absence of the member itself plus the whole suite passing against the core alone |
| Publication/thread evidence | The io_uring C++ happens-before chain: the kernel publishes completion results into the CQ ring under io_uring's own kernel→userspace ordering (liburing's documented barriers for `io_uring_peek_batch_cqe`); the reaper consumes them on the progress/submitting thread under the context `access_mtx_` and the backend `dispatch_mtx_` (acquired inside `handle_one_cqe`), constructs the `IoOutcome` and offers it through the core mutex (whose acquire makes the physical outcome visible to every later core observation); `release_execution` and the publication-pending push are sequenced after the offer in the same critical section. The publisher pops under `dispatch_mtx_`, calls `begin_publication` (core mutex; by-value payload copy), writes the caller's `Completion` storage and executes `state_.store(ready, release)` — the pre-existing compatibility visibility edge with the storage write strictly sequenced before it — then `complete_publication` and the ready-sink event. A consumer's acquire load of `ready` therefore synchronizes with kernel→backend→core→publisher before reading the result or reusing the buffer (REQ-04; S4/S5). The borrow boundary: the borrow-touching ref retires only after the original CQE (the kernel can no longer touch the caller buffer for that operation; a control SQE never touches caller memory), and the ready store is the publisher's last access to the `Completion` — `deliver_event` afterwards touches only delivery-record bookkeeping. The epilogue race is closed by the core mutex alone (release between thunk and `complete_publication` observes `publication_inflight`, drops `binding_live`, defers reclaim to the publisher's completion — measured deterministically). Lock order: context `access_mtx_` → backend `dispatch_mtx_` → core mutex, acquired only in that order; the core never calls out while held; publication and delivery run outside `dispatch_mtx_`; the wait-source park protocol is unchanged. TSan passes the cutover, threadpool-cutover and ownership suites; the fiber-runtime tests remain excluded from sanitizer claims by the pre-existing gcc `fiber_ctx.cpp` attribute blocker |
| Backend/kernel evidence | Real kernel: liburing 2.14, kernel 6.18.33 (WSL2), tmpfs/ext4 regular files; `uring_backend_smoke_test` (read/write/sync_data/sync_all/zero-op/invalid-offset), `semantic_backend_conformance_test` (0 divergences) and the cutover suite's real-kernel cases (read through core publication/reclaim; cancel convergence with the recorded `-EALREADY` disposition; six rounds of slot reuse with identity resolution) all pass on the live ring. Deterministic seams (fake submit + injected CQEs) cover orderings the kernel cannot schedule on demand (original-first vs control-first, stale cookies) — they do not replace the real-kernel runs. Bounded accounting: cookies ≤ request capacity, ledger entries ≤ SQ ring depth, control records ≤ one per live request (coalesced by the router's `control_state`). Known #400/#401 boundary recorded, not closed: a hard submit failure is treated as kernel-invisible per liburing's atomic-failure behavior, and the recovery fabricates error terminals for those prepared entries exactly as the pre-cutover Class-A recovery did — the root's warning that a negative submit return does not prove kernel invisibility remains an open #401 refinement obligation, now at least preserved verbatim rather than widened; platform-specific cancel dispositions are recorded, not normalized |
| Authority-contraction audit | Removed lifecycle authority: `UringAsyncBackend`'s entire `RequestArena` ownership (slot state machine with `mark_running`/`enqueue`/`record_terminal`, generation table, reserve/prepare/commit transaction, `enqueue_in_flight_pin_`, ready ring, terminal arbitration and `TerminalResult` storage, publication binding and `release_completed_binding`→free path, cancel arbitration with `cancel_intent_`, waiter storage, `resolve_completion` scan, admission-closed flag, accepted-outstanding/slot-in-use/backend-ready counters) — the core is now the only accepted-set, identity, terminal, publication and reclaim authority for io_uring. Removed mirrors (each with demonstrated derivation): the per-slot `CancelScratch::cancel_queued` vector (the coalescing fact is exactly the live router entry's `control_state != none`, checked on the entry the scan already holds), `RouterEntry::deferred_terminal_stored` (a pure mirror of the stored value's own predicate — and with the terminal now offered at the original CQE, the deferred value itself is gone: kernel semantics make the original operation's CQE the single final outcome, and the control CQE carries no information about it, so the entry's lifetime obligation is carried explicitly by `control_state` + `terminal_delivered`), the backend `admission_closed_` mirror (the core's admission gate is the authority; poison closes it through `note_health_failure`), and the dead historical test machinery (router scan/fix-mode diagnostics, `RouterCookieTableForTest`, `SlotHandle`-based arena observation seams, `before_queue_exit` hook — all with zero remaining consumers).
