Retained physical mechanisms, each with its obligation: the monotonic non-reused cookie allocator (kernel anti-ABA identity, distinct from core generation safety), `PreparedUringOp` per slot (physical SQE parameters the core does not store), the bounded dispatch ring (accepted-undispatched transport backlog), the backend publication worklist `publication_pending_` (retained bounded progress worklist for the migrated io_uring path: the `RequestArena` ready ring → removed, `publication_pending_` → retained as the progress driver's publication queue — a mechanism replacement, not a transfer of lifecycle authority; terminal arbitration, canonical result, publication eligibility, public request identity, public binding lifetime and slot reclaim remain `RequestCore` authority, and `begin_publication` remains the grant; derived from core eligibility facts, non-authoritative, bounded by request capacity; removable only when the compatibility publication/delivery path no longer requires a backend worklist under #396/#397 or their authorized replacement), the `TransportLedger` (prepared-vs-submitted kernel obligations with FIFO physical positions; the poison recovery's walk order), the router entry (cookie→key routing that must outlive both CQE classes when a cancel is in flight), `live_cookies_`/`live_control_sqes_`/`submit_flushes_` (derived O(1) summaries for quiescence and evidence — not semantic authority), the delivery records with `event_owed`/`owed_key` (the same B1-B control-ref pairing for the zero-op's owed ready event). Compatibility residue: `Completion`'s arena binding flavor, `install_binding`/`rollback_claim_before_accept` and `detail::submit_transaction` now have zero production consumers and are removed with the compatibility Completion itself under #395/#402; `RequestHandleState::backend_ready` is no longer produced by any backend. Temporary: `adopt_request_core` remains a seam only while both backends exist beside unmigrated paths — with both backends migrated it collapses into the backend interface contract at B1-D |
| Status | io_uring production admission/lifecycle: the B1-C scope above is VERIFIED for this record's PR head under the recorded configurations (deterministic interleaving suite, mutation kills, asanubsan + TSan on the self-contained targets, full debug/release matrix, no-liburing build, real-kernel runs, stage-B1-1 TLC rerun with mutations). Both production backends now use the context-owned RequestCore as their single semantic lifecycle authority; the assessment row's remaining GAP content is the #400/#401 boundary recorded above. Open and explicitly not claimed: the public `Request<T>` API (#395), Observer (#396), ProgressSource/no-lost-wake (#397), RequestScope (#398), host redesign (#399), the full cancel/effect profile (#400), the shutdown/poison state machine (#401), and compatibility-surface retirement (#395/#402) |

### B1-C mechanism justification (AGENTS.md gate)

Every meaningful runtime mechanism this slice adds or retains on the io_uring
path, against the gate's questions.

| Mechanism | Fact / owning authority | Justification | Derived? | Classification | Replaces; removal condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monotonic cookie allocator (`next_cookie_`, control tag) | The kernel-visible transport identity of one prepared operation (owner: the backend) | A late CQE from an old kernel operation must never target a reused request: cookies never repeat and never enter the tagged control range; distinct from core generation safety and proved separately by the stale-cookie case and `COOKIE_REUSE` | No | Authority (transport identity) | Replaces nothing (pre-existing, presumed load-bearing, confirmed); removal only with the io_uring transport itself |
| `RouterEntry {cookie, handle, control_state, terminal_delivered, in_use}` | Which live kernel operation maps to which core request, and which of its two CQE classes have retired (owner: the backend) | A control CQE must find its target after the original CQE and vice versa; the entry must outlive both retirements when a cancel is in flight | `terminal_delivered` is an independent transport fact (the core's public surface cannot express "terminal offered but control owed"); `handle` mirrors the core's key by value — routing payload, not a second authority | Transport state | Replaces the old `deferred_terminal` pair; removable only when io_uring cancellations cease to be transport-visible |
| `TransportLedger` (FIFO, kind, cookie, handle, physical position, Class-A flag) | The prepared-vs-submitted boundary of every SQE handed toward the kernel, in submission order (owner: the backend) | BACKEND-03: a negative submit return must not be assumed to prove invisibility; recovery and accounting need the exact ordered obligation set; bounded by SQ depth | No | Authority (transport accounting) | None; removed only with the ring |
| Bounded dispatch ring | Accepted-undispatched request handles (owner: the backend) | REQ-02: accepted-but-undispatched work stays discoverable and bounded, never orphaned by queue pressure | No | Transport state | None |
| `publication_pending_` (`BoundedDispatchQueue`) | The backend's bounded progress worklist of migrated io_uring requests for which the physical/core conditions to drive compatibility publication are already produced (terminal chosen at `offer_terminal`, borrow-touching ref retired at `release_execution`) but publication is not yet serviced (owner: the `UringAsyncBackend` progress mechanism); it is not the authority for whether a request is terminal or publishable | CQE/transport processing needs a bounded way to queue publication work for later progress servicing without performing compatibility publication while holding the transport/dispatch lock; bounded by request capacity because each entry is the slot handle of an accepted-but-unpublished request | Membership is derived from core facts already fixed when an entry is queued, but materialized because the backend progress driver (`poll`/`wait_one`) needs an explicit bounded worklist to publish outside the transport lock; semantic publication eligibility stays derivable/validated through `RequestCore` (`begin_publication` remains the grant), so the queue is not canonical lifecycle state | Derived physical/progress worklist; non-authoritative; bounded by request capacity | For the migrated io_uring path, replaces the removed `RequestArena` ready ring — a mechanism replacement, not a transfer of lifecycle authority: terminal arbitration, canonical result, publication eligibility, public request identity, public binding lifetime and slot reclaim remain `RequestCore` authority. Retained while the current compatibility publication/delivery path requires a backend worklist; removable only when the downstream Observer/Progress delivery migration (#396/#397 or its eventual authorized replacement) removes that consumer and equivalent progress responsibility exists elsewhere — not merely because B1-D begins |
| `PreparedUringOp` per slot | The physical SQE parameters (fd, buffer, length, native length, offset) (owner: the backend) | The core stores borrow facts, not SQE lowering; the SQE needs the kernel-length clamp | No | Transport state | None |
| `DeliveryRecord {completion, publish, waiter fields, event_owed, owed_key}` | The compatibility publication target and the owed ready event paired with one core control ref (owner: the compatibility delivery path) | The zero-op publishes at acceptance while its ready event must still be delivered; the pairing is the B1-B corrective carried to io_uring | The record mirrors the core's binding by pointer/key for delivery only | Compatibility state + physical delivery obligation | Removed with the compatibility `Completion`/ready-event surface under #396/#395/#402 |
| `event_owed` ⇄ `acquire_control`/`release_control` pairing | "One ready event is owed and pins its slot" (owner: the core's `control_refs`) | A real deferred delivery obligation must participate in core reclaim accounting (V26) | No: the flag is the delivery-path half of the core ref | Physical obligation (compatibility) | None; same shape B1-B introduced |
| `live_cookies_` / `live_control_sqes_` / `submit_flushes_` | O(1) counts of live router entries, submitted-unretired control SQEs, submit flushes (owner: the backend) | Quiescence and wait-loop decisions must not scan; evidence counters for the suite | Yes — exact mirrors of router/ledger contents, recomputed nowhere else | Derived summary (non-authoritative) | None; retained for bounded operation |
| Poison recovery state (`fatal_error_` + `note_health_failure` + Class-A ledger walk) | Ring transport health and the per-entry recovery retirement facts (owner: the backend; admission closure owned by the core) | #16's minimum: a fatal ring must not strand or orphan accepted work; kernel-invisible work converges, kernel-visible work keeps its real completion path | `fatal_error_` is the transport health fact; admission closure is derived through the core call | Backend transport health + recovery bookkeeping | Full shutdown/poison refinement is #401; the fabricated-terminal-for-prepared-entries assumption is recorded there |
| `Submission Epilogue` gates / `inject_cqe_for_test` / fake submit / stage injections (all `SLUICE_ASYNC_INTERNAL_TESTING`) | Deterministic ordering and fault evidence the kernel cannot schedule on demand (owner: the test seam) | #19–#21's evidence classes; clearly non-production-authoritative | No | Test-only evidence | Withdrawn with the test targets that use them |

## B1-D review record — Issue #394, cross-backend evidence convergence and seam contraction

This record closes #394's B1 migration scope, in the frozen internal order:
evidence convergence, authority correspondence audit, justified contraction,
ledger closure. No production semantic transition changed in this slice: after
B1-C (base `fb59da8b`, PR #423 ancestor) both production backends already ran
under the context-owned `RequestCore`. B1-D removes the migration seam that
made the authority transferable, converges the recorded evidence, and closes
the ledger statements that still described a half-migrated repository. The
public `Request<T>` API, observer, progress, request scope, host redesign,
cancel/effect profile, shutdown state machine and compatibility retirement
belong to #395–#401 and are not claimed here. This record is not a v1 final
verdict; the assessment rows keep their statuses.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | ARCH-01/ARCH-02 finalized (the context owns the core; a backend must not own it or allocate public identity — the adoption seam that made this negotiable is gone); REQ-01/REQ-02/REQ-03/REQ-04/REQ-05/BOUND-01/BOUND-02 production halves for both backends (evidence convergence only, no transition changed); VERIFY-04 V04/V05/V09/V24/V26 converged reruns; HANDLE-03 basis. Excludes everything owned by #395–#401, and any behavior change: this slice changes mechanism shape, not behavior |
| Implementation | `AsyncBackend` loses the `adopt_context_identity`/`adopt_request_core` private virtuals (and the default returning false) and gains `slot_capacity()`, a pure private virtual, plus a protected `core_` pointer installed exactly once by the `AsyncIoContext` constructor after it allocates the core with the fresh identity and the reported capacity. `ThreadPoolBackend` and the liburing-enabled `UringAsyncBackend` delete their seam overrides and their derived `core_` mirrors — the inherited member is the single representation of "the core this backend serves" — keep a `slot_capacity()` override, and rename the internal-testing accessor `adopted_core_for_test` → `request_core_for_test`. The no-liburing `UringAsyncBackend` declaration gains no `slot_capacity()` override and therefore remains abstract, as it already was at the B1-D base (`fb59da8b`), where its pure `adopt_context_identity` override was likewise present only in the `SLUICE_HAS_LIBURING` configuration. B1-D therefore introduces no no-liburing source-behavior change. The ownership evidence target's seam case becomes `capacity_handshake_happens_once_at_context_construction` (zero capacity queries at backend construction, exactly one at context construction, none on a context move). Also removed: `UringAsyncBackend::queue_depth_`, a write-only config mirror (constructor-initialized, zero readers at base) that blocked clang `-Werror` sanitizer builds of the uring TU |
| Change | No public API or behavior change. Machinery strictly reduced: 9 files, +44/−82. The context→backend handshake is now "one capacity query + one core install" expressed in the backend interface contract; no backend can exist without the context learning its slot-table capacity (pure virtual), and no backend can hold a second core representation. `UringConfig::queue_depth` remains the ring-setup authority |
| Semantic/regression evidence | Newly executed on this branch, all against the final head. gcc debug `--liburing=y`: 44/44 test binaries — ownership 8/8 and uring ownership 9/9 (real ring), protocol 34/34, publication 11/11, consumer probe 2/2, ThreadPool cutover 14/14, uring cutover 20/20 (deterministic seam + real-kernel cases), uring smoke 8/8 (real ring), `semantic_validation_precedence_test` 21 direct compared / ThreadPool 19+2 / io_uring 19+2 / 0 divergences, `semantic_backend_conformance_test` 0 divergences, admission 4/4, file access precedence 40/40, submit boundary 2/2, uring public consumer probe, 30-suite remainder; the eight B1-C mutant binaries rebuilt with every mutation detected (same named failing cases as B1-C; `PUBLISH_BEFORE_RETIREMENT` via the core's protocol-unreachable diagnostic). gcc release `--liburing=y` 44/44. gcc `--liburing=n` 39/39 — the no-liburing library/configuration builds and passes its registered suite; no claim is made that `UringAsyncBackend` is instantiable in this configuration (the uring binaries in the tree were verified stale by timestamp so no stale-archive pass is claimed). asanubsan (clang 21.1.8) 11/11 including the library-linked admission/precedence/semantic targets that gcc cannot build in this mode. tsan 4/4 (both ownership suites, both cutover suites). `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Reused unchanged (inputs untouched): the stage-B1-1 TLC campaign stands as rerun at B1-C (6,165,549 states generated / 996,436 distinct, safety clean, `PROPERTY L1 L5` clean, 14 safety + 3 temporal mutants killed, subsumption witness clean) because `formal/tla/RequestCore.tla` and its C++ correspondence are unchanged — the seam was never a modeled transition (B1-A recorded it as migration surface); B1-B's delivery-pin mutation kills (the mutated ThreadPool paths are unchanged); B1-A's identity-domain mutation kills (`context_identity.cpp` untouched). Not rerun, deliberately: the TLC campaign (no model or correspondence change), and gcc sanitizer coverage of fiber-linked targets (the pre-existing `fiber_ctx.cpp` attribute blocker recorded since A2 — unchanged; clang asanubsan covers the full library including that TU) |
| Protocol/model evidence | Model unchanged; the B1-B/B1-C correspondence tables are inherited verbatim (Reserve/Rollback/Accept/ClaimExec/PhysicalOutcome/CancelRequest/RetireExec/AcquireCtl/ReleaseCtl/RetireCtl/BeginPublish/CompletePublish/ReleaseBind/Reclaim/CloseAdmission/NoteHealth all map to the same core API the backends call identically). The contraction removes no C++ transition any modeled action maps to |
| Publication/thread evidence | No new lock, atomic or edge. The base `core_` member is installed in the context constructor before the context is reachable by any other thread and is immutable thereafter; the destructor retires the backend before the core storage dies (the unchanged B1-B/B1-C order), so no legal interleaving observes a dangling `core_`. The single capacity query happens under the same construction discipline. TSan reruns above |
| Backend/kernel evidence | Real kernel ring (liburing 2.14, kernel 6.18.33, WSL2; tmpfs/ext4 regular files) via the uring smoke, cutover real-kernel cases and the 0-divergence conformance runs; the no-liburing configuration compiled and 39/39. Configurations enumerated in the evidence row |
| Authority-contraction audit | Removed, each with its replacement demonstrated: `adopt_request_core` (seam + default + both overrides) → the protected base `core_` installed by the context constructor — the adoption fact is now structural instead of an overridable negotiation, and no backend can be constructed beside a core it did not receive; `adopt_context_identity` (seam + both overrides + stub) → `slot_capacity()` — its identity half has been dead since B1-B/B1-C removed the arenas the identity was installed into, leaving a capacity-only handshake, and the identity now reaches the core directly at construction; the per-backend derived `core_` members → the single inherited member; `adopted_core_for_test` → renamed `request_core_for_test`; `queue_depth_` → deleted as write-only state (the config field remains the ring-setup authority). Retained, each with an explicit downstream owner: the `Completion` arena binding flavor, `AsyncIoContext::install_binding`, `detail::submit_transaction`, `detail::RequestArena` (with `adopt_context_identity`/`context_`, `identity_handle_state`, the `backend_ready` state and its counters) and `RequestHandleState::backend_ready` — zero production consumers since B1-C, retirement owned by #395/#402; `supports_request_identity`/`resolve_identity_state`/`identity_of` — compatibility identity surface until #395; the `SubmitStage` fault-injection machinery (the only consumer of `submit_transaction.hpp` in backend headers) — test-only evidence for the cutover suites, withdrawn with them; `ApplicationRuntime::admission_closed_snapshot_` — the runtime's own admission-close summary, not a core mirror; the runtime's `issue116-forensics` label `accepted_outstanding` reads the core-derived `outstanding()`, not arena state; every B1-C-classified physical mechanism (cookie allocator, router, `TransportLedger`, `publication_pending_`, delivery records/`event_owed`, prepared ops, dispatch rings) — unchanged classifications; `acquire_control`/`admission_open()`/`health_failed()` substrate surface — unchanged, consumed by both backends and #400/#401 obligations |
| Status | #394's B1 migration scope is closed at this record's PR head: the context-owned `RequestCore` is the single semantic lifecycle authority for every production request on both backends, and the migration seam no longer exists. The remaining obligations named by #394 belong to #395–#401 and are not claimed. The assessment rows above keep their statuses; this record adds no final v1 verdict |

### B1-D mechanism justification (AGENTS.md gate)

The two mechanisms this slice introduces, against the gate's questions.

| Mechanism | Fact / owning authority | Justification | Derived? | Classification | Replaces; removal condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AsyncBackend::slot_capacity()` (pure private virtual) | The capacity of the slot table this backend serves (owner: the backend's physical table) | BOUND-01: the core must be sized by the table it will serve; ARCH-02: the context owns the core, so only the backend can report the budget. Pure virtual so no backend can exist with an unreported table | No | Interface contract (the capacity half of `adopt_context_identity`) | Replaces `adopt_context_identity`; no removal condition within v1 |
| `AsyncBackend::core_` (protected, installed by the context constructor) | Which core this backend serves (owner: the `IoContext`, the sole writer) | ARCH-01/ARCH-02: one context-owned core per backend, reachable by every backend transition; materialized because the backends call the core on every submit/dispatch/CQE/cancel path. Write-once before publication of the context, never cleared; the destructor retires the backend first | No (it is a handle to authority, not a summary) | Authority-owned handle | Replaces `adopt_request_core` and both per-backend derived `core_` mirrors; removed only if backends cease calling the core, which no v1 requirement allows |

## B2 review record — Issue #395, public `Request<T>` settlement responsibility

This record implements the Phase B2 slice of MIG-01: the public result
surface moves onto the context-owned `RequestCore` without a new lifecycle
authority. `Request<T>` is a single-settlement responsibility object over the
core's existing canonical outcome; consumption is atomic with public-binding
ownership; the destructor policy and the context-lifetime precondition are
always-on diagnostics. `Completion` remains the compatibility invocation
form with zero behavior change; its retirement is #402. Observer, progress,
request scope, host redesign, the full cancel/effect matrix and the shutdown
state machine stay with #396–#401 and are not claimed here. This record is
not a v1 final verdict; the assessment rows keep their statuses.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | HANDLE-01 (empty/pending/published/consumed; move-only noexcept transfers; take_result atomic with binding release; try_result non-consuming), HANDLE-02 (destroying/overwriting a known nonterminal Request is an always-on fail-fast, release builds included; a published binding is discarded without waiting for control refs), HANDLE-03 (context destruction with any live public binding forbidden, including ready-but-unconsumed; requests require their context alive), REQ-02/REQ-03 (accepted identity, single canonical terminal), REQ-04 (publication completes without requiring a `Completion<T>*` destination; the new path's HB chain is the core mutex), REQ-05 (reclaim after binding release under the unchanged four-pin predicate), INV-01/INV-02, THREAD-01/THREAD-02 (object-level thread discipline unchanged: one owner, moves synchronize externally), CANCEL-01 routing preservation (core arbitration + backend physical cancel), ERR-01 (pre-accept Result errors, post-accept failures keep the work owned), VERIFY-02 S1–S10 correspondence, VERIFY-04 V05/V08/V14/V21/V24-request-id/V26 + the retained-result §17/§18 evidence. Excludes observer/progress (#396/#397), effect-certainty channels (#400), shutdown (#401), Completion retirement (#402) |
| Implementation | New public header `include/sluice/async/request.hpp`: trivially copyable nonowning `RequestId` (context+slot+generation projection of the core key), `CancelDisposition`, `RequestReadiness`, `RequestObservation<T>`, and move-only `Request<T>` holding exactly `{RequestCore*, AsyncBackend*, RequestKey}` — no mirrored lifecycle booleans, no control block, no shared ownership; `valid()/id()/ready()/try_result()/take_result()/discard()/cancel()`. `RequestCore` gains `observe_public_result` (published-gated, non-mutating), `consume_public_result` (payload copy + binding release + reclaim in one mutex critical section) and `discard_public_result` (published-only binding discard backing the `Request` release path; the compatibility `release_public_binding` stays inflight-tolerant for the legacy Completion ready edge) plus `CoreOccupancy::public_bindings`; two new fail-fast entries (`request_nonterminal_release_fail_fast`, `request_binding_invariant_fail_fast`) back the always-on diagnostics. `AsyncBackend`'s private submit seam is retyped from `Result<void>(Op, Completion<T>&)` to `Result<detail::RequestKey>(Op, Completion<T>*)` — a null completion publishes through the core only (no-op `publish_request_ready` thunk, nullable `DeliveryRecord::completion`) — and gains the private virtual `cancel_identity(RequestKey)` that routes into each backend's existing `cancel_key` arbitration. `AsyncIoContext` gains the four single-argument submissions returning `Result<Request<std::size_t>>`/`Result<Request<void>>`, `cancel(const RequestId&)` and `lookup(const RequestId&)`; the destructor and move-assign extend the context-outstanding diagnostic with the public-binding check (HANDLE-03). `Request<T>::cancel()` is defined once in `async_io_context.hpp` after `AsyncBackend` is complete. ThreadPool and uring backends share one submit implementation for both invocation forms; the no-liburing stub submits return `backend_error` keys and stay abstract (`cancel_identity` overridden only in the liburing configuration, matching the B1-D precedent) |
| Change | Public API addition plus a seam retyping; the compat `Completion` path's observable behavior is unchanged (the same core calls run with a non-null completion pointer). The backends' four submit overrides per backend collapse from completion-only to shared identity-returning forms; `unsupported_stub()` in the no-liburing uring TU lost its last reader to the retyping and is deleted. The new-path publication carries no `Completion` ready-atomic involvement: offer_terminal → begin/complete_publication → consumer's core-mutex acquire is the whole chain. Review-fix round: `Request`'s destructor/discard/move-assign-over release path moved from the compatibility `release_public_binding` (which must stay inflight-tolerant because a ready Completion's own release edge precedes the core's publication completion) to the published-only `discard_public_result`, so a `publication_inflight` slot is again a nonterminal for the public surface and the release fail-fasts; the Completion path's observable behavior is untouched |
| Semantic/regression evidence | Executable/model evidence is recorded per head, not collapsed into one "final head". The configuration battery below ran at head `29df935d` (the review-fix round re-ran every configuration after the fix commits `706400eb`/`8a95fc32`/`29df935d`); the only intervening delta to the PR head that the fix-round re-review examined, `2952f75d`, is documentation/formal-map ledger editing with no C++, model or test executable source change. The V14 short-write case below is the sole executable evidence added since `29df935d`; it was executed at its introducing commit on this branch in the configurations stated there. gcc debug `--liburing=y`: 48/48 registered tests — public request 13/13 on the ThreadPool build and 13/13 on the real-kernel io_uring build (the new `publication_inflight_window_keeps_the_request_nonterminal` case pins the window semantics deterministically on both backends), release-violation death suites 4/4 on both backends (destructor, move-assign-over, context-with-live-binding, and the new `discard_request_during_publication_inflight` which asserts `ready() == false` under the epilogue pause gate and then requires the always-on `SIGABRT`), ownership 8/8 + uring ownership 9/9 (both tracer-bullet cases migrated to the new API), protocol 34/34, publication 11/11, cutover 14/14 + 20/20 (seam + real-kernel), uring smoke, semantic precedence/conformance 0 divergences, admission 4/4, file-access precedence 40/40, 30-suite remainder. gcc release `--liburing=y` 48/48 — the death evidence therefore demonstrates the always-on diagnostics terminate in release builds (fork/exec death tests assert `SIGABRT`, never a debug-only assertion). gcc debug `--liburing=n` 41/41 and gcc release `--liburing=n` 41/41 (the two B2 uring-variant registrations are liburing-gated; the no-liburing library builds with the retyped abstract stub). asanubsan (clang) 48/48 full registered suite including both death suites. TSan 8/8 focused — public request ×2, death ×2, ownership ×2, cutover ×2 — zero reports (an unsynchronized shared read buffer between the two contexts of the move-over death scenario, latent since B2's first tests and surfaced by TSan timing on this round, was fixed by giving the pending submission its own buffer). Requirement-facet evidence naming (VERIFY-04): V21/HANDLE-03 = `public_request_release_violation_test` / `public_request_release_violation_uring_test` scenario `destroy_context_with_live_public_binding` (both backends, debug and release); V14 = `uring_core_cutover_test` case `cancel_racing_short_write_keeps_confirmed_count` (liburing=y, io_uring seam backend, injected CQEs: a `read_write` fixture takes a `submit_write` of 6 bytes, cancel intent is recorded on the running write, the physical completion then arrives as a successful short write of 3/6 — the slot observation pins the canonical terminal as `succeeded` with `confirmed_bytes == 3` over the racing cancel intent, the publication carries 3 with no canceled replacement and no zero-effect rollback fabricated, and the cancel control CQE then retires with full reclaim; 21/21 uring cutover cases pass with it in gcc release and gcc debug `--liburing=y`), with the earlier `cancel_racing_completion_keeps_the_confirmed_count` retained as the successful short-read flank of the same race (companion evidence; this record's earlier claim that that read case proved a successful short write was wrong and is withdrawn), backed by the backend-independent outcome oracle `semantic_effect_outcome_test` case `case_c_canceled_keeps_confirmed_bytes` (`canceled_racing_in_flight_attempt(9)` keeps `confirmed_bytes == 9` with `remaining == unknown`) and `prefix_only_terminal_cannot_carry_v15` (a racing-cancel outcome cannot carry a prefix-only terminal); the B2 pre-dispatch cancel case (`cancel_wins_before_dispatch_and_converges`) is CANCEL-01 floor evidence, not V14. Six B2 mutant binaries each killed by its named evidence (table below). `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Reused unchanged: the B1-B/B1-C delivery-pin and identity mutation kills (those paths are behaviorally untouched for non-null completions), B1-A identity-domain kills, the B1-C named mutation campaign (its machinery and binaries are unchanged) |
| Protocol/model evidence | `formal/tla/RequestCore.tla` extends B1-1 with the missing public-consumption distinctions only: action `Consume(s)` (bind live, terminal chosen, publication complete — `pub = "done"`, never inflight; atomically records the consumed ghost, releases the binding, promotes stage 3; consumer-driven, deliberately outside `Fair`), ghosts `consumed[i]` and `pubDoneIds`, invariants `InvConsumeAfterPublish` (consume presupposes this identity's completed publication — expressible only through the per-identity `pubDoneIds` fact because consume promotes stage past 2 in the same step) and `InvConsumedReleased`, plus mutant switches `MutConsumeKeepsBind` and `MutConsumeUnpublished`. Review-fix round: the model gained the public-discard distinction the C++ release-path fix required — action `DiscardPublic(s)` (public identity resolution with live binding and chosen terminal; guarded by `pub = "done"` unless mutant; atomically records the ghost, releases the binding, promotes stage 3 — consumer-driven, outside `Fair`), ghost `discarded[i]`, invariant `InvDiscardAfterPublish` (`discarded[i] => i ∈ pubDoneIds`, per-identity for the same stage-jump reason as consume), and mutant switch `MutDiscardInflight` (relaxes the gate to `pub ≠ "none"`); `ReleaseBind` remains deliberately inflight-tolerant as the compatibility release flavor (`Completion::release_binding_` → `release_public_binding`). All 22 cfgs carry the new constant; `RequestCore.cfg`/`RequestCoreLive.cfg` add `InvDiscardAfterPublish`; `RequestCoreMutDiscardInflight.cfg` joins the B2 stage; `scripts/verify_tla.sh` gains `rcore-mut-discard-inflight`. TLC rerun on the review-fix head (tla2tools v1.7.4, OpenJDK 25.0.4.1; the complete `scripts/verify_tla.sh` gate — legacy stages, rcore safety/liveness/mutant battery/witness, stage B2 — rerun end-to-end on a 20-core x86-64 Linux host with `VERIFY_TLA: PASS` and zero FAILs): safety clean (153,347,909 states generated / 26,662,372 distinct / 0 on queue, `Model checking completed. No error has been found.` — the discard branching grows the explored space ≈4.1× over the first B2 record) and liveness clean (`PROPERTY L1 L5` on the identical 153,347,909/26,662,372/0-on-queue graph, `Model checking completed. No error has been found.`; the 12 temporal-property branches checked during the run, final temporal pass 1h17m, rcore-live ≈18.5h wall on the grown graph) — the B1-1 stage facts (safety, L1/L5, 14 invariant mutants, 3 temporal mutants, subsumption witness) re-verified against the extended model; the three B2 mutants are killed on their named invariants (`MutConsumeKeepsBind` → `Invariant InvReleasedNotLive is violated`, 2,395 states; `MutConsumeUnpublished` → `Invariant InvConsumeAfterPublish is violated`, 91 states; `MutDiscardInflight` → `Invariant InvDiscardAfterPublish is violated`, 938 states — each count identical in a local pre-push run and in the gate). Consume correspondence: `Request::take_result` ≙ Consume (the `resolve_public_`/published gate is the action guard; the payload copy + `binding_live = false` + `try_reclaim_` is the atomic effect); `Request::try_result` ≙ the observation reading of the same state (no modeled action — observation does not change protocol state, and the stale→fail-fast mapping is the C++ projection of the identity-resolution guard shared with the release ops); `Request::discard`/destructor/move-assign ≙ `DiscardPublic` (published-only — the first record wrongly mapped this path to `ReleaseBind`, which is the inflight-tolerant compatibility flavor the legacy Completion release uses); `ctx.lookup` ≙ `lookup` (no action) |
| Publication/thread evidence | The new consumption path adds no lock, atomic or edge: `observe/consume_public_result` run under the core mutex, the same serializer that publishes, so a consumer that acquires the mutex after `complete_publication` reads the canonical outcome with acquire visibility — the B1 HB chain with the Completion ready-atomic removed from the new path entirely (the no-op thunk performs no user-visible write). The Request object itself follows THREAD-01: single-owner, move across threads with external synchronization, no concurrent member is data-race-free by fiat. The `cancel_identity` virtual is internally synchronized (backend dispatch/work mutex + core mutex) and never touches delivery records, so `Request::cancel` needs no context mutex and never blocks behind submit serialization. `AsyncBackend*` and `RequestCore*` inside a Request remain address-stable across context moves (the B1-A uniqueness discipline: the context's `unique_ptr`s are moved, never copied, and the pointees never relocate). Publication-window discipline (exercised deterministically by the new death scenario and the window case): while the progress owner is parked between `record.publish` and `complete_publication` it holds the context's `access_mtx_` inside `AsyncIoContext::poll`, so context-mediated probes (submit/poll/wait/`lookup`) from the user thread block until publication completes, whereas the object-level probes `ready()`/`try_result()` and the release decision touch only the core mutex, which the publisher does not hold mid-publication — the interleaving the HANDLE-01/HANDLE-02 release gate must judge. TSan runs above |
| Backend/kernel evidence | Real kernel ring (liburing 2.14, kernel 6.18.33, WSL2) for the uring public-request suite (13/13) and death suite (4/4), including the new publication-window discard scenario, the gate-window cancel win and the zero-op consumed-pin case on the live backend; the no-liburing configuration builds with the retyped stub. Configurations enumerated in the evidence row |
| Authority-contraction audit | Mechanism replacements: the `Result<void>` submit seam → the key-returning pointer form (the accepted identity now flows to the caller on every submission, shared by both invocation forms — this is what makes "post-accept failure must not hide accepted work" representable rather than invisible); the implicit "publication destination is always a `Completion`" coupling → nullable `DeliveryRecord::completion` + the no-op `publish_request_ready` thunk. Deleted: `unsupported_stub()` (last reader removed by the retyping). Redundancy check against pre-B2 mechanisms: nothing that B1 classified became redundant — the core's binding/terminal/publication/reclaim facts are exactly the authorities the new surface reads, `ReleaseBind` remains consumed by the legacy Completion compatibility release (`Completion::release_binding_`), distinct from Consume's stricter published-complete gate — the Request discard/destructor/move-assign path uses `DiscardPublic`/`discard_public_result`, not `ReleaseBind` — `lookup`/`cancel` arbitration are reused unchanged, and the B1-C physical mechanisms (cookie allocator, router, ledger, publication worklist, delivery records) keep their classifications. Compatibility residue unchanged in status, each with zero NEW consumers added by this slice: the `Completion` arena binding flavor, `AsyncIoContext::install_binding`, `detail::submit_transaction`, `detail::RequestArena` (with the `backend_ready` state — zero producers since B1-C) and `RequestHandleState::backend_ready` — retirement owned by #402 (the compat invocation form itself is deliberately retained there); `supports_request_identity`/`resolve_identity_state`/`identity_of` remain compat-only RequestHandle surface, with the new API reading the core directly so no second identity authority exists. Review-fix round: no mechanism became redundant and none was deleted; `release_public_binding` lost its only Request-path consumer and is now consumed solely by the compatibility Completion release (`Completion::release_binding_`), which is what justifies keeping its inflight-tolerant precondition; `discard_public_result` is a narrower precondition over the same core-owned publication facts — no new state, flag or counter, no second authority |
| Status | The #395 Phase B2 implementation slice is complete, and the independent final review of the first record returned REQUEST_CHANGES with one merge-blocking defect (public discard accepted a `publication_inflight` slot) and one evidence-naming gap (V14/V21); the review-fix round at head `29df935d` resolved the implementation defect — the published-only `discard_public_result` backs the Request release path with a deterministic publication-window death scenario on both production backends, release builds included, a named mutant killed by exactly that scenario, and the model split into `DiscardPublic` (published-only) versus `ReleaseBind` (compatibility, inflight-tolerant) with its mutant killed — and named V21 precisely. The re-review of that round returned CONDITIONAL_PASS with two P2 evidence/closure items, both addressed by this record: the V14 citation had described a short-read case while the root requirement names a successful short write (V14 now names the deterministic short-write race `cancel_racing_short_write_keeps_confirmed_count`, executed in release and debug), and the evidence records had conflated the executable-evidence head `29df935d` with the reviewed PR head `2952f75d` (now recorded per head with the intervening docs-only delta stated). Not claimed: observer/progress (#396/#397), cancel/effect matrix and backend refinement (#400), shutdown (#401), Completion and legacy retirement (#402). The assessment rows keep their NOT_ASSESSED-as-a-whole statuses; the narrow closure re-check has not run, the PR is not merged and #395 is not closed |

### B2 mechanism justification (AGENTS.md gate)

Every new runtime mechanism of this slice, against the gate's questions.

| Mechanism | Fact / owning authority | Justification | Derived? | Classification | Replaces; removal condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `RequestCore::observe_public_result` | Whether this identity's terminal is published, and the canonical payload if so (owner: the core) | HANDLE-01 requires a non-consuming ready probe; REQ-04 requires observation behind the publication handoff | No — a public read operation on the authority | Authority operation | None; part of the core's public contract for v1 |
| `RequestCore::consume_public_result` | One-time settlement: payload acquisition + public-binding release + reclaim eligibility (owner: the core, one mutex section) | HANDLE-01 requires consumption atomic with binding ownership so no interleaving can observe consumed-but-bound or bound-after-consumed; REQ-05 reclaims under the unchanged predicate | No | Authority operation | Replaces nothing; public Request discard uses `discard_public_result`, while `release_public_binding` remains compatibility-only for legacy Completion; no removal condition within v1 |
| `CoreOccupancy::public_bindings` | Count of live public bindings (owner: the core's `binding_live` facts) | HANDLE-03's destruction precondition must be checkable at context destruction; materialized because destruction is the only moment the obligation is enforced | Yes — derived summary over `binding_live` | Derived summary consumed by a contract diagnostic | Extends the existing context-outstanding check; removed only with the HANDLE-03 destruction precondition itself |
| `RequestId` | The public projection of the core identity (context, slot, generation), valid while the binding is live (owner: the core) | The root's copied-identity lookup/cancel semantics (stale/not_found after release) require an owning-handle-free identity value | Yes — a projection of `RequestKey` | Public value view of core authority | Replaces nothing (`RequestHandle`'s identity surface is compat); no removal condition within v1 |
| `Request<T>` | The single public settlement responsibility: empty or bound, one terminal consumption (owner: the core holds every fact; the object holds only the handle triple) | HANDLE-01/02: the root's public handle model — move-only, noexcept transfers, destructor policy, explicit discard. No mirrored booleans: every lifecycle answer is a core query | No (handle, not summary) | Authority handle | Replaces nothing yet; per-consumer `Completion` migration is #402. Removal: never within v1 (it is the product surface this slice exists to deliver) |
| `AsyncBackend::cancel_identity` (private virtual) | Cancel-by-identity routing into the backend's existing arbitration (owner: each backend's `cancel_key`) | CANCEL-01 preserved: the Request path must reach the same core arbitration + backend physical cancel without owning a `Completion`; internally synchronized so cancel never blocks behind submit serialization | No — routing, not state | Interface routing seam | Replaces nothing; removable only if identity-based cancel gains a non-virtual path, which no v1 mechanism provides |
| `publish_request_ready` no-op thunk + nullable `DeliveryRecord::completion` | Publication without a `Completion` destination (owner: the backend's delivery record) | REQ-04: the publication path must complete without requiring a `Completion<T>*`; the new path's HB proof must not route through Completion's ready atomic | The thunk is the null-destination publication effect; the nullability is a physical record field | Physical/compat decoupling | Replaces the implicit completion-only publication coupling; removed with the delivery records when #402 retires the compat publication path |
| `request_nonterminal_release_fail_fast` / `request_binding_invariant_fail_fast` | HANDLE-02 violation (destroying/overwriting a nonterminal responsibility) and the protocol-unreachable stale observation (owner: the contract, enforced at the Request surface) | Root-required always-on diagnostics — release builds included; not defensive insurance (the states they catch are reachable misuse, and the stale arm is the fail-stop for identity misuse the root forbids silently absorbing) | No | Contract diagnostics | None; stand only while the Request surface stands |
| Model `Consume(s)` + `consumed`/`pubDoneIds` ghosts | The consumption transition and its per-identity published presupposition (owner: the model's correspondence to the core op) | The B1-1 model could not express consume-before-publish (stage jumps past 2 in the same step), so `pubDoneIds` is the minimal per-identity fact that makes the presupposition statable and mutable | The ghosts monitor the modeled transition | Test/formal evidence (model-internal) | None; lives with the model |
| `RequestCore::discard_public_result` | Whether this identity's public terminal is established, and the binding discard that follows (owner: the core's `published`/`binding_live` facts) | HANDLE-01/02 require the public Request release to judge only completed publication: the compatibility release's inflight tolerance exists for the Completion ready edge and must not govern the public surface; a narrower precondition over the same facts, no new state | No — a public release operation on the authority | Authority operation (published-only flavor, distinct from the compatibility flavor) | Nothing replaced; `release_public_binding` remains for the compat Completion release. Removed only if the two release flavors ever collapse, which #402 owns |
| Model `DiscardPublic(s)` + `discarded` ghost + `InvDiscardAfterPublish` | The published-only public-discard transition and its per-identity presupposition (owner: the model's correspondence to `discard_public_result`) | The single `ReleaseBind` made the model looser than the C++ contract for the Request path; splitting the flavor keeps each release edge's guard truthful and gives `MutDiscardInflight` a killer | The ghost monitors the modeled transition | Test/formal evidence (model-internal) | None; lives with the model |

### B2 mutation evidence (C++ minimum discriminating set)

| Mutant define | Flipped mechanism | Killer (named, on the mutated build) |
|---|---|---|
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_CONSUME_KEEPS_BINDING` | `consume_public_result` returns the payload without releasing the binding or reclaiming | `public_request_test` `accepted_request_pends_until_publication`: "consumption released the binding and reclaimed", "the consumed identity stops resolving", "cancel by the consumed identity reports not_found" |
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_OBSERVE_IGNORES_PUBLICATION` | `observe_public_result` skips the published gate | `public_request_test` `accepted_request_pends_until_publication`: "try_result reports pending before publication" |
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_OBSERVE_CONSUMES` | `observe_public_result` releases the live binding | `public_request_test` `accepted_request_pends_until_publication`: the always-on stale-binding diagnostic terminates the process deterministically (the worker's final control release reclaims a slot the mutant unbound early); the same fault is discriminated by the retained-binding assertion "observing does not release the retained public binding" |
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_RELEASE_FORGETS_BINDING` | `Request::release_responsibility_` nulls the handle without releasing the binding | `public_request_test` `discard_releases_the_published_binding`: "discarding released the binding and reclaimed", "the discarded slot admits new work" |
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_NONTERMINAL_RELEASE_DETACHES` | The nonterminal release arm loses its fail-fast (treats `not_visible_yet` as released) | `public_request_release_violation_test` `destroy_nonterminal_request`: "the always-on diagnostic did not terminate" |
| `SLUICE_B2_MUTANT_DISCARD_ACCEPTS_INFLIGHT` | `discard_public_result` treats `publication_inflight` as published (the exact defect the review-fix round closes) | `public_request_release_violation_test` `discard_request_during_publication_inflight` (both backend builds carry the scenario; the mutant build is the ThreadPool seam configuration): "the always-on diagnostic did not terminate" — the scenario's `ready() == false` assertion under the epilogue pause gate proves the window is entered, and the strict gate SIGABRTs where the mutant releases silently |

## C1-A review record — Issue #427, observer state ownership migration out of the backend

This record implements the first #396 slice under the frozen boundary: the
observer registration authority moves from backend `DeliveryRecord` waiter
fields into the request domain. `RequestCore` now owns the registration
existence, the single active registration constraint (OBS-01 occupied
disposition), the already-terminal attach disposition and the
reclaim-visible observer pin (the REQ-05 "no queued/executing observer
delivery" conjunct, represented by the live registration itself). The
`AsyncBackend` interface and the shared `ReadyEvent` carry no host identity
vocabulary: `WaiterToken`/`RoutingLease` leave the backend-facing headers
(they survive only inside the legacy `RequestArena` compatibility unit) and
the ready event is keyed by request identity only. The Scheduler — the host
adapter — routes wakes through its own request-key-indexed registry and
retires the registration after routing the wake or cancelling the wait; the
`not_supported` fallback waiter branch is unreachable because every
context-owned core accepts registration. Physical submit/completion paths are
unchanged. Not claimed here: the attach/publication race oracle (#428), the
explicit Unattached→Armed→Queued→Delivering→Retired machine and the
retirement handshake (#429), external-host adapter migration (#430), legacy
mechanism removal (#431), ProgressSource alignment (#432), and the TLA+
update (#433).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | OBS-01 substrate halves this slice installs (at most one active registration per request with an explicit occupied disposition; registration existence as a core-owned fact; registration resource failure — the record-pool exhaustion path — retires the just-armed registration and leaves the accepted request owned; a host-neutral registration that never exposes Scheduler identity to a backend), REQ-05's observer-delivery conjunct materialized as the core's `observer_registered` pin (a published, binding-released, ref-free slot stays unreclaimable while a registration is live; retirement itself drives reclaim), OBS-03's "releasing a terminal Request binding is legal while a delivery remains pinned" (the pin outlives `binding_live`, so `retire_observer` resolves through the internal identity), ARCH-02/BACKEND-01 (backend owns physical execution only; no observer registration lifecycle in either backend). Excludes the full attach/terminal race oracle (#428), delivery state machine and retirement-suppression distinctions (#429), adapter migration evidence (#430), mechanism removal (#431) and ProgressSource (#432) |
| Implementation | New `include/sluice/async/detail/observer_protocol.hpp` (dispositions `ObserverRegistration{armed,duplicate,already_terminal,not_found}`, `ObserverRetirement{retired,not_registered,not_found}`). `RequestCore::Slot` gains `observer_registered`; `register_observer` (resolve public; occupied→duplicate; published→already_terminal without arming; otherwise arm) and `retire_observer` (resolve internal — the pin guarantees the slot stays accepted past binding release; clear + try-reclaim) run under the core mutex, so attachment resolves atomically with publication; `reclaimable_`/`release_slot_`/`reserve` reset and `SlotObservation` carry the fact. `AsyncIoContext` replaces `register_waiter`/`cancel_waiter` with `attach_observer(Completion&)`→`{status,key}`, `cancel_observer(Completion&)`→`{status,key}` and `retire_observer(RequestKey)`, resolving the identity through `AsyncBackend::identity_of` under the access mutex (`RequestHandle` befriends the context); the empty-compatible error mapping (`already_terminal`/`duplicate` + not-ready → `invalid_state`) preserves the previous external behavior. Both backends lose their `register_waiter`/`cancel_waiter` overrides, test seams and the four waiter fields of `DeliveryRecord`; `deliver_event` emits the host-neutral `ReadyEvent{key, kind}` with the `event_owed` control-ref pairing unchanged. `ready_sink.hpp` keeps only `OperationKind`/`ReadyEvent{key,kind}`/`SynchronousReadySink`; `WaiterToken`/`RoutingLease`/`OptionalWaiterDelivery` move to `request_slot.hpp` as legacy `RequestArena` vocabulary, and the arena's dead `reap` waiter-delivery emission is deleted. The Scheduler's `WaitRecord` carries the request key; `wait_by_request_slot_` (host-private wake index under `wait_registry_mtx_`) replaces token/lease routing; `on_ready` validates by key (generation/context/registered-state) and stages the delivered list; `drain_routed_completion_waits_locked` frees the routed record, retires the observer delivery, and only then makes the fiber runnable; `cancel_waiter` retires through `ctx_.cancel_observer` and wakes canceled only for a still-registered record (delivery already routed ⇒ the wait completes normally). `scheduler_identity_`, `wait_lease_serial_` and the token/lease construction are deleted; the `waiting_size_`/`waiting_void_` fallback maps lose their last inserters (removal is #431) |
| Change | Interface contraction plus authority move; the physical completion path (dispatch, terminal offer, publication worklist, event-owed pairing, progress signal) is byte-for-byte the same control flow. Observable behavior preserved: attach on a published request previously surfaced as `invalid_state` unless the completion was ready — the same mapping now derives from the explicit `already_terminal` disposition; a cancel during the delivery window previously reported `false` because the backend registration was already closed — the core pin now spans that window (publish→deliver→drain-retire), and `cancel_observer` may retire it, but the waiter completes normally because the host record is already delivered, so `cancel_waiter` still reports `false` |
| Semantic/regression evidence | gcc debug `--liburing=n`: 41/41 registered tests, including the four new core-level oracle cases in `request_core_protocol_test` — `observer_registration_arms_once_and_reports_occupied` (arm/duplicate/stale-not_found/retire), `attach_after_publication_returns_already_terminal` (published→already_terminal installs no pin; released binding→not_found; slot reclaims), `observer_registration_pins_reclaim_until_retirement` (binding may release while the delivery stays pinned; retirement itself drives reclaim; reuse clears residue), `observer_retirement_reports_unregistered_distinctly` (`not_registered` vs `not_found` dispositions) — and the rewritten deterministic interleaving case `threadpool_core_cutover_test` `observer_registration_and_delivery_ride_the_publication` (pause gate between accept and worker claim: arm/duplicate under the gate, delivery carries the request key, pin holds past publication, retirement releases, fresh attach returns already_terminal, final reclaim) plus `uring_core_cutover_test` `observer_registration_rides_publication` (fake-CQE driver; liburing-gated, source-maintained for that configuration). The migrated RuntimeTaskContext waiter path is exercised end-to-end by `explicit_file_ref_test` (await/cancel-wait races through the Scheduler on the ThreadPool backend) and the app consumption suites. `request_core_consumer_probe` still proves the substrate compiles standalone without waiter vocabulary. No new mutants this slice; the machine and race oracle it would discriminate belong to #428/#429 |
| Publication/thread evidence | `register_observer`/`retire_observer` are leaf operations under the core mutex; the already-terminal disposition's visibility is the core-mutex acquire that serializes with `complete_publication`. Scheduler lock order is unchanged and extended only at the leaf: global → access → core (attach/cancel/retire and poll nest exactly as `register_waiter` did); `on_ready` takes only `wait_registry_mtx_` and is invoked from the driver's poll under the access mutex, so no hook runs under a core/backend lock; retirement is deferred to the drain (after `ctx_.poll()` returns) rather than taken inside `on_ready`, keeping `retire_observer` off the poll call stack |
| Backend/kernel evidence | ThreadPool: full cutover suite green on the seam build. io_uring: the deterministic suite is source-maintained for the liburing configuration (not installable on this host; the seam file and the rewritten case compile under the target's include set and are executed by the liburing CI matrix). The no-liburing stub builds |
| Authority-contraction audit | Removed with the migration: both backends' `DeliveryRecord` waiter ownership (`registration`, `waiter_token`, `waiter_lease`, `waiter_delivery_present` — the duplicated backend registration state machines #396 names), the `AsyncBackend::register_waiter`/`cancel_waiter` virtuals and their `AsyncIoContext` pass-throughs, the `WaiterToken`/`RoutingLease`/`OptionalWaiterDelivery` definitions and waiter-carrying `ReadyEvent` from the shared backend interface header, the backend waiter test seams (`register_waiter_key_for_test`, `cancel_waiter_key_for_test`, `waiter_of_slot_for_test`, `sink_last_has_waiter/token/lease_id`), the Scheduler's `scheduler_identity_`, `wait_lease_serial_`, token/lease construction and the `not_supported` fallback branch (unreachable: every context core accepts registration). Newly materialized host-side: `WaitRecord::request_key` + `wait_by_request_slot_` — the #396 host-adapter wake routing index (scheduler/fiber identity and stale wake validation are now host-private by construction; keyed by the public request identity the event already carries; bounded by the record pool and the core's single-registration constraint). Core-side: `Slot::observer_registered` — the registration-existence authority fact OBS-01 requires, not derivable (the pin and occupied disposition are its projections). Compatibility residue with zero new consumers, removal owned by later slices: the legacy `RequestArena` waiter storage/API (its `reap` delivery emission deleted here; #431 removes the storage), the `waiting_size_`/`waiting_void_` fallback maps and their wake scans (unreachable since this slice; #431) |
| Status | C1-A (#427) implementation complete at this commit: backend interfaces are host-neutral, the core owns registration existence/constraint/pin/retirement, and the physical completion path is unchanged, all under the recorded deterministic evidence. Open and explicitly not claimed: attach/publication race oracle and duplicate/registration-failure determinism coverage (#428), the explicit five-state delivery machine with claim/retirement distinctions (#429), external-host adapter obligations (#430), legacy mechanism removal including the arena waiter storage and scheduler fallback maps (#431), ProgressSource/Armed→Queued alignment (#432), and the TLA+ model update (#433). The OBS assessment row stays NOT_ASSESSED |

## C1-B review record — Issue #428, observer registration ordering protocol

This record pins the OBS-01 attach/publication resolution semantics that the
C1-A authority move installed: attachment resolves atomically with
publication under the core mutex — either it returns `already_terminal`
(the publication is visible; no registration is installed) or it arms a
registration entitled to exactly one future terminal delivery, and it can
miss publication neither between checking readiness and registering nor by
treating a chosen-but-unpublished terminal as published. Terminal-before-
attach no longer surfaces as an `invalid_state` error anywhere: the core
returns the disposition, the completion's ready edge carries the acquire
visibility, and the host's already-terminal path proceeds on that edge.
Duplicate registration keeps the explicit occupied disposition;
registration failure (stale/foreign identity) installs no state and leaves
the accepted request owned, observable and cancelable; polling stays valid
with and without observers. Not claimed here: the explicit delivery state
machine and retirement handshake (#429), external-host adapter migration
(#430), mechanism removal (#431), ProgressSource alignment (#432), TLA+
(#433).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | OBS-01 whole (single active registration with explicit occupied disposition; registration generation/pinning basis; attachment atomically resolving with publication in both directions; already-terminal with acquire visibility; registration resource failure leaves the request owned; polling without observers; host-neutrality already delivered by C1-A), OBS-03's registration-failure arm (no operation cancel, no borrow end), REQ-04's visibility half for the already-terminal disposition (core-mutex serialization with `complete_publication` is the acquire edge the host's `Completion::ready()` pair publishes into). Excludes delivery claim/retirement discipline (#429), adapters (#430), removal (#431), progress (#432) |
| Implementation | No production semantic change against C1-A's head: this slice's production delta is the mutation seams only — `register_observer`'s publication-visibility arm is guarded by `SLUICE_C1_MUTANT_ATTACH_IGNORES_PUBLICATION` (arms even on a published slot) and `SLUICE_C1_MUTANT_ATTACH_TREATS_TERMINAL_AS_PUBLISHED` (reports already-terminal on terminal-chosen-but-unpublished), plus their registered manual-mutation targets. The ordering oracle itself is the deliverable |
| Change | Tests and mutation builds only in behavior-affecting surface; `xmake/tests.lua` extends the `request_core_target` helper with an optional extra define (mirroring the B2 `public_request_target` shape) |
| Semantic/regression evidence | gcc debug `--liburing=n`: 41/41 registered tests. New deterministic core-level cases in `request_core_protocol_test` (41 total): `attach_during_publication_window_arms_for_that_publication` (arms strictly inside the begin/complete publication window; the registration survives its own publication as the pending delivery and pins past binding release), `attach_on_terminal_choice_before_publication_still_arms` (terminal chosen, publication not begun: arms, duplicate still occupied, the registration rides the publication, polling concurrently valid), `registration_failure_preserves_accepted_ownership` (stale/foreign identities install nothing; the request stays outstanding, cancelable and settleable without observer participation). New cutover case `threadpool_core_cutover_test` `attach_after_terminal_choice_rides_the_pending_publication` (16th case; the worker's terminal is chosen with the publication queued but undriven — observed via the bounded pending-worklist wait — attach through the context API arms, the driven publication delivers the keyed event, the registration stays pinned until retirement, then reclaim). Mutation kills, executed and recorded: `request_core_mut_attach_ignores_publication` → killed by `attach_after_publication_returns_already_terminal` "attach after publication returns the already-terminal disposition" (3 failing assertions incl. the no-pin reclaim check); `request_core_mut_attach_treats_terminal_as_published` → killed by `attach_during_publication_window_arms_for_that_publication` "attach inside the publication window arms rather than reporting already-terminal" and `attach_on_terminal_choice_before_publication_still_arms` (the lost-observer direction: refusing to arm on a chosen-but-unpublished terminal would strand a waiting host) |
| Publication/thread evidence | Unchanged from C1-A: the resolution runs under the core mutex serialized with `begin/complete_publication`; the already-terminal acquire visibility is the mutex edge; the host mapping (non-armed + ready edge → proceed; else `invalid_state`) preserves the pre-C1 external behavior while the disposition itself is now protocol vocabulary |
| Backend/kernel evidence | The cutover case runs on the ThreadPool seam build; the uring deterministic suite is source-maintained for the liburing configuration (not installable on this host) |
| Authority-contraction audit | No new runtime mechanism; this slice moves no production authority. The two mutant defines are test-only evidence seams classified with the B2 mutation precedent (never compiled into registered test or production targets). Nothing became redundant: C1-A's mechanisms all keep their obligations |
| Status | C1-B (#428) complete at this commit: both OBS-01 ordering directions carry deterministic oracle cases with named mutation kills, duplicate/failure dispositions are pinned, and polling-without-observer validity is asserted. Open: delivery state machine, at-most-once claim and retirement distinctions (#429), RuntimeTaskContext adapter evidence (#430), legacy mechanism removal (#431), Armed→Queued/ProgressSource ownership (#432), TLA+ (#433). The OBS assessment row stays NOT_ASSESSED |

## C1-C review record — Issue #429, observer delivery claim and retirement protocol

This record implements the OBS-02 transition discipline as core-owned slot
state: `Unattached → Armed → Queued → Delivering → Retired`, with the
publication-coupled queueing edge (Armed→Queued at `complete_publication`),
the driver's exclusive delivery claim (Queued→Delivering in
`claim_observer_delivery`, invoked by both backends' `deliver_event` on the
progress driver before the ready event), cancellation edges
(`cancel_observer`: Armed→Retired wins, Queued→Retired suppresses the
delivery, Delivering reports `delivery_in_progress` and changes nothing),
and the delivery-retirement edge (`retire_observer_delivery`:
Delivering→Retired, taken by the host after its delivery hook). Delivery is
at most once per registration generation because the claim is the single
Queued→Delivering edge and publication itself is once per slot generation.
Observer cancellation never cancels the operation: the cancel arms touch
only the observer phase; execution, terminal arbitration, publication and
result visibility are untouched. Retirement is distinguishable from
delivery-in-progress both by phase and by the `delivery_in_progress`
disposition, and the REQ-05 pin is exactly the live registration
(unattached/retired are the only reclaimable phases). The boundary holds:
the core owns the retirement fact and the reclaim-visible pin; the host
owns its wait record (callback state) and wake routing until it acquires
retirement — the scheduler now retires deliveries through
`retire_delivery` at the drain, cancels through `cancel_observer`, and
never runs a hook under a core/backend lock. Not claimed: external-host
adapter obligations (#430), mechanism removal (#431), ProgressSource
alignment (#432), TLA+ (#433).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | OBS-02 whole (five-phase discipline with the root diagram's exact edges; exclusive bounded nonthrowing delivery claimed by the driver after publication and outside core/backend locks — the ready-event sink hook; at-most-once per registration generation; cancellation distinguishing retired from delivery-in-progress; the host owning the queued wake's lifetime and stale validation once delivery is copied into its queue), OBS-03's cancellation/timeout arms (registration retirement never cancels the operation or ends the borrow; releasing a terminal binding legal while a delivery stays pinned — `cancel/retire` resolve through the internal identity because the pin keeps the slot accepted), REQ-05's observer conjunct re-expressed as the phase predicate. Excludes adapters (#430), removal (#431), progress (#432) |
| Implementation | `observer_protocol.hpp` gains `ObserverPhase` and the operation vocabularies (`ObserverDeliveryClaim{claimed,none}`, `ObserverCancellation{retired,delivery_in_progress,not_registered,not_found}`, `ObserverDeliveryRetirement{retired,not_registered,not_found}`; the C1-A `ObserverRetirement` is superseded). `RequestCore::Slot::observer_registered` becomes `observer_phase`; `register_observer` treats retired as re-attachable and everything live as the occupied disposition; `complete_publication` queues an armed registration; `claim_observer_delivery` claims exclusively from queued; `cancel_observer` and `retire_observer_delivery` implement the two retirement edges with `try_reclaim_` on release; `reclaimable_` admits only unattached/retired. Both backends' `deliver_event` claim before emitting the host-neutral event (the compat progress event stays unconditional; the claim gates the protocol transition, not the physical reporting). `AsyncIoContext` splits its surface: `cancel_observer(Completion&)`/`cancel_observer(RequestKey)` wrap the cancel op (the key form serves host cleanup of its own failed resource acquisition, e.g. the wait-record exhaustion path — a consumer obligation C1-G later removes with the reserve-before-arm ordering), `retire_delivery(RequestKey)` wraps the delivery retirement (drain path); the C1-A `retire_observer` is superseded. The scheduler drain frees the routed record and retires the delivery before making the fiber runnable; `cancel_waiter` maps `retired`→canceled wake/true, `delivery_in_progress`/`not_registered`→false (the wait completes normally) — externally identical to the C1-A mapping, now derived from protocol dispositions |
| Change | Representation refinement of the C1-A fact plus two new core operations and the claim site in the drivers; the physical completion path gains exactly one core call (`claim_observer_delivery`) executed under the same access-mutex window the event already ran in. The C1-A/C1-B oracle cases were re-expressed in the phase vocabulary (their intent unchanged) and extended with the claim/retire edges |
| Semantic/regression evidence | gcc debug `--liburing=n`: 41/41 registered tests; `request_core_protocol_test` now 45 cases including the four new ones — `delivery_claim_is_exclusive_and_at_most_once` (no claim from armed/unpublished; publication queues; first claim wins; second claim refused; none after retirement; reclaim after retire+release), `observer_cancellation_never_cancels_the_operation` (cancel retires armed; the request stays outstanding, executes, terminalizes, publishes with visible result, owes no delivery, reclaims), `queued_cancellation_suppresses_the_delivery` (cancel after queueing retires, claim impossible twice over, publication visibility unaffected, reclaim normal), `cancel_during_delivery_is_distinguishable_and_delivery_completes` (in-progress disposition, phase untouched, repeated cancels stay in-progress, the retained delivery then retires, post-retirement cancel reports not_registered). The ThreadPool cutover observer case now asserts the queued→claimed sequence around the driven publication and that `cancel_observer` during the claimed delivery reports in-progress; the uring case asserts the delivering phase before delivery retirement (liburing-gated source). Mutation kills, executed and recorded: `request_core_mut_delivery_claim_unbounded` → killed by `delivery_claim_is_exclusive_and_at_most_once` ("an armed-but-unpublished registration owes no claimable delivery yet"/"a second claim is refused"); `request_core_mut_cancel_during_delivery_returns` → killed by `cancel_during_delivery_is_distinguishable_and_delivery_completes` ("cancellation during delivery reports in-progress, not retired"); `request_core_mut_publication_skips_queue` → killed by `observer_registration_pins_reclaim_until_retirement` ("the pin is the queued registration itself") and `delivery_claim_is_exclusive_and_at_most_once` ("publication moved the registration to queued"); the C1-B attach mutants re-killed unchanged after the vocabulary change. The RuntimeTaskContext waiter path remains green end-to-end (`explicit_file_ref_test` await/cancel races, app consumption suites) |
| Publication/thread evidence | All phase edges run under the core mutex; the claim runs inside the driver's access-mutex window immediately before the sink hook, so claim/cancel are serialized with each other and with publication by the existing lock order (global → access → core; no hook under any of them). The delivery hook remains the sink call after `complete_publication`, invoked only from the progress driver (`poll`/`wait_one`), never inline by submission or a worker; the host's record lifetime spans claim→drain-retirement exactly as OBS-02's retained-state rule requires |
| Backend/kernel evidence | ThreadPool seam build: cutover suite green with the claim in place; the uring driver mirrors the identical one-line claim; the no-liburing stub builds |
| Authority-contraction audit | Replaced: the C1-A boolean `observer_registered` and its `retire_observer` operation — superseded by the phase machine with two retirement edges whose preconditions the boolean could not distinguish (this is the #396-mandated split, not a second authority: one field, one mutex, five values). New mechanisms: `claim_observer_delivery` (the exclusive at-most-once edge — the protocol fact OBS-02 requires, not derivable) and the three-value cancellation disposition (the in-progress arm is the distinguishability #396 decision 1 demands; not derivable from retired/not_registered alone). No host mechanism moved into the core: the wait record, wake queue and stale-key validation remain scheduler-private; the core saw only request identities. Nothing else became redundant — the reclaim conjunct is a projection of the same phase fact |

## C1-D review record — Issue #430, RuntimeTaskContext waiter migration through the host adapter

This record delivers the RuntimeTaskContext waiter-path migration evidence
the frozen boundary demands. The structural migration happened with C1-A
(the interface contraction left no other route): `await_completion` arms
the core-owned registration, the fiber suspends on the host-private
wait-record registry keyed by request identity, the publication-driven
ready event routes the wake through the scheduler's sink, and the drain
retires the host record and the delivery before the fiber is made
runnable. That order is the shipped behavior of every C1 slice: the
drain first returns the wait record to the free pool (the host record is
retired before anything can reuse it), then establishes the observer
retirement fact through `retire_delivery` (the REQ-05 pin releases while
the delivery's request is still identified), and only afterward performs
the runnable transition — the wake is published last, so the resumed
fiber observes a fully retired registration and a reclaimable slot
rather than a live one. This slice adds the
dedicated deterministic adapter oracle and removes the dead historical
seam layer that had made any internal-testing build of the scheduler
uncompilable: the eleven scheduler/select TUs' `sluice_async_test` blocks
referenced a test-control framework whose implementation was deleted from
the repository in the legacy cleanup (its `async_test_control_internal.hpp`
no longer exists anywhere), so those guards were unreachable dead code;
they are stripped (including the two `e9t_record_*` park arms, collapsed
to their production `#else` bodies) while the self-contained seams (park
ledger, scheduler test access, the fe2 seam TU) are kept. The dead
`scheduler_identity` accessor is removed with the field C1-A deleted. Not
claimed: legacy waiter mechanism removal (#431 — the fallback maps and
arena waiter storage stay), ProgressSource alignment (#432), TLA+ (#433).
