Explicitly not claimed: ThreadPool no-lost-wake arm/ack/snapshot/recheck/park campaign (C2-B), real-ring CQ convergence/saturation proof (C2-C), owner exclusivity enforcement, sticky control, deadline/health outcomes, external-host stop/settle/detach lifecycle (C2-D), TLA+ model and V10/V11/V12/V23(progress)/V25 closure (C2-E) |

### C2-A six decisions

D1 — minimum persistent ProgressSource state: (1) the owned notification fd
(the pollable primitive itself); (2) `progress_epoch_` — the actionable-progress
notification epoch (epoch, not outstanding-count; written by signal/interrupt
under the leaf lock; read by the driver wait protocol; the comparison basis of
the no-lost-wake handshake); (3) `control_epoch_` — the control-request epoch
(writer: interrupt; reader: wait reason); (4) `armed_` + `armed_control_epoch_`
— the committed-wait handshake consumed by the scheduler admission protocol
(driver-only under THREAD-01); (5) `bound_readiness_fd_` — the backend-lent
poll-only physical readiness fd. No health state, no deadline state, no owner
token (D6), no wake counters (derived away), no request counts. Epoch wrap has
no reset path in C2-A; the exhaustion boundary model is C2-E/V24.

D2 — Linux pollable primitive: one context-owned eventfd created in
`AsyncIoContext` construction and closed by the source's destructor.
Construction failure (eventfd(2) failing) propagates out of context
construction as an explicit error; there is no CV-only degraded fallback. The
host may borrow the fd via `progress_notification_fd()` for event-loop
registration only; draining/rearming is owned by
`ProgressSource::acknowledge_notification()` (D5). The W-03 host example is
C2-B scope.

D3 — backend seam: `BackendProgressPort` = `signal()` (actionable progress may
exist) + `bind_physical_readiness(int fd)` (backend lends its own fd for
poll-only park inclusion; context never reads/writes/closes it). The port
cannot wait, snapshot, arm, consume, acknowledge, interrupt, or reach the
context notification fd (compile-time enforced in the evidence test). It is
installed exactly once by the context constructor before any work can exist;
there is no rebind path — a second readiness lend fails fast (enforced since
the review-fix commit, previously conventional). Rationale per method: signal is required by the 11
live backend transition sites (ThreadPool 5, io_uring 6) and derivable nowhere else; the readiness lend
is required because ring CQ readiness is kernel-side state the backend alone
knows; neither leaks host-wait or owner-control semantics, and neither allows
the backend to drain host readiness.

D4 — io_uring binding boundary: the notification fd is context-owned; the ring
fd remains entirely backend-private (created/closed by the backend's ring
state); host code never learns the ring fd (no API carries it across the
context boundary); the C2-A binding capability is the poll-only lend. Kernel
eventfd registration (`io_uring_register_eventfd`) was investigated: it binds
CQ notifications to the context fd, but a parked wait that drains the fd blind
can strand a CQE that arrived before the drain, so convergence is only correct
with the ack-before-recheck park discipline — C2-C owns that campaign. The port
seam is frozen so C2-C can swap the internal park mechanism (registered eventfd
instead of lent ring fd) without moving authority again.

D5 — stale readiness acknowledgement: draining/rearming the context
notification fd belongs exclusively to the context-owned ProgressSource —
internally before each park (drain-under-check-lock) and publicly through
`acknowledge_progress_notification()`. The backend has no drain capability, and
the lent readiness fd is poll-only, so no second authority exists after the
cutover (the backend-owned control eventfd is deleted).

D6 — progress-owner representation: no owner runtime state is added in C2-A.
One-active-owner/no-recursive-drive remains THREAD-01 caller discipline
expressed through the context's wait/poll entry points; no C2-A mechanism
consumes owner identity, so an ownership token would be speculative state.
C2-D owns enforcement and the sticky control/outcome machinery.

### C2-A mechanism justification

| Mechanism | Fact represented | Owning authority | Why required | Derivable? | Bound/lifetime | Replaces | Removal condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ProgressSource` storage (context member) | The context's persistent progress notification authority | AsyncIoContext | ARCH-01 ownership edge; the driver and future external host need one stable wait/notification object | No — it is the authority | Lives and dies with the context; heap object, stable address across context move | `ReadyWaitSource` + `UringWaitSource` as host contracts | Never (it is the target authority) |
| Notification eventfd | The Linux pollable notification primitive (W-03 direction) | ProgressSource (RAII create/close) | PROG-03 requires a pollable handle per usable backend; CV-only is insufficient | No | One fd per context; saturation tolerates EAGAIN with readiness persisting | The backend-owned control eventfd in `UringWaitSource` | C2-C may change the internal park mechanism; the fd stays |
| `progress_epoch_` / `control_epoch_` | Notification epochs for progress and control transitions | ProgressSource | PROG-02 wait-handshake comparison basis; wake-reason distinction | No — persistent comparison state, not derivable from queues | u64, monotonic, no reset in C2-A | The per-source epochs of both deleted sources | Never (authority state) |
| `armed_` + `armed_control_epoch_` | Committed-wait handshake state | ProgressSource (driver-only) | The scheduler admission protocol (arm → wait_one) already depends on it; losing it would break the existing no-lost-wake parking | No | Single-owner driver state, no bound issue in C2-A | Same fields in both deleted sources | Subsumed/refined by the C2-B handshake campaign |
| `BackendProgressPort` (signal + readiness lend) | The minimum backend→context physical signaling capability | AsyncBackend holds it; AsyncIoContext installs it | Backends must report physical progress and (uring) physical readiness without host/owner semantics | No — kernel-side readiness is not derivable by the context | Value object; source pointer valid for backend lifetime ≤ source lifetime | `AsyncBackend::wait_source()` + full `BackendWaitSource` interface | Never in v1; C2-C may add the registration capability alongside |
| `bound_readiness_fd_` (poll-only lend) | Backend physical readiness included in context parks | Context borrows; backend owns the fd | Ring CQ readiness must wake a parked owner before C2-C convergence lands | No — kernel CQ state is invisible to user epochs until reaped | Borrow only; valid while the backend is attached (backend retires first; no wait can be in flight during teardown per THREAD-01) | `UringWaitSource`'s ring-fd polling inside a backend object | C2-C kernel eventfd registration, if adopted |
| `signals_physical_progress()` probe | Whether the backend produces physical progress at all | AsyncBackend (context re-exposes via `has_split_wait_capability`) | Preserves the existing scheduler park decisions; the ApplicationRuntime build gate and the context wait refusal admit only progress-signaling backends | No — the context cannot know whether a backend will ever signal | Static capability | `wait_source() != nullptr` probe | C2-B may fold it into the progress-contract capability set |
| Admission-close wake as progress signal | "Owner should re-check; shutdown policy may apply" | Backend emits via port; context owns meaning | Root requires `close_admission()` to wake a potentially sleeping owner | No | Transient signal; sticky control taxonomy is C2-D | `interrupt_all()` called by backends | C2-D control outcomes |

### C2-A signal-source audit

| Transition | Classification |
|---|---|
| ThreadPool worker physical completion → publication → signal | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED (port signal) |
| ThreadPool/uring zero-op publication → signal | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED |
| ThreadPool dispatch-failure injection → signal (test-only path) | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED |
| ThreadPool cancel `won_before_execution` → signal | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED |
| uring physical completion reaped by any driver → signal | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED |
| uring CQ arrival while a driver is parked | PRESERVED via level-triggered ring-fd lend; kernel-notification convergence NEEDS_C2_C |
| uring poison/recover and newly-poisoned dispatch → signal | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED |
| uring cancel `won_before_execution` / running-cancel CQE | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED (running cancel wakes through ring readiness) |
| uring internal `wait_one` self-signals (2 sites) | DEAD with the deleted backend blocking wait |
| ThreadPool/uring `close_admission` owner wake | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED as a progress wake; distinguishable control outcome NEEDS_C2_D |
| ThreadPool normal dispatch → `work_cv_` only | NOT_A_PROGRESS_OBLIGATION (worker coordination; the driver obligation materializes at publication) |
| Core dispatch/control/reclaim obligations waking the source directly | NEEDS_C2_B (core-obligation signaling campaign; today they surface through the publication paths above) |
| Observer delivery | ALREADY_SIGNALLED_CORRECTLY (rides the publication chain) |
| Stop/admission wake at context level (`interrupt_progress_waiters`) | SIGNAL_ROUTE_MIGRATED (context-owned interrupt epoch); sticky/acknowledged semantics NEEDS_C2_D |

### C2-A discrimination evidence

M1 — backend reroutes signals to private state: killed by
`context_owns_one_progress_source`/`uring_zero_op_signals_context_notification`
(a private sink leaves the context notification fd unreadable and the parked
wake never fires). M2 — move leaves the backend bound to a stale source:
killed by the move continuity tests (a source replaced instead of moved loses
the fd continuity assertion and the parked driver never wakes; the port has no
rebind path, so the stale binding cannot even be repaired). M3 — backend gains
broad ProgressSource authority: compile-time killed (`port_has_no_wait_api`
concept; `backend_has_no_wait_source_member`). M4 — destruction order
invalid: the DtorSignalBackend test signals from the backend destructor; a
source-destruction-first mutant is a use-after-free under the ASan+UBSan
configuration (both variants pass there), and the explicit `backend_.reset()`
encodes the order.

### C2-A review-gate answers

1. Without backend-specific names, a host can wait on the context through
`wait_one`/`progress_notification_fd()` — yes (the io_uring ring fd never
crosses the context boundary). 2. The backend cannot decide host waiting
semantics — it can only signal and lend a poll-only fd; park policy lives in
the context. 3. The ProgressSource is genuinely context-owned storage, not a
pointer pass-through. 4. The port exposes no wait/ack/arm/control operation
(compile-time proof). 5. A context move keeps the same heap source, so no
stale binding is possible (no rebind API exists). 6. Backend teardown cannot
race source destruction: the destructor retires the backend first and
destruction is exclusive under THREAD-01. 7. No Scheduler/Fiber/Observer
identity appears in `progress_source.hpp` (no such symbol is includable
there). 8. No arm/ack campaign, kernel registration, owner enforcement,
deadline/health API, or host lifecycle was implemented. 9. Every retained
mechanism carries an owner and a removal/refinement stage (table above).
10. Every new mechanism is in the justification table; the wake counters of
the deleted uring source were judged redundant under the epoch-return design
and removed rather than migrated.

## C2-B review record — Issue #397, ThreadPool no-lost-wake + external-loop W-03 half

Stage-local status: `C2_B_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW`. This record claims the
C2-B exit gate only: the ThreadPool owner loop follows the concrete PROG-02
progress protocol and is race-wise unable to sleep past actionable work; the
ThreadPool half of W-03 is exercised by a real external `poll(2)` loop; the
progress-token exhaustion boundary required by PROG-02 exists in production for
both token domains (saturating epochs with exhaustion-sequence freshness);
the #394 L5 progress half for ThreadPool has executable mechanism evidence. It
closes no io_uring convergence (C2-C), no owner/outcome/external-host lifetime
policy (C2-D), and no formal obligation (C2-E).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | #397 stage C2-B only, under PROG-01/02/03 (no-lost-wake wait conditions, epoch-exhaustion boundary, pollable notification operation), ARCH-01 (core → progress obligation edge stays mediated), THREAD-01/02 constraints, W-03 ThreadPool half, the #394 L5 progress half mapped to ThreadPool, VERIFY-03 L2/L5 ThreadPool-side mechanism. Explicitly excluded: real-ring CQ convergence and saturation campaign (C2-C), owner-exclusivity enforcement, sticky control outcome, deadline/interrupt/health outcome taxonomy, external-host stop/settle/detach lifetime (C2-D), TLA+ model and V10/V11/V12/V23(progress)/V24/V25 closure (C2-E). RequestCore, Observer, Request<T>, Scheduler and Fiber semantics untouched |
| Implementation | Base `86bf1cb3817958f0d75503abad66c71adf18262f` (live master at start; equals the expected C2-A merge baseline; no intervening commits). Implementation commits `398695bc9f0129d50d753580f6a1f13d48eae72a` (handshake), `cf3314b52a5ffb8a2f83984f72ecf883a371b0ed` (race campaign), `09949424fd019328d97dd94af3d35275773c4213` (external loop) on branch `feat/397-c2-b-threadpool-no-lost-wake`; the PR may carry additional docs-only commits. `AsyncIoContext::wait_one` runs the canonical sequence (token snapshot → one bounded pass → authoritative recheck → `wait_if_unchanged`); `ProgressSource::wait_for_change` is renamed `wait_if_unchanged` with the fused revalidate+ack semantics explicit; new `ProgressPass {completed, immediate_work_remains, accepted_work_remains}` flows from a new `AsyncBackend::poll_progress()` virtual (ThreadPool overrides; io_uring inherits the honest default — no uring behavior change) through a public `AsyncIoContext::poll_progress()`; public `poll()` delegates; `ProgressSource::signal()` saturates at `UINT64_MAX` and hands freshness to the domain's exhaustion sequence (a later review fix; round 1 used a sticky `exhausted_` bool, derived now as `progress_exhaustion_ != 0`) and the context closes core admission at the owner's next pass; `interrupt()` follows the same saturation discipline for the control domain; test-only seams: prerevalidate/prepark pause gates inside the park, progress/control epoch setters, a one-write eventfd saturation primitive, an exhaustion query |
| Protocol before | `wait_one` loop: `snapshot()` → `backend_->poll()` returning only a completion count → `outstanding()==0` early return → `wait_for_change(token)` park. The park primitive already revalidated the token and drained stale readiness under one mutex hold (C2-A discipline), so the park itself was race-safe, but (1) the loop had no authoritative post-pass fact: a bare count cannot distinguish "no immediate work" from "work the pass did not reach", so neither a driver nor a host could implement PROG-02 clause 3 without guessing; (2) there was no external-host progress operation at all — `poll()`'s count plus `acknowledge_progress_notification()` left the W-03 owner loop shape unimplementable as specified; (3) the epoch was an unbounded `uint64_t` with a silent wrap and no exhaustion reaction; (4) the final operation was not named/stated as the root's `wait_if_unchanged` |
| Protocol after | Per iteration: `observed = snapshot()` (L2) → `pass = backend_->poll_progress()` under `access_mtx_` (L3; ThreadPool drains `publication_pending_` to empty under `work_mtx_`, delivers every `event_owed`, then reports `immediate_work_remains` from a final `work_mtx_`-held observation and `accepted_work_remains` from `core_->occupancy().outstanding != 0`) → `pass.completed > 0` returns → `!immediate && !accepted` returns 0 (idle) → otherwise `wait_if_unchanged(observed, remaining)` (L4/L5). Deadline and interrupt behavior preserved byte-for-byte from C2-A (deadline expiry interrupts the park, one final bounded pass runs, I/O is never cancelled; final outcome taxonomy stays C2-D). The external host protocol is now expressible: on notification-fd readability, `acknowledge_progress_notification()` then bounded `poll_progress()` passes until neither `completed` work nor `immediate_work_remains` remains, then return to `poll(2)` |
| SAFE_TO_PARK | Parking on the ThreadPool path happens only when all of: (1) a bounded nonblocking pass completed (`poll_progress` returned; its terminating condition is `publication_pending_` observed empty under `work_mtx_` and the `event_owed` scan exhausted); (2) `pass.completed == 0`; (3) `pass.immediate_work_remains == false` (no driver-actionable work exists at the pass boundary); (4) `pass.accepted_work_remains == true` (else `wait_one` returns 0 and never parks); (5) `observed` is the token snapshotted under the source mutex before the pass; (6) `wait_if_unchanged` revalidates epoch and exhaustion-sequence equality in both domains (`progress_epoch_/progress_exhaustion_`, `control_epoch_/control_exhaustion_`) and drains stale notification readiness under one mutex hold; (7) every producer bumps its domain's epoch (or, once saturated, its exhaustion sequence) under that same mutex before writing the eventfd, so a signal racing after the revalidation either flips an observed value before the next revalidation or its write lands after the drain and leaves kernel readiness that returns `poll(2)` immediately. Stale acknowledgement consumes only notifications whose producer transitions were already visible to the revalidation (they return before draining), so an ack can never discard an unobserved fresh obligation; ack ≠ request consumption ≠ epoch consumption ≠ actionable-work clearing — the underlying facts stay authoritative and `poll_progress` re-derives them every pass |
| Linearization points | L1 = stale-notification acknowledgement: `drain_notification_nolock_()` under `ProgressSource::mtx_`, fused with L4 in one lock hold. L2 = observed-token linearization: `snapshot()` under `mtx_` before the pass. L3 = bounded progress-pass boundary: `backend_->poll_progress()` returns under `access_mtx_`; the `ProgressPass` is the authoritative post-pass state. L4 = final safe-to-park validation: epoch/exhaustion revalidation under `mtx_` inside `wait_if_unchanged`. L5 = kernel park entry: `poll(2)` on the notification fd (+ lent readiness fd) after the `mtx_` release. No-lost-wake per interval: signals between L2→L4 are caught by the L4 revalidation (the producer's mutex-guarded value change — epoch bump, or exhaustion-sequence bump once the epoch is saturated — precedes the fd write under the same mutex); signals between L4's unlock and L5, or during L5, leave eventfd readiness that wakes L5 because their writes follow bumps that postdate the L1 drain; post-wake, the loop re-enters at L2. Fusing L1 with L4 is what makes the ack safe; the fused pair satisfies PROG-02's "stale acknowledged before the final actionable-work check" intent because the drain can only remove readiness already reflected in the validated token |
| Signal-source audit (ThreadPool) | worker physical completion → `publication_pending_` + port signal: CREATES_ACTIONABLE_DRIVER_WORK, persisted and signalled. zero-op inline publication → `acquire_control` + `event_owed` + port signal: CREATES_ACTIONABLE (observer delivery) + CREATES_RECLAIM_OBLIGATION (control ref pins the slot until `poll` delivers and releases). cancel `won_before_execution` → `publication_pending_` + signal: covered. dispatch failure after acceptance (injection) → `publication_pending_` + signal: covered. normal dispatch → `work_cv_` only: CREATES_PHYSICAL_WORK_ONLY (worker-actionable; the driver obligation materializes at publication, which signals) — correctly unsignalled. admission close → core `admission_open_=false` + signal: CREATES_CONTROL_OBLIGATION, persists in core state (distinguishable outcome C2-D). observer delivery → rides the publication chain (`publish_one` → `deliver_event`) — covered. control-reference retirement and slot reclaim → inline `try_reclaim_` on the acting thread inside `release_control`/`release_execution`/binding release under the core mutex — reclaim completes on the acting thread; no driver obligation, no signal needed. accepted-but-undispatched work → `dispatch_` ring + `work_cv_` (worker-side), visible to the owner as `accepted_work_remains`; its completion signals |
| Core/reclaim obligation audit (#394 L5 progress half, ThreadPool) | The concrete stranded-obligation case — request terminal+published, public binding already released, only the delayed control/reclaim obligation remaining, no new I/O — is created by zero-op publication (`acquire_control` + `event_owed`), is announced by the zero-op port signal, persists as `control_refs==1 ∧ event_owed ∧ slot accepted` until the owner's `poll_progress` delivers the event and releases the control ref (whose `try_reclaim_` then frees the slot), and is proven by `r13_delayed_reclaim_obligation_is_serviced_without_new_io` (obligation observed, then observed serviced while the only other request stays gated at its worker). Core → ProgressSource boundary: no direct core capability was added. Every driver-actionable core transition on the ThreadPool path materializes through a backend transition that already signals, and every purely core-local transition reclaims inline on the acting thread under the core mutex without needing the driver; adding a core-held signal capability would duplicate an existing authority edge (ARCH-01's dashed edge remains realized through the backend port) |
| Race matrix | Deterministic pause-gate tests in `threadpool_progress_race_test` (18/18 at release, release-no-uring, debug, clang ASan+UBSan, clang TSan; 5 repeated release runs green): R1 = `r1_signal_before_wait_is_serviced_without_parking` (pre-armed signal serviced by the first pass, `prepark==0`). R2 = `r2_signal_between_token_and_ack_is_revalidation_caught` (prerevalidate-gate signal; revalidation returns before drain/poll; `prepark==0`). R3 = `r3_signal_after_ack_wakes_poll_before_next_snapshot` (post-ack signal; poll wakes; next snapshot forwards; `prepark==1`). R4 = `r4_worker_completion_signal_before_final_recheck_is_caught` (worker-completion signal in the pass→park interval). R5 = `r5_postdrain_signal_readiness_persists_into_poll` (+ r4's window; readiness observable during the pause). R6/R7 = r2's production window — the implementation fuses [pass-return → actionable recheck → final token check] into one contiguous region whose tail is the L4 revalidation, so both intervals are exercised by the same gated pair (zero-op signal and worker-completion signal variants). R8 = `r8_empty_pass_signal_immediately_before_poll_entry` (empty pass, ack done, signal at the paused owner; the not-readable → readable transition is observed during the pause). R9 = `r9_signal_while_blocked_in_poll_wakes_owner`. R10 = `r10_multiple_signals_coalesce_into_one_readiness` + `coalesced_prearmed_signals_are_all_discoverable_in_one_pass` (8 coalesced signals, one readiness, one pass delivers all 8). R11 = `r11_spurious_wake_with_zero_completions_is_harmless` (control wake with zero completions; no fabricated result; safe re-park) + `r11b_stale_readiness_drained_at_park_without_fabrication`. R12 = `r12_control_wake_racing_park_boundary` (interrupt at both park-boundary gates; bounded return 0; sticky outcome taxonomy untouched). R13 = `r13_delayed_reclaim_obligation_is_serviced_without_new_io`. R14 = external-loop `host_acknowledgement_racing_new_signal_never_strands`. R15 = `r15_saturated_signal_between_pass_and_revalidation_is_not_drained` (epoch saturated via seam; the in-flight worker's completion signal lands while the owner pauses at the prerevalidate gate, i.e. between the empty pass and the L4 drain; the owner must service the completion without entering the park — `prepark==0`, no timeout fallback; this is the interval the review found uncovered). Control-domain exhaustion = `control_exhaustion_saturates_without_alias_and_wakes_interrupted` (saturated control epoch stays `MAX` with `control_exhaustion==1`; the in-window interrupt wakes the owner as interrupted, `prepark==0`, no fabricated completion). The §14 empty-poll adversarial shapes: before-final-check = r2's window; after-final-check = r3; immediately-before-poll-syscall = r8 — all return the completion without any timeout fallback (a timeout firing is asserted against, never relied on) |
| External-loop ThreadPool evidence | `threadpool_external_loop_test` (5/5, same matrix): application-owned `poll(2)` loop over `progress_notification_fd()`; wake handling is exactly `acknowledge_progress_notification()` → bounded `poll_progress()` passes until quiescent; covers multi-request settling (reads + write + zero-ops), a zero-completion control wake absorbed harmlessly, coalesced deliveries through one readable event (one wake → 5 completions), a saturated notification fd keeping wake delivery (`saturated_notification_preserves_wake`), and the R14 ack/signal race. Quiescence is proven by level-triggered silence: after the final ack, `poll(fd, 0)` is empty and a bounded `poll(fd, 150ms)` times out. No Scheduler, Fiber, condition variable, periodic timer, or busy loop appears in the host path |
| Token exhaustion | Policy (rewritten after the second review round): each domain's epoch saturates at `UINT64_MAX` and never leaves `MAX`; the sticky exhausted state is the domain's exhaustion sequence (`progress_exhaustion_`, `control_exhaustion_`) turning nonzero, and every later producer transition in the spent domain bumps that sequence under the source mutex before the eventfd write. The first design — a saturated `signal()` that only set a sticky bool and relied on the fd write — was a real lost-wake: between the empty pass and the L4 drain a fresh saturated signal changed no observed token value, so the revalidation classified it stale, the drain consumed its notification, and a parked owner slept past actionable work. `exhausted()` now derives from `progress_exhaustion_ != 0` (the standalone bool is gone), and the context still closes core admission at its next pass (`close_admission_on_progress_exhaustion_` inside `poll_progress`), after which normal submission returns `invalid_state`. Wrap bounds: the progress-exhausted signal population is bounded by the requests accepted before admission closes (≤ request capacity), so `progress_exhaustion_` cannot wrap; the control-exhausted interrupt population is application-sourced, and each interrupt terminates a parked wait as interrupted, so a token alias would require 2⁶⁴ interrupts inside one single-use token's snapshot→revalidation window. Evidence: `token_exhaustion_saturates_without_alias_and_closes_admission` (epoch seam at MAX; token stays MAX; exhaustion observable; admission closes; MAX→0 silent reuse impossible), `owner_parked_at_saturated_epoch_wakes_and_reparks` (owner not left asleep), `r15_saturated_signal_between_pass_and_revalidation_is_not_drained`, `control_exhaustion_saturates_without_alias_and_wakes_interrupted`, M-B5/M-B7/M-B8 kills. The exhaustion-sequence mechanism now owns both domains' runtime boundary; C2-E retains the formal model obligation (V24/V25), not the mechanism |
| Mutation/discrimination | Temporary-patch builds, each reverted (no permanent framework): M-B1 remove final token revalidation → `threadpool_progress_race_test` FAILS at `r2_signal_between_token_and_ack_is_revalidation_caught` (exit 1). M-B2 drain moved to a separate lock hold after the park decision → FAILS at `r11b_stale_readiness_drained_at_park_without_fabrication` (the r3/r5/r8 signal-during-pause class is lost by the same reorder). M-B3 `signal()` keeps the epoch bump but drops the eventfd write → suite aborts deterministically at r1: the owner is never woken to service the zero-op obligation and context destruction trips the non-quiescent fail-fast (every fd-wake test r3/r5/r8/r9/r10 and the external loop are blocked by the same defect). M-B4 zero-op obligation signal omitted → same deterministic abort at r1; the external suite blocks at its zero-op phases; r13's stranded-obligation scenario is the designed discriminator. M-B5 wrap allowed → `token_exhaustion_saturates_without_alias_and_closes_admission` aborts: admission stays open, the post-exhaustion submit succeeds and its outstanding binding is torn down (completion-binding destruction fail-fast); the token==MAX / exhausted / invalid_state assertions would each independently fail. M-B6 `wait_one` clamps a wake to one completion → FAILS at `r10_multiple_signals_coalesce_into_one_readiness`; the external host loop is structurally immune because it sums `pass.completed` across passes instead of assuming one completion per wake. M-B7 (round 2) a saturated `signal()` skips the exhaustion-sequence bump (the reviewed pre-fix behavior) → FAILS at `r15_saturated_signal_between_pass_and_revalidation_is_not_drained` (only failure; the owner drains the fresh notification, enters the park — `prepark==1` — and only recovers through the deadline fallback). M-B8 (round 2) `interrupt()` wraps the control epoch plainly (the reviewed pre-fix behavior) → FAILS at `control_exhaustion_saturates_without_alias_and_wakes_interrupted` (only failure; the token shows `control==0, control_exhaustion==0` instead of sticky `MAX` with `control_exhaustion==1`) |
| Lock-order audit | No new nested edge was introduced. Existing edges unchanged and direction-consistent: `access_mtx_` → backend `poll_progress` → `work_mtx_`; `access_mtx_` → core mutex (occupancy, admission close, lookups); `access_mtx_` → `ProgressSource::mtx_` (port signal on submit/publish paths); worker: `work_mtx_` released → port signal (leaf); ProgressSource mutex remains a leaf (acquires nothing). Producers signal without `access_mtx_`, so a ThreadPool worker may signal while the owner is parked. The final conditional park holds no `access_mtx_` and nothing across the `poll(2)` syscall (the drain completes before the mutex release at L1/L4). Reverse-edge search found no `core → access`, `work_mtx_ → access`, or `progress → anything` path. TSan targeted on the full new race campaign reports nothing |
| Memory visibility | Every cross-thread fact is protected by a mutex or the existing release/acquire edge, never by eventfd folklore: progress/control epochs and exhaustion sequences — written under `ProgressSource::mtx_` by signal/interrupt, read under the same mutex (the eventfd write is a wake, never a data channel); `publication_pending_` — worker under `work_mtx_`, drained by `poll_progress` under `work_mtx_` (the port signal only causes the owner to run a pass); `event_owed`/`owed_key` — zero-op publication under `access_mtx_`, scanned under `access_mtx_`; core slot fields (terminal/published/refs/observer phase) — under the core mutex on both sides; Completion payload — the existing core serialization plus the `Completion` ready release/acquire pair (#394/#395 evidence); `dispatch_` ring — `work_mtx_` plus `work_cv_` (worker-internal). No fence was added; none was needed |
| Mechanism justification (C2-B additions) | `ProgressPass{completed, immediate_work_remains, accepted_work_remains}` — the authoritative post-pass actionable state; not derivable (backend-internal queues are invisible to a bare count); per-call value, no persistent state; replaces guessing from `poll()`'s count; never removed while PROG-02 clause 3 stands. Exhaustion sequences (`progress_exhaustion_`, `control_exhaustion_`, round 2) — the freshness authority of a spent epoch domain: once an epoch saturates, no epoch value can distinguish a fresh producer transition from the observed token, so the revalidation-drain proof collapses exactly as the second review demonstrated; the sequences restore the bump-before-write ordering edge; physical/lifetime obligation class, not derivable from the frozen epochs; replace the round-1 sticky `exhausted_` bool (whose fact is now derived: `progress_exhaustion_ != 0`) — removal condition: never while a saturating epoch exists. Test-only seams (pause gates, epoch setters, saturation write, exhaustion query) — `SLUICE_ASYNC_INTERNAL_TESTING` only, removable with the campaign that uses them. Deliberately NOT added: pending-wake counts, parked counts, wake counters, completion counters, a second notification fd, a ThreadPool readiness queue — C2-A deleted these classes of state and no race in the matrix required them |
| Build/test evidence | Linux WSL2 kernel 6.18.33.2, gcc 15.2.0 release/debug, clang 21.1.8 asanubsan/tsan. Release+uring full group green via the CI-shaped runner (`xmake test`): 53/53 including the race suite 18/18 and the external loop 5/5 at 0.308 s; progress ownership 12/12 (+uring 12/12), threadpool cutover 16/16, uring cutover 21/21, request core protocol 45 / publication 11 / ownership 8 (+uring 9), public_request 13/13, uring smoke 8/8 (real ring), app consumption suites green. Release liburing-disabled targeted: race/external/progress-ownership/threadpool cutover/public_request green (round-1 matrix re-run on the shared-source change). Debug+uring targeted: race 18/18 / external 5/5 / cutover 16/16. clang ASan+UBSan targeted: race 18/18, external 5/5, progress ownership 12/12 — all pass. clang TSan targeted: race 18/18, external 5/5 — no reports. 5 repeated release runs of both new suites green. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean. Continuous sanitizer CI remains absent — recorded as a hardening follow-up, not a C2-B semantic mechanism (per the C2-A record's standing limit) |
| Authority-contraction audit | No production authority moved in C2-B: the C2-A ownership boundary is frozen and preserved (context-owned source, narrow port, no backend wait authority). Newly eligible for removal: none — the C2-B slice adds mechanism, it retires none. Retained-with-stage items from C2-A are unchanged: ThreadPool `work_cv_` (worker-internal, never a W-03 contract — re-verified: no condition variable participates in any host-facing wait path), io_uring ring fd + lend (C2-C's swap candidate), admission-close-as-progress-signal (outcome taxonomy C2-D). `AsyncBackend::signals_physical_progress()` kept: still the capability gate admitting backends into the wait protocol |
| Status | C2_B_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW — handshake implemented and deterministically evidenced for the ThreadPool path. Review-fix round (first independent five-axis review): the R14 external-loop test now services any remaining delayed zero-op obligation on every exit path before resetting its completion — the drain-wins interleaving (signaler's write landing before the host's acknowledgement) previously could observe the completion without a servicing pass and strand the obligation into the destruction fail-fast; two requirement citations were removed from C++ comments and a brace re-indent reverted. Second review round (independent code review, verdict REQUEST_CHANGES): P1 — the round-1 saturated-domain design had a real lost-wake (a fresh `signal()` between the empty pass and the L4 drain changed no observed token value, so the revalidation classified it stale, drained its notification, and a parked owner slept past actionable work); fixed by the exhaustion-sequence mechanism and R15 with mutation M-B7. P2 — `interrupt()` still wrapped the control epoch silently; the "control-epoch bound lives in C2-E" staging was rejected (a formal stage cannot invent runtime mechanism), so both domains now share the saturate-and-sequence boundary with mutation M-B8. P3 — `immediate_work_remains` still lacks an owner-decision-difference oracle proving it is not implied by the other pass fields; accepted as a follow-up for the C2-C/C2-D slices, no mechanism change this round. Exact-head CI redness (run #570, both configs) was diagnosed as a test-runner timeout unit error — xmake's `run_timeout` is milliseconds, so the configured 180 killed the external-loop suite at 0.184 s (its quiescence poll alone budgets 150 ms); corrected to 120000 and the CI-shaped runner is green. Explicitly not claimed: io_uring CQ/eventfd convergence and ring saturation (C2-C), owner-exclusivity runtime, sticky control acknowledgement, deadline/health outcome representation, external-host stop/settle/detach and fd-reuse-after-detach (C2-D), ProgressSource.tla and V10/V11/V12/V23(progress)/V24/V25 formal closure (C2-E) |

## C2-B final corrective pass — Issue #397, committed control freshness + terminal outer exhaustion

Third independent review of PR #438 accepted the C2-B mechanism and returned
exactly two exhaustion-edge defects, fixed in this narrow pass. No accepted
C2-B mechanism was reopened; RequestCore, Observer, Scheduler, Fiber, the
backend ownership boundary, io_uring convergence (C2-C), owner/outcomes
(C2-D) and the TLA+ obligation (C2-E) are untouched.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | #397 stage C2-B exhaustion edge only, under PROG-02 (no alias for an active wait; exhaustion resolves before aliasing) and PROG-04 (control pending is sticky for the waiter). Explicitly excluded: everything listed in the C2-B record above |
| Previous P1 — committed wait froze only the control epoch | `arm_committed_wait()` froze `armed_control_epoch_` but not the exhaustion half, and `consume_committed_wait()` returned the consume-time `control_exhaustion_` next to the arm-time epoch; `wait_one` restored only `observed.control`. In the saturated first-stage domain (`control_epoch_ == MAX`) an interrupt landing after the arm moved only `control_exhaustion_`, so the consumed token mixed the arm-time epoch with the consume-time exhaustion, revalidated the fresh interrupt as unchanged, drained its readiness, and parked the owner past it |
| P1 fix | `arm_committed_wait()` freezes the complete control freshness pair (`armed_control_epoch_`, `armed_control_exhaustion_`); `consume_committed_wait()` returns that arm-time pair; `AsyncIoContext::wait_one()` restores both `observed.control` and `observed.control_exhaustion` from the committed baseline at both wait constructions (progress coordinates stay live per-call snapshots). No mixed-time token is constructible: both control coordinates in any observed token come from the same mutex-held arm |
| R16 | Superseded by the C2-D corrective round: the armed-commit mechanism was deleted when control freshness moved to the current/observed/acknowledged generation pair (see the C2-D record's authority-contraction audit); the row below is the historical C2-B/C evidence. `r16_saturated_control_interrupt_between_arm_and_consume_is_observed` (threadpool_progress_race_test, 20/20): forces `control_epoch_ = MAX, control_exhaustion_ = 7` via seams, performs the committed arm on the test thread (the scheduler's `arm_progress_wait_commit` edge), runs a joined producer thread through `interrupt_progress_waiters()`, verifies the saturated freshness moved (`MAX, 8`) with notification readiness present, then runs the driver's bounded `wait_one`. Assertions: interrupted outcome (0), `prepark == 0` (the owner never reached the kernel park), readiness still present after the wait (the fresh interrupt was recognized as control, not drained as stale), no fabricated completion; then release + a servicing pass completes the real read. The 8 s bound is a watchdog; the state assertions, not any timeout, are the oracle |
| M-B9 | Temporary patch restoring the old defect (`consume_committed_wait` returns live `control_exhaustion_`), rebuilt, suite run, reverted. Killed: exit 1, `FAIL: r16_saturated_control_interrupt_between_arm_and_consume_is_observed`, and the run consumed the full 8 s bounded park — the owner drained the fresh interrupt, reached `prepark`, and slept to the deadline, which is exactly the lost-interrupt semantics rather than an unrelated assertion failure |
| Previous P2 — outer control exhaustion could itself wrap | `interrupt()` bumped `control_exhaustion_` without a bound, so the alias horizon moved from the epoch to the exhaustion sequence; a wrapped `{MAX, 0}` would eventually recreate an earlier active token value |
| P2 fix | The control pair terminates in a non-reusable absorbing state: `interrupt()` advances the epoch only while below `MAX`, then the exhaustion sequence only while below `MAX`, and once both are saturated the pair freezes at `{MAX, MAX}` for good. `control_changed_nolock_()` treats a spent-domain observed or live pair as permanently pending control, so no wait can revalidate as unchanged and enter an ordinary park while the domain is spent; every later interrupt still writes the notification fd, so persistent readiness remains observable. No reset path exists in production code (the only writers are the seams under `SLUICE_ASYNC_INTERNAL_TESTING`); the terminal is monotonic and derived from the existing authoritative pair — no new runtime state field was added |
| Outer exhaustion test | `control_outer_exhaustion_is_terminal_and_never_aliases`: seams place the pair at the last pre-terminal value (`MAX, MAX-1`); one interrupt enters the terminal pair by the final representable increment (no wrap); a committed arm holds the terminal token; three further interrupt rounds verify the pair stays frozen, readiness persists, and each bounded `wait_one` returns interrupted with `prepark == 0` (an ordinary unchanged-token park is unreachable). Release then exercises the no-park owner shape: the driver re-enters `wait_one` until a pass publishes the completion, which is the stage-appropriate PROG-01 owner-loop obligation in a state where parking is permanently refused |
| M-B10 | Temporary patch replacing the terminal-aware advance with plain `++control_exhaustion_`, rebuilt, suite run, reverted. Killed: exit 1, `FAIL: control_outer_exhaustion_is_terminal_and_never_aliases` — the mutant wrapped the spent pair back to `{MAX, 0}`, failing the terminal-pair assertion deterministically |
| Progress/control asymmetry | Progress exhaustion keeps the accepted structural argument untouched: saturation closes admission, so the post-saturation progress signal population is bounded by accepted request capacity and retained obligations, and `progress_exhaustion_` cannot wrap under that bound. Control interrupts are externally callable (`application_runtime`, scheduler wake path, hosts) with no request-capacity bound, so control alone needs the terminal non-aliasing state at C2-B. Formal closure for both domains remains C2-E (V24/V25); this pass claims the runtime mechanism only |
| Test-harness corrections found by this pass | (1) In a terminal-control (no-park) phase a single `wait_one` cannot absorb backend publication lag, so the new outer-exhaustion test's final phase re-enters `wait_one` as the driver loop would; a single-shot shape raced the worker's post-gate publication window (~1–2 % under WSL2 scheduling) and hit the fixture's unpublished-Completion destruction fail-fast. (2) Pre-existing r15 latent race, unchanged from the base commit: `read.release()` orders only the worker's gate exit, not its subsequent offer/push/signal tail, so r15's single readiness poll could lose that race and take an early-return path that destroyed the unpublished completion; the check now waits bounded while the driver stays paused. Both are test-infrastructure corrections; neither changes any protocol claim. After both fixes: 500 consecutive release suite runs green |
| Locking/visibility re-audit | Discipline unchanged: every freshness field (`progress_epoch_`, `progress_exhaustion_`, `control_epoch_`, `control_exhaustion_`, `armed_control_epoch_`, `armed_control_exhaustion_`, `armed_`) is written and read only under `ProgressSource::mtx_`; producers mutate state under the mutex and write the eventfd only after the mutex release (state transition established before notification; the fd is a wake, never the memory-order proof); the L4 revalidation and the stale-readiness drain share one mutex hold; `wait_one`'s committed baseline is a local copy consumed under the same mutex. No new lock, no nesting change, no inversion |
| Wait-path re-audit (A–F) | A: unsaturated epoch change before L4 — numeric pair difference at the L4 revalidation (or at the post-poll revalidation if it lands during L5), interrupted before any drain. B: saturated first-stage interrupt before L4 — exhaustion-half difference against the complete committed baseline (P1 fix), interrupted before the drain; R16 is the deterministic proof. C: saturated interrupt after L4's check — the interrupter's mutex-ordered mutation either precedes the owner's check (case B) or follows it, in which case the eventfd write postdates the fused drain and the persistent readiness wakes L5's `poll(2)`; the post-poll revalidation then sees the exhaustion difference. D: outer exhaustion before L4 — a live spent pair differs numerically from any earlier baseline, and an observed spent pair is refused by the terminal predicate, so no park can be licensed. E: outer exhaustion after L4 — an owner parked with a pre-terminal baseline is woken by the interrupt's eventfd write and revalidates as interrupted; an owner with a terminal observed pair can never have passed L4, and at terminal every wait returns interrupted so no unchanged-token park exists. F: arm-then-interrupt — the arm freezes both coordinates under the mutex, so any post-arm interrupt changes the live pair away from the frozen baseline, and consume returns exactly the frozen pair (never a mixed-time token) |
| Build/test evidence | Linux WSL2 kernel 6.18.40.1, gcc 15.2.0, clang 21.1.8, liburing present. CI-shaped: release 45/45 (`xmake test`), debug 45/45. Release liburing-disabled targeted: race 20/20, external loop 5/5, threadpool cutover 16/16, progress ownership 12/12, public_request 13/13. clang ASan+UBSan targeted: race 20/20, external loop 5/5, progress ownership 12/12 (no reports). clang TSan targeted: race 20/20 ×3, external loop 5/5 (no reports). Release suite repeated 500× green after the harness corrections. M-B9 and M-B10 each killed their mutant deterministically and were reverted. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean; `run_timeout = 120000` unchanged and no new test depends on the runner timeout (every assertion is a state assertion) |
| Authority-contraction audit | No production authority moved: the C2-B handshake and C2-A ownership boundary are preserved. The round-2 exhaustion sequences remain the freshness authority; this pass adds no runtime state (the terminal is a derived predicate on the existing pair, materialized as `control_domain_spent_`/`control_changed_nolock_`) and retires nothing. New test-only seam: `set_control_exhaustion_for_test` (removable with the campaign that uses it). The r15 harness wait is test-infrastructure, not runtime mechanism |
| Status | C2_B_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW — the two exhaustion-edge defects of the third review are fixed with deterministic evidence (R16/M-B9, outer-exhaustion test/M-B10); awaiting the independent review of exactly these two paths. Explicitly not claimed: C2-C io_uring convergence, C2-D owner/outcomes/lifetime, C2-E TLA+ formal proof of exhaustion non-aliasing |

## C2-C review record — Issue #397, io_uring readiness convergence on the context ProgressSource

Stage-local status: `C2_C_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW`. This record claims the
C2-C exit gate only: the io_uring backend's kernel CQ completions converge on
the context-owned ProgressSource through a kernel-safe one-fd park protocol;
the poll-only ring-fd readiness lend is deleted; registration is a narrow
one-shot seam; the external host's W-03 loop is exercised against the real
ring; and the K-campaign reproduces every interval a kernel producer can
signal relative to the park. It closes no owner/outcome/lifetime policy
(C2-D) and no formal obligation (C2-E).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | #397 stage C2-C only, under PROG-01/02/03 (dispatch-as-progress, no-lost-wake wait conditions, pollable notification operation), ARCH-01 (core → progress obligation edge stays mediated through the backend port), THREAD-01/02 constraints, W-03 io_uring half, the #394 L5 progress half mapped to io_uring, VERIFY-03 mechanism evidence. Explicitly excluded: owner-exclusivity enforcement, sticky control outcome, deadline/interrupt/health outcome taxonomy, external-host stop/settle/detach lifetime (C2-D); TLA+ model and V10/V11/V12/V23(progress)/V24/V25 closure (C2-E). RequestCore, Observer, Request<T>, Scheduler and Fiber semantics untouched. Accepted frozen facts honored: the backend owns physical execution only (no `BackendWaitSource`, no backend `wait_one`, no backend-owned host eventfd, no periodic polling; the ThreadPool worker CV stays internal) |
| Implementation | Base `79819ab6` (live master at start; the C2-B merge commit; no intervening commits). Implementation commits `02ee39d9` (production convergence), `468843ac` (K-campaign), `aa8ce87e` (external loop + registration lifecycle), `96b63bef` (drive-pass delivery fix, k14 H-interval, recording unregister hook) on branch `feat/397-c2-c-uring-progress-convergence`. Production surface: `BackendProgressPort::register_notification_with_kernel(install, context)` — a one-shot, borrowed, registration-only seam; `UringAsyncBackend::progress_port_attached()` installs the context notification fd via `io_uring_register_eventfd` (the full variant, not `_async`) through a test-overridable hook; `AsyncBackend::has_immediate_physical_work()` (private virtual, default false) — the nonblocking kernel CQ probe; `ProgressSource::wait_if_unchanged` gains `PhysicalProbe`/`probe_context` parameters and drops its second pollfd; `UringAsyncBackend::poll_progress()` reports the honest pass (`completed`, `immediate_work_remains` from `publication_pending_` observed under `dispatch_mtx_`, `accepted_work_remains` from outstanding); `finalize_operation_terminal_` signals at the `publication_pending_` pending transition; publication in `poll()` is silent; the destructor unregisters before `io_uring_queue_exit`. Test-only seams: `RegisterEventfdFn`/`UnregisterEventfdFn` submit-hook typedefs, registration/unregistration counters, the pre-existing pause gates and prepark counter |
| D1 — registration seam shape | The fd crosses exactly one call frame: `install(context, fd)` receives the ProgressSource's notification fd and returns a verdict; the seam neither stores, reads, dups, closes, nor returns the fd, and no `notification_fd()` accessor is exposed through the port (the port's no-wait-API concept already asserts this). Failure verdicts are propagated: `progress_port_attached()` returns false and `attach_progress_port()` fails context construction with an explicit exception — no silent fallback to the ring fd and no hidden non-uring degradation path. Member order (`core_`, `progress_`, `backend_`) guarantees the backend dies before the ProgressSource during construction unwind |
| D2 — ring-fd lend deletion | `ProgressSource::bind_physical_readiness` and `bound_readiness_fd_` are deleted; uring was their sole user. Structural negative evidence: `progress_source_has_no_readiness_lend` and `port_has_no_readiness_lend` concepts static_assert the absence of the binding operation on both the source and the backend port. Repo-wide grep: `bind_physical_readiness` occurs only inside the two negative concept expressions; `bound_readiness_fd_` occurs nowhere; no ring-fd accessor exists anywhere in `include/`/`src/` |
| D3 — minimum state | `eventfd_registered_` (bool) is the only new backend runtime field; it is written at registration, read at unregister, and never inspected for I/O behavior decisions. No generation/lease/refcount was added: the registration is one-shot for the ring's lifetime and the kernel frees registration state at `io_uring_queue_exit`; the explicit unregister exists so teardown is reviewable and observable (recording-hook evidence), not because the kernel requires it |
| Protocol before | The uring park lent the ring fd into the wait: `poll(2)` raced both the context eventfd and the ring fd; ring-fd readability indicated CQEs, which the owner's next pass reaped. CQ convergence existed only through this lend: the kernel CQ producer bypassed the userspace epoch protocol entirely (no epoch bump accompanies a CQE), and the external host had no real-ring W-03 story — the C2-B record explicitly deferred kernel-eventfd registration to C2-C because registration without an ack-before-recheck park discipline could strand a CQE whose notification the stale drain consumed |
| Protocol after | Per park iteration: `observed = snapshot()` (L2) → `pass = poll_progress()` (L3) → `completed > 0` returns; `!immediate && !accepted` returns 0 → `wait_if_unchanged(observed, remaining, probe, this)`: [prerevalidate gate] → under one `mtx_` hold: control/progress revalidation, exhaustion checks, stale-notification drain (L1/L4a) → unlock → **P2 physical probe**: `probe()` = `io_uring_cq_ready() > 0 || io_uring_cq_has_overflow()` with the mutex released (mutex-holding sections never span the probe; §24) → under a second `mtx_` hold: **P3 final non-draining revalidation** (L4b) → [prepark gate, prepark counter] → `poll(2)` on the single context notification fd (L5). The probe is a pure kernel CQ fact: userspace producers (token-covered) answer the default false; only the uring backend overrides it. A `WakeReason::progress` from the probe or either revalidation re-enters the loop top; P3 never drains — readiness observed after the probe is what wakes L5 |
| Kernel facts the protocol relies on | (1) `io_uring_register_eventfd` (full variant) writes the registered eventfd synchronously on CQ completion publication, including CQEs parked on the kernel overflow list; (2) `io_uring_peek_batch_cqe` observes only the shared-memory CQ — overflow-listed CQEs are invisible until an `enter` flushes them, so `reap_cqes` flushes via `io_uring_get_events` when `io_uring_cq_has_overflow` and re-peeks; (3) the CQ ring defaults to twice the SQ entries, so a batch beyond the ring is an overflow case the probe must report and reap must be able to service; (4) the kernel frees registration state at ctx exit, so the explicit unregister is a reviewable-lifecycle obligation (§10), not a kernel requirement. Verified against the kernel 6.12 io_uring source and liburing 2.14 headers; runtime: WSL2 kernel 6.18, liburing 2.14 |
| SAFE_TO_PARK (kernel variant) | Parking happens only when all of: (1) a bounded nonblocking pass returned with `completed == 0` and `!immediate_work_remains` (uring: `publication_pending_` observed empty under `dispatch_mtx_`); (2) `accepted_work_remains` true (else wait returns 0); (3) L4a revalidated both domains and drained stale readiness under one mutex hold; (4) the P2 probe observed an empty CQ (no CQ-ready, no overflow) with the mutex released — the fact the kernel producer mutates but the epoch cannot express; (5) P3 revalidated both domains non-drainingly, after the probe, so anything the probe missed as a race is caught as an epoch change; (6) every userspace producer bumps its domain epoch under the source mutex before writing the eventfd, so a post-P3 userspace signal either flips a revalidated value or leaves readiness that wakes L5; (7) kernel completions signal through the kernel's own eventfd write at CQE publication — a post-P3 CQE leaves readiness that wakes L5, and a pre-drain CQE is caught by (4) |
| No-lost-wake intervals (§27, A–H) | *Historical C2-C stage evidence; superseded for the current implementation by the C2 A-D final record: interval H's covering transition signal was deleted (out-of-matrix schedule), so H is no longer a live mechanism at HEAD; the in-contract A–G claim is unaffected.* **A** — CQE/userspace signal before the iteration's snapshot: the loop-top pass discovers it; no park (U1's report is this interval's evidence). **B** — after the snapshot, before L4a: userspace signals are caught by the L4a epoch revalidation; a kernel CQE leaves either fd readiness (drained → see C) or CQ state the probe reports. **C** — during the L4a drain, after the epoch checks: the drain can consume a kernel notification whose CQE is unreaped — the P2 probe observes the CQ state and returns progress (`k1_k2`; M-C1 kills). **D** — after L4a, before P2: probe true (`k1_k2` second variant, `prepark==0`). **E** — after P2, before P3: a userspace bump is caught at P3; a kernel CQE leaves fd readiness that wakes L5 and the post-wake drain+revalidation returns progress (`k3`, `k10a`, `k10b`). **F** — between P3 and the poll entry: readiness persists into L5 (`k5_k6`). **G** — while blocked in poll: the kernel or a userspace write wakes L5 (`k7`; M-C3 kills; `k9` proves zero-CQE notifications are harmless). **H** — beyond the single-owner execution matrix: a second thread running `poll_progress` reaps the CQE before the parked owner's probe, consuming both the notification and the CQ state; the reaper's publication_pending_ transition signal bumps the epoch before publication, so the owner's L4a/P3 revalidation returns progress (`k14`; M-C6 kills). THREAD-01 fixes poll and driver wait at one progress owner, so this interval is coverage beyond the contract's execution matrix, retained because the signal is the relocated pre-existing post-publish mechanism; the in-contract no-lost-wake claim is carried by A–G. Saturation and coalescing are tolerated at every interval: readiness persists level-triggered, and `k8`/`k11a`/`k11b`/`k13` exercise 1→N, saturated-then-CQE, drained-saturation-then-CQE, and overflow respectively |
| Reap-side pending-transition signal | *Historical C2-C stage evidence; superseded for the current implementation by the C2 A-D final record: this signal was deleted in that pass's C1 contraction (the reaper is never a peer under the fixed single owner), so the "signal is kept" verdict below no longer describes HEAD.* The C2-B discipline (signal when work becomes pending; publication is silent) has an uring-specific gap: the pending transition for a kernel completion is observed by whichever pass reaps it, and the kernel's eventfd write is consumed by the observing waiter's own stale drain. A second driver that drains the fd and probes after the peer's reap but before the peer's publish sees no epoch change and no CQ state — an execution the root's THREAD-01 matrix does not license (poll and driver wait are fixed-progress-owner operations), so this is not a contract obligation; the signal is kept because it relocates the pre-existing post-publish signal rather than adding a mechanism, and the in-contract no-lost-wake proof rests on intervals A–G. `finalize_operation_terminal_` therefore signals at the push to `publication_pending_` (same transition point and granularity as the ThreadPool worker push), and `poll()`'s post-publish signal (a pre-existing master mechanism) is removed: publication itself creates no new obligation. Consequences for external hosts: a servicing pass that is the first observer of a kernel completion re-asserts readiness exactly once, so one settling `acknowledge`+pass round returns the fd to quiet (asserted by the external-loop suite); and the submitting pass can be the servicing pass when the kernel finishes inside its submission window, so a PROG-01 host must accumulate deliveries across its passes (the external-loop and registration tests do) |
| Probe soundness | *Superseded in part by the C2 A-D final record: the interval-H transition-signal closure it cites was deleted (out-of-matrix schedule; the flush-failure probe-true now terminates through the composed health verdict).* The probe reports CQ facts, not reapability: it counts overflow via `io_uring_cq_has_overflow` so a probe-true is always serviceable — `reap_cqes` flushes the overflow list with `io_uring_get_events` before re-peeking (a peek-only reap would spin on an unflushable probe-true). A false-negative probe (a concurrent reaper emptied the CQ between the drain and the probe) is closed by interval H's transition signal, and a probe-true that races the owner's own pass is closed by the L3 pass re-deriving completed work. The probe never fabricates completions: `k9` proves a wake with zero CQEs re-parks safely |
| Signal-source audit (uring) | *Superseded in part by the C2 A-D final record: the reap-side `publication_pending_` push signal at `finalize_operation_terminal_` was deleted; every other site listed below is retained.* Normal kernel completion → kernel eventfd write at CQE publication (covers every waiter) + reap-side `publication_pending_` push signal at `finalize_operation_terminal_` (covers peers that missed the kernel write to a concurrent reaper): CREATES_ACTIONABLE_DRIVER_WORK, announced by both the kernel and the transition. Zero-op inline publication → `publish_zero_op_inline` + signal (event_owed): CREATES_ACTIONABLE + CREATES_RECLAIM_OBLIGATION. cancel `won_before_execution` → push + signal: covered. submit/cancel transport failure → `poison_and_recover_locked` retires kernel-invisible work, pushes, then signals (its own signal at the recovery walk's end; the dispatch wrapper's `injected_dispatch_failure || newly_poisoned` signal covers the test-injected dispatch-failure push): covered. Class-A recovery retirement → the poison path's signal: covered. admission close → `close_admission` signal: CREATES_CONTROL_OBLIGATION. observer delivery → rides the publication chain silently. owner-side publication loop in `poll()` → silent (the obligation was signalled at the pending transition; the removed post-publish signal was this audit's finding) |
| Poison/health wake audit (§17) | `poison_and_recover_locked` is the single `fatal_error_` writer; its callers are the transport result path (submit/cancel enter failures outside EINTR/EAGAIN/EBUSY) and it ends with a signal after the ledger retirement walk, so every poison transition announces itself. The parked owner converges: `poison_transition_wakes_parked_owner` deterministically creates a poison from a non-owner submit (two accepted-but-undispatched submissions fill the two-entry SQ ring; the fifth submit reaches the transport hook) and the parked owner wakes with the drained dispatch failures' terminals well inside its deadline (M-C5 kills: with both poison-path signal sites neutralized the owner strands to the deadline). Late CQEs from in-kernel reads are retired by bounded drain passes before teardown |
| Registration lifecycle evidence (§9/§21/§22) | `uring_registration_lifecycle_test` (4/4): a standalone backend registers nothing; context attachment registers exactly once and a real kernel completion reaches the context fd (the drive-pass delivery joins the wait's count); a forced install failure (`forced_register_failure` hook) fails context construction with the explicit exception after exactly one install attempt; teardown unregisters exactly once with a zero syscall result before ring exit, observed by a test-owned recording unregister hook that wraps the real `io_uring_unregister_eventfd` (the counters live in the backend, which is destroyed with the context — the hook is what survives teardown). The seam carries no capability beyond install/uninstall verdicts |
| External-loop evidence (§18) | `uring_external_loop_test` (4/4) against the real ring, poll set = exactly `ctx.progress_notification_fd()`, host operations = the documented ack/progress pair plus a post-submit drive pass whose delivery accumulates with the wake loop's (PROG-01): multi-request settling with a zero-completion control wake and level-triggered quiescence (idle fd blocks in `poll(2)`); kernel completions discovered through the wake loop with bounded wakes and no stranding; the userspace zero-op publication wake; a saturated fd with a real completion. After every servicing sequence a settling round returns the fd to quiet — the uring/ThreadPool external-loop contract is the same level-triggered shape |
| Mutation evidence (§13/§26) | Temporary patches, each rebuilt, run, reverted. **M-C1** (remove final post-drain CQ probe): killed by `k1_k2_cqe_before_drain_recovered_by_final_probe` — the owner strands to the 2000 ms watchdog and the elapsed bound (< 1000 ms) fails. **M-C2** (restore the ring-fd lend): killed at compile time by the `progress_source_has_no_readiness_lend` static assertion. **M-C3** (omit registration): killed by `attachment_registers_kernel_notification_once` (zero registration attempts observed) and the K-campaign aborts on the first un-wakable park. **M-C4** (omit unregister): killed by the lifecycle suite's registration/unregistration pairing assertions. **M-C5** (omit poison signal): killed by `poison_transition_wakes_parked_owner` only when both poison-path signal sites are neutralized — the campaign itself surfaced the second site, and the mutated path was the only wake. **M-C6** (omit the reap-side pending-transition signal): killed by `k14_peer_pass_consuming_cqe_wakes_parked_owner` — the peer-reaped interval strands to the deadline *(historical kill only: the C2 A-D final record deleted the signal because the schedule is outside the final THREAD-01 matrix and C2-D rewrote k14 to the peer-rejection oracle, so M-C6 does not apply at current HEAD)*. No timeout is a success oracle: every kill's discriminator is a state or elapsed bound |
| Lock-order audit (§24) | The ProgressSource mutex remains a leaf: `signal()`/`interrupt()` acquire nothing else; `wait_if_unchanged` holds it only across short scoped sections. The physical probe is invoked with the mutex **released** — the holding sections never span the probe call, so no backend lock is ever acquired under the progress mutex. The uring backend's `dispatch_mtx_` → ProgressSource mutex edge is one-directional (signals under poison recovery, under `finalize_operation_terminal_`, and from `cancel_key`/`close_admission` all run while holding `dispatch_mtx_`); the reverse edge does not exist because the probe (the only backend call from the wait path) runs mutex-released and takes no backend lock (`io_uring_cq_ready` reads kernel-shared state lock-free). The core mutex is always acquired and released inside a single `dispatch_mtx_`-held region or a single publication step, never nested with the progress mutex. ThreadPool edges (`work_mtx_` → signal) are unchanged from C2-B. No new lock was introduced |
| Memory-order audit (§23) | *Superseded in part by the C2 A-D final record: the interval-H/parked-peer transition-signal edges it cites were deleted with the reap-side signal; the in-matrix A–G argument is unaffected.* All epoch/exhaustion state stays mutex-confined (C2-B discipline unchanged). `eventfd_registered_` is single-threaded lifecycle state (constructor-attached, destructor-consumed). The probe reads kernel-shared CQ head/tail and the overflow flag lock-free: the loads are aligned 32-bit reads of kernel-published ring state, and the probe is a hint whose soundness does not rest on its own ordering — a stale false is closed by interval H's mutex-ordered transition signal, a stale true is closed by the L3 pass. Completion visibility: kernel CQE publication → shared-memory CQ → reap under `dispatch_mtx_` → `offer_terminal` under the core mutex → owner publication → the Completion publish's release store; a parked peer's wake path goes through the transition signal's mutex-ordered epoch bump, so no waiter can return without the core's publication being visible. The eventfd write is a wake, never the memory-order proof |
| Authority-contraction audit | Redundant after this slice and removed: `bind_physical_readiness`/`bound_readiness_fd_` (the lend), and the post-publish signal in `poll()` (its no-lost-wake role moved to the pending transition, where it also covers the earlier sub-window). Apparently redundant but retained: the dispatch wrapper's `newly_poisoned` signal arm (apparently shadowed by the poison path's own signal) is load-bearing for the injected-dispatch-failure push, which does not enter poison recovery; the P3 non-draining revalidation (apparently shadowed by the probe) is the authoritative epoch check that closes userspace races the probe cannot see. New state census: `eventfd_registered_` (bool, physical teardown obligation, D3); the registration seam (compatibility surface between backend and source, D1); test counters and hooks (test-only evidence, removable with the campaign). No core, observer, or scheduler state changed; no new semantic fact was materialized outside the backend's own CQ authority |
| Build/test evidence | Linux WSL2 kernel 6.18.40.1, gcc 15.2.0, clang 21.1.8, liburing 2.14. Full group release+uring: 53/53 registered test binaries green (including the eight uring cutover mutants and six public-request mutants, each run failing exactly at its injected defect, mutant failure modes verified identical on the pristine base). Targeted C2-C set (race 14/14, external loop 4/4, registration lifecycle 4/4, threadpool race/external loop, ownership ×2, cutovers ×2, public request ×2, submit boundary): release, debug, clang ASan+UBSan (leak detection on), TSan — 12/12 per configuration, no sanitizer reports. `uring_backend_smoke_test` is excluded from the TSan set by the pre-existing gcc/`fiber_ctx.cpp` `disable_sanitizer_instrumentation` attribute blocker recorded since A2 (unchanged; the seam-built targets carry the TSan claim). No-liburing build: 43/43 registered binaries green — the honest stub; the uring progress targets are absent in that configuration, and no instantiability claim is made for it. The external-loop and registration suites looped 40× consecutive green under ASan after the drive-pass delivery fix (the pre-fix flake ~1/100 is recorded in the commit history: the submitting pass can be the servicing pass, so host assertions accumulate deliveries across passes — a test-harness correction, not a protocol change). The K-campaign race suite repeated 5× green at release before k14 and rerun green with k14. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean; no test depends on the runner timeout |
| Limits | The K-campaign proves the exercised interleavings on this kernel/liburing pair, not protocol completeness beyond them (the A–H argument is the structural claim; the campaign is its deterministic evidence). Interval H exercises an execution outside the THREAD-01 single-owner matrix (a concurrent `poll_progress`); it evidences the relocated signal's coverage, not a contract obligation; concurrent `wait_one` callers would share the same mechanism through the same signal. Kernel behavior claims (eventfd write on overflow publication, peek/overflow invisibility) were verified against the 6.12 source and exercised on 6.18; no claim is made for kernels that deviate from that source. The eventfd counter is consumed as a wake only; no semantic value is read from it. C2-D and C2-E remain open and unclaimed |
