## C2-C corrective round — post-accept transport retry obligation and overflow-flush health

Independent review of #439 at `99fb9070` returned REQUEST_CHANGES with one P1
(post-accept transport retry hole against PROG-01) and one P2 (the overflow
flush discarded `io_uring_get_events`'s result, leaving a probe-true hot loop
and an unacknowledged failure path). This round closes exactly those two
findings; the accepted convergence protocol, registration seam, and signal
relocation from the round above are unchanged and were not re-reviewed. Stage
status after this round: `C2_C_CORRECTIVE_ROUND_IMPLEMENTED`, awaiting the
reviewer's re-check of the two closures only.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| P1 mechanism — dispatch-time kernel entry | `dispatch_after_accept` now ends with `submit_transport_locked()` under `dispatch_mtx_`, so a newly accepted request reaches the kernel on the accepting thread's dispatch pass and PROG-01's "work needed to dispatch a newly accepted request is itself a progress obligation" is discharged at the dispatch, not deferred to a later owner pass. The SQ-full refill submit inside `dispatch_one_locked` already ran on accepting threads under the same lock, so no new concurrency pattern is introduced; all SQ-ring mutation stays serialized by `dispatch_mtx_`, and `io_uring_enter` from submitting threads is the kernel's documented multi-threaded use. Steady-state signal behavior is unchanged (a successful dispatch-time submit leaves the ledger empty and signals nothing), so the saturation tests (`k11a`/`k11b`) and external-loop quiescence evidence keep their preconditions |
| P1 mechanism — retryable-failure obligation | When the submit attempt still leaves accepted transport outside the kernel (EINTR/EAGAIN/EBUSY retained entries, or entries stuck in `dispatch_` behind a full SQ), the pass advertises it and the owner schedules a further bounded pass. (1) `dispatch_retry_remains` (new `ProgressPass` field, default false; ThreadPool passes are unaffected) is computed under `dispatch_mtx_` as `!dispatch_->empty() || (ledger non-empty && !all_class_a_recovery_retired())` — a derived summary of the transport ledger and dispatch queue, which remain the authoritative facts; poisoned (retired) ledger populations deliberately do not count. (2) `wait_one` refuses the idle-return while it is set and caps the park handshake at `kTransportRetryInterval` (10 ms) so the next pass retries the transport. The review's five questions: the obligation is created by the backend at accept/submit and owned by the progress owner through `wait_one`/`poll_progress`; the persistent state is the existing ledger/dispatch physical fact (no new runtime state); the next retry is the next pass, at most one interval away under persistent failure; the wake without a CQE is the interval expiry (real CQEs wake through the registered kernel eventfd, control through the control epoch); persistent EAGAIN/EBUSY retries at ≤ 100 Hz instead of spinning (W-03), bounded by the caller's own deadline, with a wedged-kernel wait remaining interruptible — no health escalation is invented for transient pressure, because poison would fire on legal interleavings. PROG-02(3) ("or a further pass is explicitly scheduled") is the license for the nap; the classification slack (1 ms) only biases clock-granularity wake classification toward the expired deadline and can add at most one extra pass |
| P1 mechanism — retained-transport signal | If the dispatch-time submit fails retryably (or a cancel-control SQE stays prepared), `dispatch_after_accept` signals the ProgressSource so a concurrently parked owner re-polls instead of sleeping past kernel-invisible accepted work — closing the peer-accept variant of the same PROG-01 hole (previously an accept that raced a parked owner produced no wake and no submission until an unrelated completion). The signal fires only when transport is actually retained, so accept-rate eventfd noise is not added in the success path |
| P2 mechanism — overflow-flush health | `reap_cqes` captures the flush result through a new `GetEventsFn` test seam: `rc < 0` poisons the backend (`poison_and_recover_locked`, the single `fatal_error_` writer, ending in its own signal) and stores `overflow_flush_serviceable_ = false`; success stores true. `has_immediate_physical_work` now reports overflow as actionable only while that mirror holds, so the P2 probe's serviceability claim ("probe-true is always serviceable") survives a failing flush instead of hot-looping on overflow it cannot drain; the ring-visible disjunct is untouched, so post-poison reap-driven retirement keeps its convergence. The mirror is a relaxed atomic because the probe must stay lock-free (the accepted no-backend-lock-under-progress-mutex property); the authoritative state remains `fatal_error_` under `dispatch_mtx_`. Per PROG-03, a failed notification path becomes an observable health event with a documented retirement path rather than silent sleep; flush failures are not placed on the retryable transport list because an unserviceable overflow claim cannot be honestly maintained across a failing flush without a second retry obligation, and the health path is the retirement path PROG-03 names. Public `health_failure` outcome taxonomy stays C2-D |
| Mutation evidence | Temporary patches, each rebuilt, run, reverted; both mutant targets are registered in `xmake/tests.lua` as manual C2-C builds. **M-C7** (`SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_TRANSPORT_RETRY_UNADVERTISED`: report `dispatch_retry_remains == false` and skip the retained-transport signal): killed by `u2_k15_retryable_submit_failure_does_not_strand_owner` — the owner parks at the full handshake, never advertises the retained transport, and the wait strands to the deadline with value 0 against the elapsed and completion assertions; every earlier suite member stays green under the mutant. **M-C8** (`SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_OVERFLOW_FLUSH_IGNORED`: restore the discarded flush result and the ungated probe): killed by `overflow_flush_failure_becomes_observable_health_event` — no flush attempt is recorded and no poison occurs, so the health-probe submission is accepted (completion accepted and retired through the ordinary drain) and both the attempt and health assertions fail. No timeout is a success oracle: every kill's discriminator is a state or elapsed bound |
| Test evidence | `uring_progress_race_test` 17/17 (14 previous + `u2_k15_retryable_submit_failure_does_not_strand_owner`, `u2b_accepted_dispatch_wakes_parked_owner`, `overflow_flush_failure_becomes_observable_health_event`), repeated 20× green in debug+uring. U2/K15 arms an `-EAGAIN` submit hook before acceptance and requires the owner to resubmit (≥ 2 transport attempts observed) and complete well inside the 2000 ms watchdog; U2b freezes the owner at the prerevalidate gate and requires a peer acceptance to leave `sq_ready == 0` (the dispatch-time submission boundary) and both reads to complete; the overflow test posts three CQEs against the two-entry CQ ring before any reap, requires the failing flush to be attempted (`calls >= 1`), the poisoned backend to reject a probe submission, and every read to retire once the injected failure clears (the reap path stays live under poison; no double-terminal hazard arises because poison only retires ledger entries). Suite-wide: debug+uring 56/56, release+uring 56/56, no-liburing 45/45 registered tests; targeted ASan+UBSan and TSan runs of the race, external-loop, and cutover suites green with no reports; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean |
| Boundary-change ledger notes | The submit boundary moved from "owner pass submits prepared transport" to "the dispatching thread submits its own dispatch batch", so three pieces of pinned evidence were reshaped with the mechanism: `uring_core_cutover_test`'s ownership check now observes the dispatch-time submit (ledger consumed, routing alive), its two poison narratives label the failure as dispatch-time, and the race suite's `poison_transition_wakes_parked_owner` creates the poison from a third acceptance's dispatch-time transport failure (single-entry Class-A retirement; the multi-entry walk remains evidenced by the cutover cancel test, where the prepared cancel-control SQE and request B are retired by one poison). Mechanism justification: `dispatch_retry_remains` = derived summary (authority: transport ledger + dispatch queue), `overflow_flush_serviceable_` = derived summary (authority: flush attempt outcome, materialized for the lock-free probe), `kTransportRetryInterval`/`kDeadlineClassifySlack` = physical obligations of the scheduled-further-pass rule, retained-transport signal = existing mechanism applied at a new transition; none replaces an existing mechanism. Authority-contraction audit: no authority moved and nothing became eligible for removal; the only new test-only surface is the `GetEventsFn` seam, removable with the campaign |
| Limits | The retry cadence is wall-clock; a persistently failing transport keeps the owner looping at ≤ 100 Hz until the caller's deadline (or indefinitely under an unbounded wait) rather than inventing a fatal escalation for legal transient pressure. The overflow test's convergence relies on this kernel/liburing pair's overflow behavior — the dependency the kernel-facts row above records, with one additionally observed nuance: on liburing 2.14, `peek_batch_cqe` performs the overflow flush itself when it finds the ring empty, so CQEs can complete through liburing's own enter without our flush recording an attempt, which is why the test asserts `calls >= 1` across the whole wait rather than attributing a flush to each CQE; kernels or liburing versions where a failed flush leaves overflow unreachable get the probe-silenced park path instead, which the code carries but this suite cannot stage on a healthy kernel. Cancel-control SQEs prepared by `issue_running_cancel_locked_` still submit at the next pass and are covered by the same advertisement; their parked-owner wake latency is unchanged from the reviewed head and out of this round's scope |

## C2-C final corrective round — external W-03 hosts consume the dispatch retry obligation

The frozen review verdict on this branch accepted the owned-driver U2/K15 retry, the
overflow-flush health path, the kernel CQ wake, the registration lifecycle, and the
ring-fd authority removal, with one remaining P1: the external-loop retained transport
retry. The previous corrective round closed the owned-driver half of the
`dispatch_retry_remains` obligation, but an external W-03 host following the then-current
public rule — acknowledge the notification, then poll_progress until neither completed
work nor immediate work remains — could acknowledge the acceptance-time retained-transport
notification, run a drive pass whose submit fails retryably again, observe only
`completed == 0 && !immediate_work_remains`, and enter an indefinite fd-only wait while
the accepted request stayed outside the kernel: no CQE exists, no eventfd write can
arrive, and the stranded work is real. The stale rule lived in the public
`AsyncIoContext::poll_progress` driver-contract comment and in both test-side
`ExternalLoopHost` helpers; the backend and `wait_one` were already correct. This round
closes exactly that hole; the accepted convergence mechanism, registration seam, signal
relocation, overflow path, and internal wait cadence are untouched. No root requirement
changed: PROG-01 names the dispatch obligation, PROG-02(3) licenses the explicitly
scheduled further pass, and PROG-03's "drain/recheck until quiescent or arrange a
self-scheduled continuation" already demanded this shape from external hosts.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Public contract fix | The `AsyncIoContext::poll_progress` comment now states the full external-host drive rule: acknowledge notification, then bounded passes; keep passing while `immediate_work_remains` holds; when `dispatch_retry_remains` holds, schedule a future progress pass — that accepted transport produces no completion and no notification until a pass submits it, so an fd-only wait strands it; `accepted_work_remains` alone parks normally, waiting for the kernel completion notification. The three pass fields stay distinguishable (in-kernel accepted work parks; owner-serviceable work passes now; retained transport schedules later); nothing collapsed into `accepted_work_remains` |
| External-loop host fix | `uring_external_loop_test`'s `ExternalLoopHost` now models the compliant W-03 host: `wait_and_service(horizon)` is one application event-loop wait whose timeout is the time remaining to the scheduled retry pass when one exists and the plain horizon otherwise; a readable fd runs acknowledge plus a bounded immediate drive; an expired deadline runs exactly one progress pass; after every pass the host reconciles — `dispatch_retry_remains` arms exactly one future retry, a pass without it clears the schedule. The retry schedule is obligation-scoped: created only by a pass that reports the retry, consumed by the retry pass, cleared the first pass that does not report it — no periodic idle timer exists, and after the obligation clears an ordinary 150 ms fd poll returns nothing. Per-pass retry reporting never re-signals the eventfd, so the host cannot turn acks into a self-polling loop; persistent EAGAIN retries at the host's own one-shot cadence (10 ms local constant, not public API — the internal `kTransportRetryInterval` stays private). `drive_submission` (the post-acceptance obligation pass) reconciles the schedule too, so a retryable dispatch-time failure schedules the continuation before the host ever waits. The ThreadPool host keeps its shape (its passes never report the retry obligation — verified default-false) with the contract comment updated; `threadpool_external_loop_test` is regression-only |
| New deterministic test | `external_retryable_transport_does_not_strand` (uring external-loop suite, 5/5) arms a count-based submit hook failing `-EAGAIN` on attempts 1–4 before acceptance. Observed trace: the acceptance dispatch-time submit fails (attempt 1) and the retained-transport signal makes the context fd readable; the post-submission drive pass fails again (attempt 2) and the host must already hold the schedule; the first wake's acknowledge+drive fails a third time (attempt 3) with no completion and the obligation standing; scheduled retry passes fail (attempt 4) and reschedule one-shot continuations until the success pass (attempt 5) submits the retained transport into the kernel; the real CQE writes the registered eventfd, the host wakes through the context fd, and the completion is delivered. Assertions: exactly 5 transport attempts, schedule held before successful entry and after the wake, completions delivered before any watchdog bound, retry schedule cleared after success, fd quiet (150 ms poll returns nothing, no pass runs in between), pass count bounded (≤ 12 — no spin, no ticker). Every exit path retires the accepted request before teardown |
| Mutation M-C9 | `SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_HOST_IGNORES_DISPATCH_RETRY` (manual target `uring_external_loop_mut_host_ignores_dispatch_retry`): the host's per-pass retry reconciliation becomes a no-op — the exact stale rule "external host ignores dispatch_retry_remains". Killed by `external_retryable_transport_does_not_strand` 3/3 runs at ~0.4 s, exit 1: under the mutant the schedule never arms, the test's schedule-held assertion fires with the transport frozen at 2 attempts and the completion unpublished (the request never enters the kernel); the four retry-free suite members stay green under the mutant, so the kill is specific to the obligation path. No timeout is a success oracle: the success discriminator is the attempt count, schedule transitions, real submission, real completion, and fd quiescence |
| Validation | Targeted: uring external loop 5/5 ×20 consecutive green (release + debug), race 17/17 (U2/K15, U2b, overflow-flush health included — unchanged), threadpool external loop 5/5, threadpool race 20/20, registration lifecycle 4/4. Full group release+uring and debug+uring: 56/56 registered binaries. No-liburing: 45/45, uring targets absent. clang ASan+UBSan (leak detection on) and TSan: the five targeted suites green with no sanitizer reports. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean |
| Mechanism justification and authority-contraction audit | No production authority moved: the only production change is the `poll_progress` contract comment (documentation of an existing fact). New state is test-only evidence — the host's `retry_deadline_` one-shot schedule, pass/delivery counters, and the M-C9 define — removable with the campaign that uses them; the host's retry interval is a local test constant, not API. Nothing became redundant: the owned-driver nap (`kTransportRetryInterval`), the retained-transport signal, and `dispatch_retry_remains` itself keep their recorded roles; `wait_one` was not redesigned and passed U2/K15/U2b regression in every configuration. No ThreadPool behavior change (its external-loop file changes are comments only). C2-D and C2-E remain open and unclaimed |
| Limits | The host's 10 ms retry cadence is a local test choice mirroring the owned driver's internal nap; the public contract remains "schedule a bounded future progress pass" with no cadence API. The test's persistent-EAGAIN shape is injected through the submit seam on this kernel/liburing pair; the contract claim rests on the `dispatch_retry_remains` field, not on EAGAIN specifics. The immediate-drive budget exhaustion path (64-pass bound) keeps its pre-existing continuation behavior and was not reworked in this round |

Do not merge without it; Issue #397 stays open (C2-D/C2-E) |

## C2-D review record — Issue #397, single progress owner, owner outcomes, sticky control, external-host lifetime

Stage C2-D lands in one implementation pass (no D1–D4 sub-PRs) on the frozen kickoff
direction, baseline `master @ c0a3f04d`. The four frozen items: (D1) a context-owned,
move-only, thread-affine progress-owner capability; (D2) an owner wait report
distinguishing progress / control interruption / deadline expiry / health failure;
(D3) sticky owner control retired only by an explicit acknowledgement; (D4) external-host
stop / settle / detach ordering with a descriptor-reuse probe, so a retired registration
can never authorize or receive events for a later context. The design is the
kickoff-preferred narrow one: an optional owner identity plus a claim/release discipline
inside the existing context — no `ProgressOwnerHandle`, lease, registration-manager, or
shutdown-coordinator class hierarchy, and none of #398 (RequestScope), #399
(Scheduler/Fiber), #400 (cancellation redesign), #401 (shutdown/Destroyable). The
internal dispatch-retry wake stays internal: it is not a public wait outcome. Deadline
expiry never cancels or settles I/O, and the wait clock stays `steady_clock`.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | PROG-01 (one fixed progress owner; no live transfer, no nested polling), PROG-02 (no-lost-wake epoch/token handshake preserved), PROG-03/W-03 (external loop: borrow, ack, detach before teardown), THREAD-01 (poll/prepare/driver wait single-owner; submit/cancel/query stay concurrent), PROG-04 (owned-driver wait outcomes; sticky interruption until the owner observes and acknowledges; monotonic deadline clock), ERR-01 (outcome domains, not semantic rejections), V23 (fd reuse must not route old events to a new context), SHUT-04 (destructor preconditions now include live progress bindings). Explicitly out of scope: #398/#399/#400/#401 |
| Public contract changes | `AsyncIoContext::poll()` returns `Result<std::size_t>` and `poll_progress()` returns `Result<ProgressPass>`: an inert (moved-from) context or a drive from a thread other than the attached owner rejects driving with `invalid_state` instead of racing a second owner. `wait_one()`/`wait_one(duration)` return `Result<ProgressWaitOutcome>` with `Kind::{progress, control_interrupted, deadline_expired, health_failure}` and a completion count; the quiescent observation is `{progress, 0}`, so "idle" and "interrupted" are distinct values. Driving belongs to the context's fixed progress owner: the first drive-family call or `claim_progress_owner()` on an unattached context attaches the calling thread, and the returned move-only `ProgressOwner` is the release handle — its destruction or move-assignment releases the claim and detaches the authority, so a later driver may attach sequentially (PROG-01 fixed ownership; no live transfer). `acknowledge_progress_control()` retires exactly the control generation the owner last observed; control that arrived after that observation stays pending. `progress_notification_fd()` is non-const (a borrow marks the external interest live, and a retired borrow may be followed by a new one); `detach_progress_host()` retires the interest, returns the borrowed fd the host must drop (or -1 when none was live), and is idempotent. The destructor fails fast on an active drive, a live owner handle, or a live notification interest (SHUT-04); the move operations fail fast only on a live owner handle — the driving attachment and any live interest travel with the members they describe; moved-from contexts are inert |
| Single-owner enforcement | One fixed owner thread per context (`owner_thread_`, set by the first drive-family call or `claim_progress_owner()` on an unattached context, cleared only by a `ProgressOwner` release): any drive (`poll`/`poll_progress`/`wait_one`) from another thread while attached fails with `invalid_state`, and a `poll_progress` from inside a delivery hook is rejected through the same owner check plus the drive-overlap guard. `drive_active_` remains as the single-drive overlap/reentrancy guard under the context mutex — it is no longer the ownership authority. `claim_progress_owner()` fails when a handle is already live or another thread holds the attachment. Submit, cancel, query, and observer paths stay concurrent (THREAD-01). Rejections are the documented ERR-01 outcome domain; they are never internal retries or silent skips |
| Sticky control and health | Control state is three generations on `ProgressSource`: current (advanced by `interrupt()` under the shared saturation discipline), observed (recorded when a wait is about to report control), and acknowledged (advanced only by `acknowledge_control()` copying observed). Pending control is the gap between current and acknowledged, plus the spent-domain absorbing state — so an acknowledgement can never retire control that arrived after the observed generation (the mandated A-observed / B-arrives / acknowledge-A / B-still-observed regression is deterministic). `wait_one` runs the progress pass before the control observation: reaped work reports `progress` while raced control stays pending for the next work-free wait, and an interrupted wake re-enters the loop pass-first — control can never strand completable work, including at a spent control domain where every interrupt stays pending forever. Wake reasons: `progress`, `interrupted`, `deadline` (only the caller's own bound yields `deadline_expired`; the internal dispatch-retry nap expiring schedules another bounded pass instead), and `failed`. A non-EINTR `poll(2)` failure inside the park no longer terminates the process: the notification domain can no longer distinguish silence from a lost wake, so the failure is a sticky `health_failed_` verdict that every later wait observes as `health_failure`; `POLLNVAL` keeps fail-fast because it proves a lifetime violation. Sticky control cannot livelock retained consumers: the scheduler's elected worker and `Batch::await_one` acknowledge the control they observe, so the next wait parks |
| External-host lifetime (W-03/V23) | The context tracks a `notification_interest_live_` bool. A `progress_notification_fd()` borrow marks it live; `detach_progress_host()` clears it and hands back the fd the host must drop. Detach is not terminal: the context may lend the fd to a later host, whose borrow is its own registration; a retired registration grants no authority over any later context, including one that reuses the numeric descriptor. Host acknowledgement is a plain drain of the notification source — the interest discipline is the borrow/detach pair, not an acknowledgement-authority check. Destroying a context with a live interest, a live owner handle, or an active drive fails fast (SHUT-04) |
| Retained-consumer migration | The scheduler's elected backend-park worker claims the driving authority for the park and releases it after `wait_one` (a claim rejected because a foreign driver holds the attachment backs the election out and re-runs the cycle); it still acknowledges the `control_interrupted` it observes. The scheduler's drain path claims before its single pass and skips the pass when a parked elected driver already holds the authority (that driver's own passes reap the work); the previous contended-wait branch was unreachable under the fixed owner and is deleted. `Batch::await_one` claims only when it will actually wait (no ready completion and outstanding work) and acknowledges observed control. `read_all`/`write_all` return 0 for zero-length buffers before claiming (zero work acquires no ownership); with work they claim at entry and a rejection returns the error. `one_step`/`sync_step` run under the helper's claimed authority, so their passes cannot be rejected. No consumer hides a rejection or retries around the owner domain |
| New deterministic tests | `threadpool_external_loop_test` in the pinned-owner host shape (19 tests): stop/settle/detach retires the registration (a second detach has nothing to retire; a later host may re-lend the same fd), destroy-with-live-interest is a forked fail-fast scenario (as are destroy/move-assign with a live owner handle), a foreign driver is rejected while an owner is attached, the first drive attaches a fixed owner, the mandated sticky-control regression (a wait observes control A, control B arrives, acknowledging A leaves B observed by the next wait), deadline expiry does not cancel or settle (park-gate held through the expiry; the request completes afterwards), idle and control outcomes are distinct and sticky (three waits all report control until ack), control set before a wait survives a notification + ack cycle, injected health failure is sticky and distinguishable, a batch wait stops on the sticky health verdict through the error channel instead of spinning while it holds the claim, concurrent drives cannot both succeed, nested drive from a delivery hook is rejected, and zero-length `read_all`/`write_all` acquire no ownership. `uring_external_loop_test` carries the same pinned host with wait-outcome control observation and detach at every exit (5 tests). `progress_source_ownership_test` (11) covers outcome kinds, detach semantics, moved-from inertness, and responsibility transfer on move-assign. `threadpool_progress_race_test` (19; the armed-commit R16 retired with its mechanism) and `uring_progress_race_test` (17; driver threads claim/release so sequential attachment is exercised on both backends, with claim-scoped setup passes on the calling thread) keep the epoch/no-lost-wake campaigns; the coalesced control+CQE wake (k10a/k10b) now asserts the pass reaps the completion while the raced control survives the reap and is observed by the next wait — the pre-corrective control-first expectation is withdrawn (control may not strand completable work under the pass-first ordering). Cutover and registration-lifecycle suites adapted to the claim discipline and Result forms |
| Validation | Baseline master `c0a3f04d`, branch `feat/397-c2-d-owner-outcomes-lifetime`, corrective-round head, Linux WSL2 kernel 6.18, gcc 15 debug and clang 21 sanitizers. gcc debug `--liburing=y`: 56/56 registered binaries green after the last code change. clang ASan+UBSan (leak detection on) over the focused C2-D set — both progress-race suites, both external-loop suites, both cutover suites, both ownership suites, both public-request suites: 12/12, zero reports. clang TSan over the concurrency set — both progress-race suites, both external-loop suites, both cutover suites: 6/6, zero reports. All 22 registered mutation binaries still killed at this head (3 C2-C progress/external-loop mutants, 5 request-core mutants, 6 public-request mutants, 8 uring-cutover mutants). `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean; `git diff --check` clean. The discriminating evidence for this round is the direct fork-based fail-fast scenarios, gate-driven interleavings, the mandated sticky-control regression, and the sticky/identity assertions named above. The same matrix re-ran green at the closing-pass correction head (56/56, 12/12, 6/6, 22/22 mutants) |
| Mechanism justification | New production state, each answering the gate: the control generation pair (current/observed/acknowledged) — represents "control not yet retired, distinguished by generation"; no single pending flag can both stay sticky and retire only the observed control (the acknowledgement-erase defect this round closed); replaces `control_pending_` and the armed-commit machinery. `owner_thread_` — the fixed-owner attachment fact (PROG-01), cleared only by a `ProgressOwner` release; not derivable. `owner_claimed_` — the at-most-one release-handle fact the borrow contract enforces. `drive_active_` — the single-drive overlap/reentrancy guard (a physical fact independent of who owns). `health_failed_` — physical obligation: after a park poll(2) failure the wake domain cannot distinguish silence from a lost wake; replaces the old process-wide `wait_domain_fail_fast_` on that branch (POLLNVAL branch retained as fail-fast). `ProgressPass::health_failed` — derived report of that verdict (PROG-01 names context health in the pass report); no independent state. `WakeReason::deadline/failed` — derived refinement of the old `interrupted` so nap expiry, caller deadline, and health are distinguishable; replaces the `kDeadlineClassifySlack` heuristic (deleted in the first pass). `notification_interest_live_` — the external registration's live-borrow fact behind the fd-reuse hazard. `ProgressWaitOutcome` — the D2 outcome vocabulary; replaces a bare completion count that conflated idle with interruption. `Result`-returning drives — the ERR-01 rejection domain for non-owner driving. `release_progress_owner_()` — derived convenience for the handle's release path; no independent fact |
| Authority-contraction audit | First pass removed: `kDeadlineClassifySlack`; the non-EINTR `wait_domain_fail_fast_` branch (superseded by the sticky health verdict); the bare-count `wait_one`/`poll` signatures (replaced by the outcome vocabulary); `const` on `progress_notification_fd` (incompatible with interest marking). Corrective round removed: the armed-commit machinery (`ProgressSource::arm_committed_wait()`/`consume_committed_wait()`, the `armed_*` fields, the public `AsyncIoContext::arm_progress_wait_commit()`, and the R16 regression that pinned it) — obsoleted by the control generation pair; `control_pending_` and the public `progress_control_pending()`; the `NotificationInterest::detached` terminal state (a live bool remains; detach is non-terminal); `async_progress_acknowledgement_fail_fast` (the acknowledgement-authority fail-fast class — acknowledgement is a plain drain); `ProgressOwner::owner_thread_` (the context's attachment is the only thread fact, so the moved-handle thread-affinity machinery was unreachable in the corrected design); the `Token` control fields (a snapshot no longer carries control); the scheduler's `wait_drive_contended` branch (unreachable under the fixed owner) and `ParkLedgerRecord::control_generation` (forensics field of the deleted mechanism); and the move-source fail-fasts for an active drive or live interest (only the live-handle fact, which names the source object, is checked; the attachment and interest travel with the moved members; move-assign keeps the destination teardown checks). Retained deliberately: the epoch/exhaustion token machinery (still the no-lost-wake authority), the `kTransportRetryInterval` nap (internal retry obligation, unchanged), `dispatch_retry_remains` (its consumer contract was repaired in C2-C), `interrupt_progress_waiters` (the producer-side control signal), and `drive_active_` as the overlap guard (an independent physical obligation, not a duplicate of the owner check) |
| Limits | A pinned external owner (or any thread attached by a bare drive call — only a `ProgressOwner` release detaches) plus a running Scheduler on the same context is not a defined v1 workload; in that mixed mode the elected worker's rejected claim backs the election out, so the worker re-runs its cycle without parking until the foreign driver releases (no guard mechanism was added — out of root scope). A `ProgressOwner` handle may be released from any thread; releasing while the attached thread is mid-drive re-anchors the attachment at the next claim — `drive_active_` still forbids any overlap, but callers should release handles from the claiming thread's scope. The scheduler claims and releases per park cycle rather than pinning permanently: a permanent pin would deadlock re-election after worker shutdown, and PROG-01's sequential re-attachment via release is the legal shape. `wait_one` observes control only on the owner's drive path; host acknowledgement is a plain drain. Deadline outcomes carry no remainder estimate; the caller re-arms its own bound. The C2-E formal closure (TLA+ mapping of the sticky-control and single-owner transitions, V10–V12/V23–V25 verdicts) is not claimed here |
| Status | C2_D_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW — the corrective round, the first re-review round, and the closing independent pass (semantic + minimum-mechanism) are complete: no open correctness/authority findings, no P1/P2 defects, NET-CONTRACTION-CONFIRMED at the closing head. Awaiting merge authorization; do not merge without it. #398/#399/#400/#401 remain unclaimed; Issue #397 stays open (C2-E) |

### C2-D corrective round — independent review findings and resolutions

The first C2-D pass was reviewed by two independent subagents (semantic/concurrency and
minimum-mechanism) against the review baseline `f2940143`. Four findings were accepted and
fixed in one corrective pass on the same branch; no new driver framework, control queue,
event hierarchy or registry was introduced.

| Finding | Defect at the baseline | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| P1-1 sticky control | `acknowledge_progress_control()` cleared a single `control_pending_` flag: acknowledging the observed control also erased a control that arrived after the observation (the mandated A-observed / B-arrives / ack-A sequence lost B) | Control generations: current (advanced by `interrupt()`), observed (recorded by the reporting wait), acknowledged (advanced only by acknowledgement copying observed); pending is the current-vs-acknowledged gap, so only the observed generation is retired. Deterministic regression: `acknowledging_observed_control_keeps_a_later_control` |
| P1-2 fixed owner | Ownership was the transient per-drive `drive_active_` domain plus a claimable capability — any thread could drive whenever no drive was active, which is not PROG-01 fixed ownership | Attach-on-first-drive: `owner_thread_` pins at the first drive-family call or claim; only a `ProgressOwner` release detaches (sequential re-attachment legal); `drive_active_` remains as the overlap/reentrancy guard. The scheduler claims per park cycle and backs the election out on a rejected claim; `Batch` claims only when it will wait; helpers claim once with work |
| P1-3 permanent detach | `NotificationInterest::detached` was terminal: after one external host detached, the context could never lend the notification fd again, and acknowledgement after detach was a fail-fast — a permanent single-attachment rule the root never states | A live-interest bool; detach retires the current borrow only, a later host may re-lend the fd (same descriptor, new registration); acknowledgement is a plain drain. The moved-from/teardown fail-fasts stay |
| P2 zero-work ownership | `read_all`/`write_all` claimed the driving owner before checking the buffer, so a zero-length helper could reject or attach for no work | Zero-length check first: zero work acquires no ownership (`zero_work_helpers_skip_owner_acquisition`) |

Two design corrections were forced by the fixed-owner work and are recorded as part of this
round: `wait_one` now runs the progress pass before the control observation (control can never
strand completable work, including at a spent control domain — the k10a/k10b expectations were
re-anchored accordingly, still proving the raced control survives the reap and is observed by
the next wait), and the armed-commit machinery (`arm_committed_wait`/`consume_committed_wait`,
R16) was deleted as obsoleted by the generation pair. The uring-side race/cutover/public-request
suites were converted to the claim discipline (driver threads claim/release; calling-thread
setup passes are claim-scoped), which is what the fixed owner makes the legal single-owner
shape for those interleavings.

### C2-D final re-review round

Two independent subagents re-reviewed the corrective head (semantic/concurrency;
minimum-mechanism). Verdicts: semantic A–F PASS (sticky generations, fixed owner, no-lost-wake
with the progress-only token, THREAD-01 paths, W-03/SHUT-04 lifecycle, scheduler claim/release
balance including every election back-out path); minimum-mechanism NET-CONTRACTION-CONFIRMED
(every retained state field carries a concrete MUST-fail consequence; no dangling reference to
any deleted mechanism; all test seams consumed). Accepted items fixed in the re-review round:

- `Batch::await_one` hot-spun forever on the sticky `health_failure` outcome (its loop had no
  exit for it; the defect dates to the first C2-D pass): the verdict now ends the wait through
  the error channel (`backend_error`), never as a zero-completion report, with the regression
  `batch_await_stops_on_health_failure` — which also gives `Batch`'s conditional claim its first
  direct evidence.
- Deleted two unreachable guards flagged as defensive insurance: the `progress_ == nullptr`
  disjunct in `detach_progress_host` (interest is only ever set with a live source) and the
  post-switch fallback return in `wait_one` (all four wake reasons return or continue).
- The binding fail-fast message again names relocation, not only teardown (moves with a live
  source handle call the same diagnostic).

Deferred to the record, not fixed (reviewer observations): the foreign-thread `ProgressOwner`
release and the attach-without-handle mixed-mode election spin are caller-misuse/undefined-
workload territory and are now named in Limits; `Batch` itself has no consumer outside its
regression — retirement or a consuming slice is a follow-up decision, not a C2-D change; the
five protocol-narration comments in `progress_source.hpp`/`async_io_context.cpp` follow the
files' established header-doc convention and the comment-authority check is clean.

### C2-D closing independent pass

Two independent subagents re-reviewed the re-review head `303be4ea` (semantic/concurrency;
minimum-mechanism). Verdicts: no P1/P2 correctness or authority defects — the six audited
areas (fixed owner, control acknowledgement races, wait outcomes, notification lifetime,
move/destruction preconditions, Scheduler/Batch/helper adaptation) are clean;
minimum-mechanism NET-CONTRACTION-CONFIRMED with zero mechanism deletions. Adjudication of
the remaining P3 findings and suggestions:

- ACCEPT (P3, semantic): the pass report omitted the context-health fact PROG-01 names.
  `ProgressPass::health_failed` now reports the source's sticky wait-health verdict, filled
  at the single pass site — a derived summary, no new state, no second health channel; the
  sticky-health regression now asserts the sick pass reports it.
- ACCEPT (P3, semantic): `signal()`'s exhaustion sequence could wrap at the outer boundary
  where `interrupt()`'s already saturates. The clamp mirrors the control-domain guard
  (PROG-02 no-alias discipline); the boundary is at 2^64 and untestable, so no seam was
  added for it.
- ACCEPT (minimum-mechanism): deleted the unreachable `!backend_` guard in
  `run_progress_pass_` (every caller sits behind the admission gate that rejects inert
  contexts) and the two armed-commit tombstone members in the ownership suite's negated
  port concept — naming deleted APIs made the whole `requires` conjunction vacuously true,
  so the five real negations were untested.
- REJECT: hoisting the in-park health re-check (the leaf's own contract — no wait parks or
  reports idle once health failed — is evaluated at every park transition; no measured
  hotspot); consolidating the move operations' lock sections (no semantic gain); the
  `AsyncStats` data-race observation (incorrect: every field has a single writer thread or
  all writers under the context mutex, and disjoint fields cannot race — the zero TSan
  reports agree).

Re-validated at the correction head: gcc debug `--liburing=y` 56/56, clang ASan+UBSan
focused 12/12, clang TSan concurrency 6/6, all 22 mutation binaries still killed;
`scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` and `git diff --check` clean.

### C2-D #441 evidence re-anchor — staged-delivery cancel regression

PR #440's release CI failed only `cancel_during_the_staged_delivery_window_completes_normally`
at `b88ba040`, on the assertion "the canceling thread enters the scheduler window"; the
semantic refusal assertion never failed. Issue #441 re-anchored that C1-era case; no
production semantic changed.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Previous evidence mechanism | The C1-era host regression (C1-G row above) ordered cancel-before-drain with a delivery pause plus a canceler blocked behind the progress serialization while holding `Scheduler::global_mtx_`; the test probe waited until that lock was observed held. |
| Why it expired | C2-D made progress ownership fixed (`poll_progress()` → owner admission → `DriveGuard` → `run_progress_pass_()`) and removed the whole-progress-pass `access_mtx_` serialization, so submit/cancel/query/observer paths stay concurrent (THREAD-01). A cancel inside an already-claimed delivery now completes immediately and is refused by the observer state machine (`delivering → delivery_in_progress`); the remaining `global_mtx_` hold is far too short for the probe to observe. The probe was an implementation lock-topology oracle, not the contract. |
| New evidence mechanism | The delivery gate pins `ObserverPhase::delivering`, and the cancellation thread must complete while that gate is still pinned. The rewritten case proves: delivery pinned (`wait_registry_live_count == 1`, `slots_in_delivering_phase == 1`) → cancel completes before gate release (the 10 s deadline is a hang watchdog only, never the oracle) → refusal (`cancel_outcome == 0`) → wait record still live → observer still delivering → gate released → normal delivery, retirement and reclaim. This adds executable THREAD-01 concurrency evidence to the retained OBS-02 semantic obligation. |
| Discrimination | `SLUICE_C1_MUTANT_CANCEL_DURING_DELIVERY_RETURNS` built into this host suite kills the case 5/5 (refusal plus both undisturbed-window assertions); a temporary local restoration of the old whole-pass `access_mtx_` serialization fails only the concurrent-completion assertion, 3/3, bounded at the watchdog with no hang; a temporary local `delivering → queued` perturbation on refusal fails the delivering-phase assertion deterministically. |
| Evidence | gcc 15.2, Linux WSL2 kernel 6.18, `runtime_waiter_observer_test`, liburing off. Pre-fix at `b88ba040`: release 11/20 fail on this host (historical #441 record: 15/20), debug 1/60 fail, master `c0a3f04d` 0/20 — every observed failure was the removed lock probe; the refusal assertion never failed in any run. Post-fix: release 100/100 and debug 100/100 consecutive green; full CI-shaped suites (`xmake test -v`) 45/45 in both modes; exact-head CI all green. Sanitizers: clang 21 TSan ran the suite 30/30 with zero data races (all five cases pass), and ASan+UBSan ran 30/30 with zero reports once the pre-existing first-case harness use-after-free below is locally removed. Both sanitizers report that pre-existing defect — `completion_wake_rides_the_observer_adapter` reads `Scheduler::wait_registry_live_count` after `rt->join()` destroyed the Scheduler (reproduced identically at `b88ba040`), so a clean sanitizer verdict for the committed suite is **NOT RUN — known pre-existing blocker**, out of #441's scope; the local-adjustment runs are recorded in the [issue evidence comment](https://github.com/jnhu76/Sluice/issues/441#issuecomment-5932318885) |
| Authority contraction | Deleted `scheduler_global_lock_becomes_held()` and `Scheduler::AsyncTestAccess::try_lock_global_for_test`/`unlock_global_for_test`; repo-wide `rg` (including ignored build trees) reports zero occurrences. |
| Non-change | No OBS semantic changed; no cancel semantic changed; no production lock was added; no RequestCore state-machine rule changed; the C1-G row above remains at its own baseline. Only the obsolete C1 lock-topology observation mechanism was retired and re-anchored to the C2-D authority boundary. |

## C2 A-D final conformance and contraction record — Issue #397, closing pass over the merged C2-A/B/C/D implementation

One pass over the merged A-D implementation after PR #440 and the #441 evidence
re-anchor. Two questions only: does the combined implementation conform to the
normative progress/wait contract, and which defensive/historical mechanisms
became deletable once the final single-owner protocol existed. C2-E is not
started by this record; no historical stage record above was rewritten.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | PROG-01 (bounded pass, pass report incl. context health), PROG-02 (no-lost-wake under the final single-owner matrix), PROG-03 (external-loop detach ordering), PROG-04 (health outcome composition and ordering), THREAD-01/02 (owner matrix unchanged), the narrow SHUT-04 external-binding precondition. Explicitly excluded: C2-E TLA+/V24, #398 RequestScope, #399 Scheduler/Fiber, #400 cancel redesign, #401 shutdown state machine |
| Implementation | Base `86571e48` (live master, the PR #440 merge). Branch `fix/397-c2-abcd-conformance-contraction`. Candidates were verified against code before any change; the two contraction decisions ran as ablations against the retained deterministic evidence |
| Change — F1 bounded pass | Both backends' publication loops and the uring CQ reap now snapshot their eligible work at pass entry: ThreadPool `poll_progress()` publishes at most `publication_pending_.size()` observed under `work_mtx_` at entry; uring `poll()` bounds its publication loop by the entry `publication_pending_->size()` and `reap_cqes()` reaps at most `io_uring_cq_ready()` at entry (a second snapshot covers the post-overflow-flush batch). Work arriving during the pass is the next pass's immediate work: uring `poll_progress()` now folds `has_immediate_physical_work()` into `immediate_work_remains` so leftover CQ/overflow state is never reported as idle (PROG-02: draining an eventfd while leaving unadvertised queued work is invalid). The ThreadPool report was already honest (`immediate_work_remains` from the post-pass queue state). No budget object, policy type or pass-serialization was introduced; the #441 concurrency property is untouched (per-item lock scopes unchanged) |
| Change — F2 health composition | `run_progress_pass_()` is the single aggregation point: `pass.health_failed = backend report ‖ core_->health_failed() ‖ progress_->wait_health_failed()`. `UringAsyncBackend::poll_progress()` reports `fatal_error_` under `dispatch_mtx_` (ThreadPool has no poison state; the base default reports false). `wait_one()` lost its pre-pass health shortcut: each wait runs its bounded pass first, reports `progress` when the pass published caller-visible completions, `health_failure` next when sticky health is present, then control/deadline/park — so sticky health never prevents one required pass from publishing already-actionable work (the M-C8 poison-retirement shape), and the deadline branch got the same two-step precedence. The pass report and the wait outcome now both expose backend poison, closing the silent-park gap the C2-D record scoped to the wait domain only |
| Change — C1 contraction (reap-side signal) | Deleted `signal_ready_progress()` in `finalize_operation_terminal_`. The C2-C record itself classified its covering interval (H) as beyond the THREAD-01 execution matrix, and C2-D's rewrite of k14 to the peer-rejection oracle removed the last test that killed its absence. Under the fixed single owner the reaper is never a peer: kernel completions wake the registered eventfd at CQE publication and the parked owner's `has_immediate_physical_work` probe covers the drain race, so no supported schedule consumes the signal. All obligation signals are retained verbatim: ThreadPool worker completion, zero-op delayed reclaim, cancel `won_before_execution`, backend poison/recovery, transport-retry advertisement, admission close |
| Change — C2 contraction (`overflow_flush_serviceable_`) | Ablated after F2: with the failed flush poisoning `fatal_error_` and the composed health verdict ending every wait behind the unflushable overflow (`wait_one` returns `health_failure` after one empty pass; the external host's drive loop stops on `health_failed`), the shadow bit had no remaining independent responsibility — the probe spin it guarded is unreachable because health is checked before any re-park. Deleted the field, its store, and its mutant variant at the probe; the `SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_OVERFLOW_FLUSH_IGNORED` macro remains at the flush site where the M-C8 oracle still kills it (no poison → no health → no submit rejection), re-verified |
| Change — F3 detach ordering | `detach_progress_host()` is now `void` and its contract is reversed: the host must unregister the fd from its event loop and retire every callback that might use the context **before** calling it; the call retires the context's external-notification interest, acknowledging the registration authority is already gone. The previous contract (detach first, then the returned fd tells the host what to drop) left a host-controlled window in which the context believed no external binding existed while a loop still owned one — the wrong direction for the SHUT-04 teardown precondition and the `notification_interest_live_` fail-fast that enforces it. The fd return value is gone: a host that registered through `progress_notification_fd()` already knows the fd |
| Change — F4 control acknowledgement contract | Documentation only: `acknowledge_progress_control()` now states its caller precondition — the attached progress owner, retiring the control generation that owner's own wait outcome observed (PROG-04 owner action; THREAD-01 driver-wait family). The current/observed/acknowledged generation triple was already sufficient; no thread-id guard was added |
| Public contract changes | `AsyncIoContext::detach_progress_host()` returns `void` (was `int`, the borrowed fd or -1). The root specifies the host-detach ordering (PROG-03) but never the return shape; the old return documented the inverse ordering, so this is a conformance correction, not a root amendment. No other public signature changed; `NEW_PRODUCTION_CLASSES=0`, `NEW_POLICY_TYPES=0`, `NEW_MANAGER_TYPES=0`, `NEW_REGISTRATION_ABSTRACTIONS=0`; `NEW_PRODUCTION_ENUMS=0`; `NEW_PRODUCTION_FIELDS=0` |
| New deterministic evidence | `f1_publication_pass_is_entry_bounded` on both backends (threadpool: the held worker completing a gated request mid-publication, observed through the progress token — the pass stops at its entry bound and reports `immediate_work_remains`; uring: a won-before-execution cancel of an undispatched entry held undispatched by a retryable submit failure). `f2_backend_poison_reaches_owner_health_verdict` (uring: the poisoned submit retires the request; the first wait reports `progress` with the retirement's completion, the pass report exposes `health_failed` with nothing accepted or immediate, and the next wait returns `health_failure` promptly instead of parking). Host drive loops in both external-loop suites now stop on `health_failed`, modelling the documented host contract |
| Regression evidence | gcc 15.2, Linux WSL2 6.18. Release `--liburing=y`: threadpool race 20/20, uring race 19/19, threadpool external loop 19/19, uring external loop 5/5, progress-source ownership 11/11, registration lifecycle 4/4, cutover 16/16 + 21/21, public request 13/13, release-violation 4/4, core protocol 45/45 + publication 11/11 + ownership 8/8, admission 4/4, runtime-waiter 5/5 (#441 case green), uring smoke 8/8, semantic conformance 0 divergences, app consumption 10/4/5/5. Debug `--liburing=y`: the same 17-suite focused set green. Release `--liburing=n`: the ThreadPool-side 11-suite set green. clang 21 ASan+UBSan (`-fsanitize=address,undefined`, leak detection on): both race suites, both external loops, ownership, both cutovers, public request — all green, zero reports. clang TSan: both progress race suites + threadpool cutover green, zero reports. Mutation re-verification at the new head: `SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_OVERFLOW_FLUSH_IGNORED` killed by `overflow_flush_failure_becomes_observable_health_event`, `SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_TRANSPORT_RETRY_UNADVERTISED` killed by `u2_k15`, `SLUICE_B1C_MUTANT_HOST_IGNORES_DISPATCH_RETRY` killed (exit 134, no pass). Full registered suites (`xmake test`): 56/56 at gcc release `--liburing=y` (rerun after the contraction round); 55/56 under a full clang TSan sweep of every target, the sole failure being the case below. The contraction round reran: mutants 3/3 killed, debug focused 9/9, ASan+UBSan focused 4/4 with zero reports, TSan both race suites with zero reports, no-liburing release 5/5 focused. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Pre-existing blocker unchanged and out of scope: `runtime_waiter_observer_test` case `completion_wake_rides_the_observer_adapter` reads the freed Scheduler after `join()` under sanitizers (heap-use-after-free in the test seam access, reproduced identically at `b88ba040` per the #441 record; this pass changes no scheduler or test-harness code for that suite, and the suite is green unsanitized) |
| Authority-contraction audit | Deleted this pass: the uring reap-side pending-transition signal (C1), `overflow_flush_serviceable_` (C2), the `detach_progress_host()` fd return (F3), the `wait_one` pre-pass health shortcut (F2 — a branch, not a fact); and from the independent contraction review, three verified-dead historical mechanisms — the `tax0` ablation seams (`tax0_ablation_seams.hpp`, the two `Completion` gate wrappers, the dual `tax0_f01_update_max_outstanding` definitions and their 12 call sites; no setter exists anywhere, all modes permanently false), the uring `reap_cqes` reaped-op counter (sole call site discards it), and the ThreadPool dead accessors (`dispatch_high_water_mark`, `dispatch_high_water_for_test`, the write-only `high_water_`/`high_water()`, `BoundedHandleRing::capacity()`, `configured_worker_count()`; zero callers repo-wide) plus the empty `src/async/fdg0_unowned/` directory. `reap_seq_` itself is retained (consumed by `Batch`). Audited and retained with named obligations: every remaining signal site (worker completion, zero-op reclaim, cancel, poison, retry advertisement, admission close); `dispatch_retry_remains` + `kTransportRetryInterval`; `notification_interest_live_`; `drive_active_` (physical overlap fact independent of ownership); the epoch/exhaustion machinery (PROG-02 authority; its reduction is C2-E/V24); the three control generations; `RequestCore::health_failed_` (core authority fact; now consumed by the context composition); `has_immediate_physical_work` (the kernel-producer fact the userspace epochs cannot express); kernel eventfd registration and the destructor unregister pairing |
| Independent review | Two subagent reviews of the final patch. Conformance reviewer: CONFORMS in all eight areas (PROG-01/02/03/04, THREAD-01/02, W-03, narrow SHUT-04), #441 property explicitly protected, with one attributable finding (the k14 test comment still narrating the deleted signal — fixed) and one pre-existing observation recorded below. Contraction reviewer: both in-diff deletions confirmed safe and complete (after the same k14 comment fix and the supersession markers now on the C2-C probe-soundness and memory-order rows), plus six further candidates. Main-agent adjudication: ACCEPTED the three verified-dead removals above and the hygiene items; DEFERRED `has_bounded_split_wait_capability()` (tautological public probe; a public-API removal wanting its own slice with scheduler evidence) and collapsing `UringAsyncBackend::available_` onto `have_ring_` (representation rename, no semantic delta); REJECTED deletion of the scheduler claim-rejection error paths (they are the `Result` error returns of a public operation, not retry mechanism) |

## C2 A-D corrective pass record — Issue #397, DeliveryRecord synchronization handoff

One corrective pass over the final A-D implementation, closing the one
limitation that record left open. The final A-D record had observed that
`DeliveryRecord` fields are initialized by submit threads without a backend
lock while the owner's event-owed sweep reads them — behaviorally masked by
slot pinning, but formally a race under THREAD-01's
submit-concurrent-with-poll permission, which #441 had made reachable by
removing whole-pass `access_mtx_` serialization. The stale header comments
still claiming the owning context's access mutex serialized the records were
false since C2-D. No other A-D decision is revisited; C2-E is not started.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | THREAD-02 (backend-private mutable state carries a valid synchronization discipline) at the narrowest site: the delivery-record handoff between submit and the progress owner. THREAD-01's owner matrix is unchanged and non-negotiable; no PROG contract, pass report or signal semantics changed |
| Change — ThreadPool | Delivery-record handoff now runs under the pre-existing `work_mtx_`: (1) `submit_request` initializes `completion/publish/kind/event_owed/owed_key` inside a `work_mtx_` critical section before the slot becomes backend-visible; (2) `publish_zero_op_inline` marks `event_owed/owed_key` under `work_mtx_` with the completion publish callback still outside the lock; (3) the `poll_progress` sweep consumes and clears `event_owed` and copies `owed_key/kind` to locals under `work_mtx_`, then delivers the event and releases the control ref outside; (4) the test-only `event_owed_for_test` reader takes the same mutex. `publish_one` stays lock-free: its `publish`/`completion`/`kind` reads happen while `publication_inflight` pins the slot (`reclaimable_` requires `published && !publication_inflight`, so neither a binding release nor anything else can free the slot mid-publication), and `kind` is copied to a local before `complete_publication` — the earliest point the slot can be reclaimed and re-initialized by a concurrent submit — so no record field is read past the pin. The reads' ordering against the initializing writer rides the queue-transition chain (init under `work_mtx_` → dispatch push → worker pop → `publication_pending_` push → owner pop, every transition under `work_mtx_`, which also covers the cancel-won-before-execution push that bypasses `dispatch_`) |
| Change — io_uring | The same three sites under the pre-existing `dispatch_mtx_` (`submit_request` init, `publish_zero_op_inline` owed marking, `poll()` sweep with local copies); `publish_one` lock-free with the same pinned `kind` copy before `complete_publication`, ordered by the same queue-transition chain (dispatch and CQE handling already run under `dispatch_mtx_`). No callback, sink or scheduler code runs under a backend lock on either backend |
| Independent review | Two subagent reviews of the patch. Concurrency reviewer verdict after the first round: the three mutex sites and the queue-transition chains are sound, no callback-under-lock, no lock-order change, #441 property intact — with one attributable finding, accepted and fixed in this pass: `publish_one` read `record.kind` after `complete_publication`, where a `Completion::reset()` releasing the binding during `publication_inflight` (sanctioned by the core's visibility clause) or a bindingless submit lets `complete_publication` reclaim the slot, so a concurrent submit re-initializes the record before that unlocked read — a pre-existing formal race on the same handoff. Fix: copy `kind` to a local while the pin still holds (both backends), all suites rerun green. Contraction reviewer verdict: CONTRACTION_CLEAN (no new mechanism hunk-by-hunk, no whole-pass serialization, no callback under lock, no site removable without losing an edge on a supported execution). The concurrency reviewer also noted the regression tests' TSan discrimination covers the owed-marking pair rather than init-vs-sweep (the pause gate itself orders the init writes); accepted as recorded — the init section's edge is established by the reviewer's own interleaving analysis, the cancel-before-dispatch path included |
| Deterministic evidence | `record_handoff_survives_submit_racing_owner_sweep` in both progress race suites: a submitting thread paused at the pre-accept-commit gate between record initialization and binding while the owner sweeps every slot through backend passes, then the delivery loop runs concurrently with the submitter's owed-event marking before any join — the assertions are nothing-completed-in-window, exactly one completion (0-byte success), exactly one owed-event delivery, quiet settle pass. Mutation-style discrimination: with the production change reverted, clang TSan reports the exact unsynchronized pair (ThreadPool write `threadpool_backend.cpp:342-343` vs sweep read `:536`; uring write `uring_backend.cpp:1144-1145` vs sweep read `:1217`, both exits 66); with the change, zero reports. The pause gate alone cannot carry the report: its release/acquire publishes the initialization writes, so the discriminating overlap is the post-release owed marking versus the concurrent sweep, which is what the test drives |
| #441 non-regression | No `access_mtx_` is held across `backend_->poll_progress()`; no whole-pass serialization was introduced (the sweep's critical sections are per-record; `publish_one` and the publication loop are unchanged lock-free). The staged-delivery concurrent-cancel case is green in every configuration below (cancel completes while the delivery gate is pinned, is refused, the wait record stays live, normal completion follows) |
| Contraction audit | Nothing added: no new mutex, atomic, per-slot lock, class, production field, public API or callback wrapper — only narrower scopes of `work_mtx_`/`dispatch_mtx_` and local copies. Nothing became removable: each synchronization site is independently load-bearing (removing the init section reopens the sweep-scan-versus-initialization race — the sweep reads `event_owed` for every slot, pinned or not — and strands a cancel that wins before dispatch with no post-init edge yet; removing the owed-marking section reopens the zero-op marking race the mutation run reproduces; removing the sweep section reopens the read side of both pairs) |
| Build/test evidence | Linux WSL2 6.18.40.1, gcc 15.2.0, clang 21.1.8, liburing 2.14. gcc release `--liburing=y`: full registered suite 56/56 (race suites 21/21 and 20/20 with the new tests). gcc debug `--liburing=y` focused: both race suites, both external loops, both cutovers, runtime-waiter 5/5 (the #441 case green), progress ownership, registration lifecycle — all green. gcc release `--liburing=n`: ThreadPool-side set green (race, external loop, cutover, runtime-waiter, ownership, public request). clang ASan+UBSan: both race suites, both external loops, both cutovers, ownership — green, zero reports; `runtime_waiter_observer_test` aborts on the pre-existing first-case UAF recorded by the #441/A-D records (test seam reads the freed Scheduler after `join()`; this pass changes no scheduler or harness code; the suite is green unsanitized). clang TSan: both race suites (3× consecutive), both external loops, threadpool cutover — zero reports; `runtime_waiter_observer_test` runs all five cases green with exactly the two pre-existing heap-use-after-free warnings of that same test-seam defect, byte-identical with the production change reverted (verified), so no new finding is masked. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean |
| Status | `C2_AD_CORRECTIVE_PASS_PENDING_REVIEW` — the DeliveryRecord limitation of the A-D final record is closed and THREAD-02's delivery-record handoff now conforms at implementation level; this is implementation conformance only, distinct from formal closure: C2-E (TLA+, V24 saturation/no-alias, V10/V11/V12/V23/V25) remains not started and still owns formal closure. C2 is not complete |

## C2-E formal closure record — Issue #397, ProgressSource TLA+ campaign and the V24 boundary finding

Stage C2-E is the formal-methods closure pass over the frozen C2-A/B/C/D
implementation (PR #442, master @ `8be2ced5`; the reviewed A-D head
`1b468fc8` is its parent). Model follows C++: no production line changed
(`PRODUCTION_CPP_CHANGED=NO`). One focused model
(`formal/tla/ProgressSource.tla`) plus thirteen configurations and the gate
`scripts/verify_c2e_progress.sh` (pinned TLC 1.7.4, checksummed, never
committed) formalize the context-owned ProgressSource, the fixed owner's
wait loop, and the external-host binding lifecycle.

The campaign's central result is a **split verdict**: every required
property holds below the progress domain's absorbing saturation pair, and
the exhaustion campaign (V24's explicit mandate) produces a counterexample
exactly at that boundary — a recorded implementation defect of the frozen
protocol's terminal-state design, gated behind 2^64 producer transitions.
Per the C2-E mandate this finding stops the pass: recorded, not fixed;
C2-E does not close.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| MODEL_SHA | `formal/tla/ProgressSource.tla` sha256 `60a5e9009bed4b1ba90f26903d8ef6db8baca5fa3072edc0270e05b3bf0eef20` |
| IMPLEMENTATION_BASE_SHA | `8be2ced54e03ef527a2fb5a41d1ae9c2cbb06985` (live master; PR #442 merge; reviewed head `1b468fc8` included) |
| ROOT_REVISION | `b26ec910875ae7233081d42fe314b81155dbb9d6` |
| TLC_VERSION | TLC2 2.19 of 08 August 2024 (rev 5a47802), tla2tools 1.7.4 pinned sha256 `936a262061c914694dfd669a543be24573c45d5aa0ff20a8b96b23d01e050e88` |
| CONFIGURATIONS | 13: Safety, MutParkDrain, MutNoProbe, MutNoExhaustion, Deadline, Host, ExhNear, ExhTermControl, ExhTermProgress, Liveness, CertSticky, CertSat, CertSpent, CertHost (Safety/Deadline/Host/ExhNear/ExhTermControl/Liveness complete cleanly; the three mutants and ExhTermProgress violate `InvSafePark`; the four certificates are violated — the house must-be-reachable pattern) |

### C2-E reachability adjudication — Issue #397, is `{MAX,MAX}` production reachable?

Adjudication pass over the C2-E record above, on the same frozen
implementation (master @ `8be2ced5`, implementation sources last touched at
`bb8fe8e6`; reviewed formal head `3be4d34c`; root revision `b26ec910`).
`PRODUCTION_CPP_CHANGED=NO`. The original `ExhTermProgress` result is
preserved unchanged above; this record adds its production-reachability
classification. New model text is gated behind three constants
(`CloseRepeat`, `UnboundedWorkload`, `NoExhClose`, all `FALSE` in every
pre-adjudication configuration) plus one proof-oriented invariant; the full
pre-existing 14-configuration gate re-ran at the amended model with every
recorded outcome and state count reproduced identically.

**Signal-source census at the frozen baseline** (every progress-epoch
bumping site; kernel CQE eventfd writes carry no epoch and are excluded):
ThreadPool worker completion (`threadpool_backend.cpp:467`, one per
accepted request), zero-op inline publication (`:350` and
`uring_backend.cpp:1153`, one per accepted zero-op), cancel
`won_before_execution` (`:720`/`:1265`, once per request identity — core
arbitration latches `terminal_chosen`, later attempts return
`already_terminal`), uring retained-transport advertisement at accept
(`:651`, one signal per accept even when the dispatch loop flushes many
SQEs; `newly_poisoned` one-shot; the injected-dispatch-failure term is
test-only), poison recovery walk (`:897`, one-shot — the
`fatal_error_.has_value()` entry guard; `fatal_error_` is never cleared),
and `close_admission()` (`threadpool_backend.cpp:775-780`,
`uring_backend.cpp:1318-1325`): `core_->close_admission()` is an
idempotent bare flag write, then `signal_ready_progress()` fires
**unconditionally on every call** — no admission-open guard, no capacity,
fuel or owner precondition. Retry outcomes are silent
(`account_transport_result_locked`: `rc==0`/`EINTR`/`EAGAIN`/`EBUSY`
return without signaling; only non-retryable errors poison), so no
per-retry-attempt signal exists. Host operations
(`acknowledge_progress_notification`, `poll_progress`, host ack/detach)
never signal; `interrupt_progress_waiters` is control-domain;
`close_admission_on_progress_exhaustion_` (`async_io_context.cpp:462`)
calls the core directly without signaling; `ApplicationRuntime::
request_stop` is one-shot-guarded and control-domain.

**Request-driven bound (scheduling-independent).** Once
`progress_exhaustion_ > 0`, every owner pass closes core admission; in the
window before the first such pass, accepts go only into free slots, and
slot reuse requires owner service on both backends (publication happens
only in the owner's pass; the zero-op `event_owed` control pin holds the
slot until the owner's owed sweep; retained transports hold theirs until
an owner/acceptor submit succeeds). With C = slot capacity:
`BOUND_BEFORE_OWNER_CLOSE <= 2C` (each request contributes at most an
accept-time signal and one terminal signal, completion and cancel-won
mutually exclusive), `BOUND_AFTER_ADMISSION_CLOSE <= C + 1` (outstanding
requests each at most one terminal signal, plus the one-shot poison), so
`TOTAL_POST_FIRST_EXHAUSTION_SIGNAL_BOUND <= 3C + 2 < UINT64_MAX` for
every valid configuration — independent of owner latency. Ranking: Φ =
outstanding requests + pending publications + owed pins + retained
transports + unspent poison; every request-source signal consumes one Φ
unit and no transition replenishes Φ once admission is closed.

**The pump.** `close_admission()` on the backend is the one progress-domain
producer that consumes nothing. THREAD-01 documents the operation as
idempotent and concurrently invocable; PROG-04 requires each call to wake
a potentially sleeping owner (the per-call signal is the root-mandated
shape — a host-side "signal only on state change" guard would recreate the
missed wake PROG-04 forbids). The operation is public on both public
header classes; the live-attached-context invocation pattern (host retains
the reference it constructed, then moves `unique_ptr` ownership into the
context) is exercised in-tree (`threadpool_core_cutover_test.cpp:584`).
A caller looping `close_admission()` executes one legal transition per
iteration: 2·(2^64−1) calls reach `{MAX,MAX}` from any valid initial
state — a finite legal trace with no seam, no undefined behavior and no
contract violation; it needs no accepted request, no capacity and no
owner involvement. The C2-B asymmetry argument ("control interrupts are
externally callable with no request-capacity bound; progress is bounded by
capacity") missed that `close_admission` is an externally-callable
progress-domain signal with no capacity bound; this adjudication
falsifies the ledger's earlier population-bound sentence at the C2-B
record ("post-saturation progress signal population is bounded by
accepted request capacity") — that bound holds only for the
request-driven sources.

**MaxFuel audit.** `INV_NO_PROGRESS_TERMINAL_DEPENDS_ON_MAXFUEL=NO` for
the request-driven sources: `ReachNoFuel` (plain `Submit` consumes no
fuel; `SubmitTransport`/`ZeroOp` keep fuel as the image of their
physically bounded ledger-entry/owed-pin consumption) completes cleanly,
and `MutNoExhClose` (the pass never closes admission on exhaustion)
violates — the load-bearing bound is the exhaustion-close rule plus the
capacity/pin gating, not the fuel. Two fidelity disclosures: (1) the
pre-existing model's `CloseAdmission` action carries an `/\ adm` guard —
one signal per admission-open period — which diverges from the C++
per-call signal; the prior `InvNoProgressTerminal` clean runs rested
partly on that divergence, and `CloseAdmissionRepeat` now encodes the
C++ fact; (2) the campaign added the C++-faithful slot-capacity guard
`acc + accT < MaxAcc` to `SubmitTransport` (accept requires a free slot,
BOUND-01/02) — unobservable in every pre-adjudication configuration
(their `MaxFuel <= 1` already excluded the removed states) and required
for the fuelless runs' finiteness.

**Reachability campaign** (`bash scripts/verify_c2e_progress.sh`,
campaign R; TLC 1.7.4, same pinned jar; all from `InitMode = "std"`):

| Config | Result | States generated / distinct / depth | Terminal reached |
|---|---|---|---|
| ReachClose | `InvNoProgressTerminal` VIOLATED | 452 / 209 / 5 | YES |
| ReachClosePark | `InvSafePark` VIOLATED | 17461 / 4488 / 10 | YES |
| ReachClosePub | `InvNoOrdinaryParkPastPublication` VIOLATED | 21719 / 5295 / 11 | YES |
| ReachNoFuel | clean | 781351 / 161755 / 35 | NO |
| MutNoExhClose | `InvNoProgressTerminal` VIOLATED | 875155 / 191459 / 26 | YES |

`ReachClose` (5 states): `SubmitTransport` → `CloseAdmission` →
`CloseAdmissionRepeat`×3 → `{MaxPE,MaxPX}`. `ReachClosePub` (11 states,
the production-staged composition, mapping 1:1 to C++): `Submit` (the
blocking read accepted) → `CloseAdmission` (epoch+1, admission closed) →
`CloseAdmissionRepeat`×3 (epoch saturates, exhaustion pair completed) →
`OWTop` (pass finds nothing actionable, accepted work remains, ordinary
park licensed at the frozen token) → `WorkerComplete` (publication owed;
its signal bumps nothing, only fd readiness) → `OL4a` (token unchanged,
drain consumes that readiness) → `OProbe`/`OP3` (no CQ, userspace work)
→ ordinary park past the pending publication. `ReachClosePark`'s shortest
trace exercises the poison facet of the same mechanism (EnvPoison's
signal invisible at the frozen pair, its fd write drained, park past
health).

**Reviewer verdicts.** `REVIEWER_A=REACHABLE_TRACE_FOUND` — independently
verified every census site, the cancel one-shot arbitration, the silent
retry outcomes, the owner-gated slot recycling and the request-source
ranking; re-ran all four campaign configs itself (identical outcomes);
confirmed the pump's contract legality (PROG-04's wake mandate makes the
per-call signal the compliant shape) and the retained-reference pattern
(in-tree precedent); found no additional pump; P3 severity note: ~2^64
calls at nanosecond rate is decades and no production caller exists today
— a contract-sanctioned theoretical trace, recorded as severity context,
not a reachability argument (the adjudication standard forbids dismissing
it on practicality). `REVIEWER_B=INFINITE_SIGNAL_SEQUENCE_FOUND`
(blinded, launched without this adjudication's conclusions) —
independently constructed the same repeating trace (`raw->close_admission()`
loop on the live attached context), confirmed every other source bounded
(residual post-exhaustion request-source bound ≤ C+1), and confirmed
legality under THREAD-01/PROG-04. Both reviewers converged without
contradiction; all findings adjudicated and recorded above.

**Health-abstraction alignment (L4a).**
`L4A_HEALTH_ABSTRACTION=TRACE_EQUIVALENT_FOR_CURRENT_HEALTH_SOURCES`: the
model's `OL4a` observes `waitSick` inside the fused step while the C++
checks `health_failed_` at the loop head before the fused section — but
`health_failed_` has no external production writer (only the owner's own
non-EINTR `poll(2)` failure inside the same loop, which returns
immediately), so no interleaving distinguishes the two placements; the
model's `OL4a` waitSick disjunct is in fact unreachable defensive text.
A future external health setter (e.g. a progress-spent sticky-health fix)
would break this equivalence and must participate in the stale-drain race
discipline.

**Adjudication.** The absorbing progress pair is reachable from valid
production initialization through supported transitions: the C++-faithful
close semantics (TLC-certified from `"std"` Init) plus the already-local
`{MAX,MAX}` lost-wake behavior compose into a production-reachable V24
defect. The C2-E `termP` finding is therefore reclassified from
"boundary/assumption-bounded" to production-reachable:
`LOCAL_COUNTEREXAMPLE` + `PRODUCTION_REACHABILITY = REACHABLE` (smallest
concrete trace: the `ReachClosePub` sequence above, at 2^64 scale in the
counting coordinates). Per the adjudication mandate: no production C++
changed in this pass, no fix applied (any repair — a progress-domain
spent rule mirroring `control_domain_spent_`, or refusing unbounded parks
while exhausted, or bounding the close-signal side effect — is a
production design decision requiring separate authorization and root
alignment, and the health-setter caveat above applies to the
sticky-health variant), PR #443 carries the amended campaign for review,
#397 stays open, and the pass stops here for human review.
