## C2-E production corrective pass — Issue #397, transition-bound `close_admission` progress signal

The production half of the corrective split ordered by the C2-E
reachability adjudication (`V24_TERMINAL_PROGRESS_REACHABLE_COUNTEREXAMPLE`,
confirmed). The formal campaign, the boundary finding and the adjudication
itself live in PR #443 and are not duplicated here; this record covers the
C++ side only. Defect being corrected: both backends ran `core_->close_admission()`
(an idempotent core no-op after the first call) followed by an unconditional
`signal_ready_progress()`, so every repeated `close_admission()` bumped the
progress epoch while creating no new core fact — the one post-exhaustion
progress producer with no resource-backed bound. THREAD-01 makes the call
legal from any thread, any number of times, so the progress domain could be
pumped toward its absorbing `{MAX,MAX}` pair, where the C2-E model shows the
park handshake is locally unsafe (`InvSafePark` violated: the fused drain
consumes the producer's fd write and the owner parks over pending work).
Root reading, per the adjudication: THREAD-01 makes `close_admission`
idempotent, and PROG-04's wake obligation attaches to the control fact the
call creates — the first open→closed transition. PROG-02 permits spurious
notifications but does not require manufacturing them. No root amendment is
needed; the unconditional per-call signal was an implementation conformance
gap.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | PROG-04 (`close_admission()` wakes a potentially sleeping owner — satisfied by the open→closed transition signal), THREAD-01 (`close_admission` idempotent, linearized against acceptance, concurrently invocable), PROG-02 (no unbounded non-consuming progress producer; spurious notifications allowed, not mandated). Unchanged: PROG-01 pass semantics; PROG-03 external loop; the exhaustion-close pass path (`close_admission_on_progress_exhaustion_` never signaled and still does not — the owner is mid-pass there); the poison/health path (`note_health_failure()` closes admission inside `poison_and_recover_locked`, which carries its own terminal `signal_ready_progress()`) |
| Change — authority | `RequestCore::close_admission()` returns `bool`: `true` exactly when it performed the open→closed transition under the core mutex; `false` for every repeated close, and for a close after a health failure already closed admission (that transition belongs to the health path, which owns its own wake). The core keeps the serialized admission authority and gains no new state — the verdict is the transition itself, the same verdict shape as `rollback`/`release_*` |
| Change — signal edge | `ThreadPoolBackend::close_admission()` and `UringAsyncBackend::close_admission()` signal only when the core reports the transition; the repeated-call pump is gone. The unattached-backend case (`core_ == nullptr`, port not attached) is observably unchanged — its old signal was already a no-op on an unattached port. `AsyncIoContext::close_admission_on_progress_exhaustion_()` ignores the verdict (owner-internal close, no wake obligation). Authority split preserved: the core answers "did admission transition?", the backend/context decide the notification — no progress capability moves into the core |
| Mechanism justification | (1) Fact: whether the serialized open→closed admission transition occurred; owner is RequestCore under THREAD-01. (2) Need: PROG-04 attaches the wake to that control fact, and the adjudication proved the unconditional per-call signal is the unique unbounded post-exhaustion producer. (3) Not derivable from another authority — the admission bit is core-private. (4) Not a materialized duplicate — it is the transition report itself. (5) Classification: authority verdict replacing a caller-side assumption. (6) Replaces: the unconditional `signal_ready_progress()` after the core call in both backends. (7) Migration: none — no retained consumer depends on repeated-close wakes; a caller with a new shutdown intent must use the control/stop path, which owns its own wake (a future #401 shutdown-policy transition likewise owns its own). Rejected alternatives, per the adjudication: a progress-domain spent rule, health-failure fallback parking, or any terminal-pair defensive mechanism — production safety comes from the pump's unreachability, not from defending the absorbing pair, which stays locally unsafe by design evidence |
| Authority-contraction audit | No runtime state, flag, counter, token or lock added or removed; `admission_open_` is unchanged. The change contracts one signal edge (the unbounded producer). Every other signal site keeps its recorded obligation: worker completion, zero-op reclaim, cancel, poison, retry advertisement, and the exhaustion-sequence bumps in a spent domain. The `SLUICE_C2E_MUTANT_CLOSE_ADMISSION_ALWAYS_SIGNALS` define is test-only mutation evidence, never defined in production builds |
| Deterministic evidence | Core level — `close_admission_transitions_exactly_once` (`tests/request_core_protocol_test.cpp`): the first close reports the transition, three repeats report none, admission stays closed and keeps refusing reservations, and a close after `note_health_failure()` reports no new transition. Backend/owner level — `threadpool_close_admission_signals_only_the_open_to_closed_transition` and the io_uring twin (`tests/progress_source_ownership_test.cpp`, one shared oracle): the first close advances the progress epoch by exactly one and leaves fd readiness (the PROG-04 wake exists); two further closes advance neither the epoch nor the exhaustion sequence and leave the acknowledged fd quiet; the closed core still refuses submission with `invalid_state` |
| Mutation evidence | `SLUICE_C2E_MUTANT_CLOSE_ADMISSION_ALWAYS_SIGNALS` (both backends) restores the pre-fix unconditional signal; built as the manual targets `progress_source_mut_close_admission_always_signals` and `progress_source_uring_mut_close_admission_always_signals`. Both are killed: exit 1, `FAIL: threadpool_close_admission_signals_only_the_open_to_closed_transition` (the shared oracle's first discriminating execution; the uring instance asserts the identical invariant). This is the concrete-side twin of the model mutant `MutRepeatCloseSignals` that the #443 re-closure will run |
| Build/test evidence | Linux WSL2 6.18.40.1-microsoft-standard, gcc 15.2.0 (`/usr/bin/g++`), liburing 2.14 enabled (`xmake f -m <mode> --liburing=y`). gcc debug: full registered suite 56/56 via `xmake test -v` (includes both ownership flavors — 12 and 13 tests — and the 46-case protocol suite). gcc asan and gcc tsan, touched set: progress ownership 12/12 + 13/13, protocol 46/46, threadpool cutover 16/16, uring cutover 21/21, threadpool race 21/21, uring race 20/20 — green, zero reports. Mutant kills as above. `git diff --check` clean; `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py .` clean |
| Remaining limitations | No formal closure is claimed and no V-status converts here. The C2-E TLA+ campaign — including the `termP` boundary finding, which must keep failing because the absorbing pair stays locally unsafe while production safety comes from its unreachability — the `CloseAdmissionRepeat` reclassification to the load-bearing `MutRepeatCloseSignals` mutant, and the full re-run on fixed master belong to PR #443 rebased onto this change. The health abstraction (`L4A_HEALTH_ABSTRACTION` trace-equivalence note) is untouched, and any #401 shutdown-state upgrade owns its own future wake obligation |

## C2-E formal re-closure record — Issue #397, the reachable-state proof on the corrected close semantics

The formal half of the corrective split, completing the interrupted C2-E
campaign against the adopted production state. History preserved end to
end: the original campaign recorded the `termP` local counterexample
(`C2_E_FOUND_IMPLEMENTATION_COUNTEREXAMPLE`); the reachability
adjudication proved the absorbing pair production-reachable through the
per-call `close_admission` signal; #444 removed that pump; this pass
re-based PR #443 onto the corrected master and re-proved every
obligation over reachable states only. `PRODUCTION_CPP_CHANGED=NO`
(`git diff 7bd1976f..5895bda9 -- 'src/' 'include/' 'apps/' 'xmake.lua'
'xmake/' 'tests/'` is empty). Rebase record:
`PR_HEAD_BEFORE=3be4d34c`, adjudication commit `45b4974a` preserved
(backup ref `backup/397-c2-e-adjudication`), `LIVE_MASTER=7bd1976f`
(the #444 merge), final model commit `5895bda9`.

The central result is now a **three-part verdict**, not a split one:

```text
LOCAL_SAFETY_AT_TERMP            = FAIL (preserved certificate)
PRODUCTION_REACHABILITY_OF_TERMP = UNREACHABLE (proved)
MUT_REPEAT_CLOSE_RESTORATION     = REACHABLE AGAIN (mutant discriminator)
```

These do not contradict: the representation boundary remains locally
unsafe by design evidence (the representation cannot express the
unadvertised obligation, so a seam-placed `{MAX,MAX}` initial state
violates `InvSafePark`), production safety arises from the
unreachability of that state under the corrected transition system
(#444 removed the only non-consuming producer), and the
`MutRepeatCloseSignals` mutant restoring exactly the removed signal
edge re-reaches the pair and its lost-wake composition from normal
Init — so the unreachability proof rests on the actual pump removal,
not on domain sizes or fuel.

## C2-E final amendment — human-review adjudication (Issue #397)

The human final review accepted the C2-E method and the three-part
verdict and returned `REQUEST_CHANGES` with one formal P1 and one
closure P2. `PRODUCTION_CPP_CHANGED=NO` still holds: the amendment
touches `formal/tla/ProgressSource.tla` comments, this ledger and the
PR description only; the TLA transition system, constants and
configurations are semantically untouched.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| HUMAN_ADJUDICATION | `REQUEST_CHANGES` — P1: the previous production instantiation of the post-exhaustion bound ("C is configuration-bounded, so `3C + 2 < UINT64_MAX`") did not entail the inequality; a bounded C can still exceed the 64-bit domain. P2: the top-level PROG/W-03 row remained `NOT_ASSESSED` despite the recorded review chain |
| P1_RESOLUTION | The bound is parameterized over `C_eff` = the maximum number of simultaneously live, distinct request identities able to produce a counted signal — exactly the C of the window accounting. Every live obligation holds one uint32 `SlotIndex` (`request_key.hpp:15-18`) and simultaneously-live obligations hold pairwise-distinct values (bijection fill, single-token pop/push, retirement never re-pools — `request_core.cpp:18/118/565`), so `C_eff` is at most the `SlotIndex` domain size, `2^32`, by representation; BOUND-01 excludes the identity-domain-overflow configurations from validity. Therefore `3*C_eff + 2 <= 3*2^32 + 2 < 2^34 << UINT64_MAX` for every valid configuration — an identity-domain bound, not a machine-RAM heuristic |
| BOUND-01_DEBT | Recorded as `IDENTITY_DOMAIN_DEBT` in the re-closure record above: uring enforces the identity-domain bound today, the `RequestCore`/threadpool admission path does not; the V24 bound neither claims nor needs that enforcement |
| REVIEWER_A_BOUND_RECHECK | **FORMAL_SOUND** — dispatched specifically on the parameterized bound; independently verified the uint32 representation, the one-token-per-slot pool discipline, BOUND-01's wording, the arithmetic (`3*2^32 + 2 = 12,884,901,890 < 2^34`), the comment-only diff (SANY clean, TLC re-runs byte-identical) and consistency with the surrounding window accounting. Findings: P2 (the model comment cited the ledger debt before this record existed, and the `POST_EXHAUSTION_BOUND` row still carried the superseded justification) — resolved by this very record; P3 (the injectivity premise was compressed into "by representation alone") — accepted, the premise is now spelled out in the model comment |

## D1 review record — Issue #398, bounded RequestScope / W-02 structured settlement

Stage-local status: `D1_VERIFIED` (HUMAN_FINAL_REVIEW=APPROVE). This record claims the
D1 slice only: the minimum bounded `RequestScope` required by HOST-02 —
bounded preallocated tracking, reserve-before-acceptance, a noexcept and
allocation-free post-accept responsibility commit, owned-driver settlement
over the existing C2 progress contract, and every-exit cleanup with the
construction-selected policy. It delivers the W-02 tracer bullet and the
V18/V19 counterexamples as deterministic C++ evidence. It closes no
#399 Scheduler/Fiber redesign, no #400 cancel/effect closure, no #401
shutdown/poison state machine, and no external-host retained-state half
(#397's W-03 contract).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | HOST-02 (bounded owned-driver scope: preallocated tracking budget, reserve-before-acceptance, every-exit settlement, cleanup policy selected at construction, no driver stealing), W-02 (bounded pipeline with short I/O, admission failure, independent results, best-effort cancel), CANCEL-02 (deadline bounds initial waiting, never settlement or borrow release), BOUND-01/02 (scope tracking budget with rejection before the tracking bound; capacity visible), THREAD-01/02 (fixed progress owner; no recursive drive; caller-thread confinement), HANDLE-02 (low-level Request nonterminal-drop fail-fast untouched), VERIFY-02 S7, VERIFY-04 V18/V19. Explicitly excluded: #399 stackful host, #400 cancel/effect matrix (cancel retryable/unsupported dispositions), #401 shutdown/poison and shutdown-outcome reporting, W-04, external-host retained state, any observer mechanism inside the scope |
| Authority map (D1-0 audit) | Request terminal/result: RequestCore (unchanged). Public settlement responsibility: `Request<T>`, transferred into the scope at the noexcept commit. Progress owner: the context's `ProgressOwner`, claimed by the scope at construction (no second owner mechanism). File/buffer storage: caller (borrows only). Scope tracking capacity and its reservation: RequestScope (new authority, mandated by HOST-02). Cancel physical result: backend/core. Observer lifecycle: unused by the scope (OBS-04 N/A for this path; S7 covered by the exception/unwind evidence). A context-level rejection after reservation leaves no borrow, no acceptance, and (per the review fix) no reservation |
| Implementation | Base `291c8ec54c33a09306720cda1e6ee217edda9030` (live master at start). Branch `feat/398-bounded-request-scope`, commits `6416fcd2` (substrate), `34afb4ef` (tracer + V18/V19 evidence + mutation builds), `a3fc4a66` (`wait_for(nanoseconds::max())` overflow guard), `cfe73f1e` (review fix: reservation rollback + wait-capability setup validation), `e91f6174` (human-review P1-1: reservation rollback when the submission throws), `a6bb052f` (human-review P1-2: `finish()` reports unconsumed operation errors), `6b64adea` (human-review P2: saturating park deadlines, scope + narrow C2 corrective), `2612c4cd` (human-review round-2 P1: explicit finish no longer invokes the cleanup cancel). New public surface: `RequestScope(AsyncIoContext&, capacity, ScopeCleanupPolicy)` (non-movable), `ScopeTicket<T>` (trivially copyable RequestId copy), `submit_read/submit_write/submit_sync_data/submit_sync_all` → `Result<ScopeTicket<T>>`, `take(ticket)` → `RequestObservation<T>` (nonblocking consume, frees the slot), `wait_for(ticket, duration)` → `Result<ScopeWaitStatus{ready, timeout, interrupted, health_failure}>`, `finish()` → `Result<void>` (idempotent; reports the first unconsumed operation error), `capacity()`. Two new fail-fast diagnostics (`request_scope_settlement_driver_fail_fast`, `request_scope_settlement_health_fail_fast`). Files: `include/sluice/async/request_scope.hpp`, `src/async/request_scope.cpp`, fail_fast additions, `tests/request_scope_test.cpp`, xmake block; the P2 round additionally touches the shared wait surface `src/async/async_io_context.cpp` and `include/sluice/async/detail/progress_source.hpp` with the one-line saturating deadline computation |
| Protocol (D1-A frozen) | Slot state model: `free → reserved → owning → free` (scope-membership fact; terminal/readiness/results are never mirrored — every check queries RequestCore live through `Request<T>`). Submit protocol: reserve a free slot → construct the `Reservation` guard (so the guard already exists when the submission runs) → call the public `ctx.submit_*(op)` → a rejection or an exception both unwind through the guard (slot back to `free`, nothing accepted) → acceptance commits by in-place nothrow variant emplace (`static_assert` nothrow move) then the `owning` state store; the ticket mints from the key captured before the move. Every step after context acceptance is noexcept and allocation-free; no user code, allocation, or failure point exists between acceptance and scope ownership. Settlement (`finish`/destructor) takes the cancellation decision as a call-path argument, because HOST-02 gives the two exits different shapes: explicit `finish()` is a normal exit — it never cancels, drives every owning slot to its natural public terminal, and reports the first unconsumed operation error verbatim (including a `canceled` terminal an external control path produced); destructor/unwind cleanup is the implicit exit — it applies the construction-selected policy (best-effort `cancel()` on nonterminal slots for `cancel_then_drain`) before draining, and ignores the report. Both paths share the drive loop (`poll_progress` bounded pass, then unbounded `wait_one`; control acknowledged and settlement continues; driver rejection or `deadline_expired` under an unbounded wait = driver fail-fast; `health_failure` = settlement-health fail-fast) and the observation pass (each ready slot's `take_result` is observed, the first error recorded, every slot released). Error reporting never retains responsibility: every request is settled and released whether or not an error is reported |
| Capacity and allocation audit | Scope capacity chosen at construction (zero rejected as `std::invalid_argument` before the owner claim; slots backing storage is one bounded construction-time allocation). `ALLOCATIONS_DURING_SCOPE_CONSTRUCTION=1 (the bounded slot array)`, `ALLOCATIONS_DURING_TRACK_RESERVE=0`, `ALLOCATIONS_AFTER_ACCEPT_BEFORE_SCOPE_OWNERSHIP=0`, `ALLOCATIONS_PER_ACCEPTED_REQUEST=0`. Runtime evidence: the suite's global allocation counter asserts zero allocations across a full submit (reserve + acceptance + commit), consumption/slot release, and capacity rejection; the ThreadPool submit path is ring-based (no allocation). No historical growth: `std::unique_ptr<Slot[]>`, never resized; ticket staleness derives from the core's existing generation discipline (no new epoch) |
| Mechanism justification | `Slot::State` — the scope's own reserve→commit→consume lifecycle that the core cannot see (the reservation deliberately precedes the core acceptance transaction); the `reserve_after_accept` mutant proves the execution that fails without it. `finished_` — not derivable from an empty slot set (a fresh scope is also empty but must admit); refuses post-finish submissions and makes finish idempotent. `policy_`/`owner_`/preallocated `slots_` — directly mandated by HOST-02's text. `ScopeTicket<T>` — a non-authoritative copy of the existing core identity (THREAD-01 blesses copied IDs); generation discipline stays owned by the core. `ScopeWaitStatus` — projection of `ProgressWaitOutcome` plus live readiness; no new semantic fact. The two fail-fast functions — the house one-fact diagnostic idiom. Deliberately not added: scope mutex/atomics (caller-thread confined), thread-id field, background thread, busy-poll fallback, observer state, cancel-intent or completion mirrors, task records, `std::function`, type-erased request storage (the variant is the closed two-shape v1 operation set) |
| Semantic/regression evidence | `tests/request_scope_test.cpp`: 25/25 ThreadPool, 16/16 io_uring (the eight worker-occupancy/gate-stalled forms are ThreadPool-only; see limitations). | Known limitations | Health failure during cleanup fails fast rather than returning (the documented D1 boundary; the #401 shutdown/poison state machine owns any recoverable form). Destructor cleanup records nothing: an unconsumed operation error surfaces only through explicit `finish()` or through `take` before settlement — during unwinding the original exception is preserved by design. `finish()` never cancels: an operation that has not run when finish is called settles naturally and its real outcome is what finish reports. `finish()` reports only the first unconsumed operation error of that call and a later `finish()` reports success (responsibility is already fully released; per-call report, no stored error state). Cancellation initiated by the scope exists only on the destructor/unwind path under `cancel_then_drain`; its report is ignored there by design. Cross-thread scope use is undetected until it reaches a context gate. The V19/sticky-control/cancel-won/health/near-max-stall deterministic forms are ThreadPool-seam forms (see backend evidence). `wait_for` returning `interrupted` leaves control sticky by design; acknowledgement is the caller's owner action |
| Status | `D1_VERIFIED` — implemented, independently reviewed (two blinded agent reviewers, verdicts above), human review rounds 1 and 2 returned REQUEST_CHANGES with all four findings accepted and re-evidenced (rounds 1–2 at `6b64adea`, round 2 at `2612c4cd`), and the human final review returned HUMAN_FINAL_REVIEW=APPROVE; the merge of #447 and the closure of #398 were authorized and executed immediately after this docs-only record |

## D2 review record — Issue #399, narrow stackful host / W-04 support decision

Stage-local status: `D2_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW`. This record claims the D2
slice only: the SUPPORT_W04 decision for the optional stackful host
(`StackfulIoHost`/`IoTaskContext`), its single-owner composition with the C2
progress contract, the HOST-03 failure matrix evidence, and the W-04 tracer on
both backends. It closes no #400 cancel/effect matrix, no #401
shutdown/poison state machine, no W-03 external-loop retained state, and no
legacy-runtime retirement (#402 owns that).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| D2-0 decision | `SUPPORT_W04`. Every blocking condition for a legitimate DEFER is absent: the contracts the host needs are frozen (public `Request<T>` B2, the C2 progress owner/wait/ack/interrupt protocol, the fiber substrate with sanitizer annotations); no new public contract beyond HOST-01's delegated names is required; cancel dispositions are consumed as-is and health failure during driving fails fast (the same boundary D1 recorded). Kill gate: **Model A** — the host is the sole `ProgressOwner` for `run()`'s duration; task-local settlement is the host's completed-return await helper itself (the HOST-03/OBS-04 form); `RequestScope` keeps its owned-driver meaning unchanged and its construction inside a host task fails the owner claim (observed as `std::runtime_error` by the task). No delegated-driver mode, borrowed owner, or RequestScope semantics change was introduced, so no root amendment was needed |
| Requirement scope | HOST-01 (optional stackful host: launch/join/error/stop, bounded; no hidden threads), HOST-02 (stackful cleanup under the host driver contract — the D1 scope composes unchanged), HOST-03 (task failure/stop/unwind matrix, teardown never discards suspended stacks with active borrows), W-04 (stackful pipeline tracer), PROG-01/02/04 (single owner, entry-bounded passes, composed health, expired-deadline-never-cancels), OBS-01/04 (allocation-free polling wake; post-acceptance storage with no allocation, no throwing registration, and no fallible observation setup before suspension), CANCEL-01/02 (the host never initiates cancellation: stop and deadline expiry leave accepted requests their responsibility, settle-before-exit by observation; dispositions consumed as-is), THREAD-01/02 (cross-thread stop latch + task token; the host writes nothing into the context control plane; no new shared state), BOUND-01 (task/stack budget with visible rejection), ARCH-01/02 (core-only firewall; host above IoContext only), HANDLE-02/LIFE (borrow released at settlement; live-task teardown fail-fast), MIG-02 (dispositions recorded, nothing deleted). Excluded: #400 operation/cancel-effect matrix, #401 shutdown/poison and recoverable health, W-03 external-loop hosts, task-facing open/close/resize (blocking boundary documented in ADR §10) |
| Authority map (D2-0 audit) | Progress authority: the context's `ProgressOwner`, claimed by the host for each `run()` call (no second owner, no transfer). Task stop authority: the host's latched `stop_requested_` atomic, read by the await precheck and spawn admission; the host-level `CancelToken` is the task-visible read-only projection. Request terminal/result/borrow: RequestCore through public `Request<T>`, consumed by `take_result()` at settlement. Wake routing: `Request::ready()` polling after each progress pass (OBS-01 arm; no observer registration, no allocation). Cancellation authority: the explicit caller action alone — the host never calls `Request::cancel()` on stop or deadline expiry and never calls `interrupt_progress_waiters()`, so it plants no context control state. Task/stack capacity: `StackfulIoHost` (new authority, HOST-01 delegated). Physical completion/cancel: the backend, unchanged. Error mapping: shared errno/`bad_alloc` table (see limitations for the duplication note) |
| Implementation | Baseline `79153cac` (live master at start; #396/#397/#398 all merged). Branch `feat/399-narrow-stackful-host`, commits `df11c7e6` (ADR-0003), `2d53ea92` (host substrate: `include/sluice/async/stackful_io_host.hpp`, `src/async/stackful_io_host.cpp`, three fail-fast additions to the shared `detail` diagnostics), `bd3ad824` (evidence suite + xmake wiring), plus this record. Corrective pass after the human adjudication (see the adjudication rows): `fad06192` (host stop/deadline no-cancel contraction, control-plane decoupling, per-run error reset), `6770d4cc` (no-cancel discriminators, run-reuse and control-plane regressions, mutants M7–M9), `387bcc17` (ADR-0003 corrections), `517417c6` (stage-identifier cleanup: suite renamed to `tests/stackful_host_test.cpp`, test functions and labels de-tagged, mutant macros renamed to `SLUICE_STACKFUL_HOST_MUTANT_*`), plus this record. Final corrective evidence pass: `c4b216cf` (host `read_exact`/`write_all` conveniences over the shared composition rule, header admission-linearization contract, three mutation seams with xmake targets), `91b97f39` (the seven new evidence regressions), `94223988` (ADR-0003 authority/wording round), plus this record. Round-4 corrective pass: `dc6d5c9d` (convenience invocation-boundary refinement with the empty no-op request and `Result<CompositionOutcome>` surface, run-exit control retirement, the empty-precheck-bypass seam), `f1fc59af` (the parity oracle, the deterministic control-release discriminator, the fd-level stop control-plane observation), `4028d85b` (ADR-0003 §9/§10/§11), plus this record. Round-5 corrective pass: `b9c1e390` (first-primitive admission classification — the await helper reports whether the submission transaction accepted, so a rejection before the invocation's first acceptance rejects the whole invocation through the outer result while an accepted primitive's terminal error keeps the outcome arm — and the run-exit control release as a loop whose first completion-free pass is the release linearization point), `f627bfd9` (the two admission-classification discriminators and the external-completion exit-drain discriminator), `bfece304` (ADR-0003 §9/§10/§11), plus this record. | Semantic/regression evidence | `tests/stackful_host_test.cpp`, 28 cases (29 with liburing), both backends: pipeline tracer (7000-byte payload, 512-byte chunks: read-fill/write-all/sync_data chain; asserts byte count, destination content via `/proc/self/fd`, zero outstanding, zero live tasks, zero await links, owner reclaimable; io_uring variant runs on the real kernel, no skip); HOST-03 matrix slot 1 — task `system_error(ENOENT)` while a sibling task's gated I/O is outstanding (run returns `not_found`, sibling's 200-byte copy still settles); slot 3 — sibling observes the reserved await state (`== 1`) while the gated read is suspended; slot 4 — deadline expiry does not cancel (one-worker pool with an occupier task pins the awaited read undispatched behind a claimed worker: the 50ms deadline expires with the operation still queued, no cancel is initiated, and the helper returns the real 48-byte result when the queued operation runs — a reinstated expiry-cancel would win before execution and return canceled); slot 5 — exception during resumed processing (post-read throw maps to `backend_error`, prior read settled); slot 6 — stop racing publication and retirement (`WorkerOutcomePreTerminalPauseGate`: exactly-once body entry, the computed outcome publishes unchanged, delivery never touches retired storage); run-reuse regression (a failing run reports its own error and the next run succeeds — no cross-run error accumulator leakage); stop control-plane discriminator (a stop landing while the driver is inside a task, so the run never waits, writes nothing to the notification domain — observed directly as notification-fd readability at stop time through a `progress_notification_fd` borrow retired by `detach_progress_host` — and leaves the next progress owner a clean idle `wait_one`); progress-owner composition (external owner blocks `run()` with `invalid_state` before any task executes — probe task provably never ran; RequestScope inside a task throws and is caught; concurrent claim during `run()` rejected; owner released after `run()`); capacity/stop bounds; capability rejection with a non-signaling `NullBackend`; fork-based evidence that destroying the host with a spawned unretired task fails fast; nested `run()` from a task rejected; await after stop rejects before acceptance with the pause gate proving no physical claim. Final corrective round additions: the accepted-undispatched stop discriminator (a test-owned occupier pins the only worker; the victim's read is accepted and paused pre-dispatch by the accepted-pre-dispatch gate; stop lands strictly after acceptance and strictly before dispatch/claim; after release the victim executes naturally and reports its real 48-byte result, the occupier its real 8 — kills the stop-implicit-cancel mutant); the expired-deadline park bound (one-worker occupancy holds physical completion 150ms past a 50ms `_for` deadline; the victim still settles on its real result, and the context's existing `AsyncStats` poll/wait counters stay ≤64/≤256 — orders of magnitude below any zero-duration spin — kills the expired-deadline-spin mutant); the external-control acknowledgement regression (the prepark pause proves the driver committed to a park with the notification fd drained; control planted next is either reported by the next wait or consumed by a progress return and retired at run exit — both legal paths leave the next owner clean; the task settles naturally; a fresh `claim_progress_owner` + `wait_one` observes clean `progress`, not stale `control_interrupted` — kills the skip-control-ack mutant); nested-spawn evidence (capacity 1: the in-task spawn returns `no_space`, the parent completes, the host retires cleanly and the slot recycles; capacity 2: the child is admitted, the parent observes two live tasks and the queued child within the ring bound, the child runs exactly once strictly after the parent completes); first-task-error selection (both admission orders of immediate ENOENT/EACCES throwers pick their own first error; | Human adjudication round 1 | The human reviewer returned REQUEST_CHANGES against `6bdb64ce` with the final adjudication: `POLLING_READY_SCAN=ACCEPT` (no Observer registration required for the D2 host; `POLLING_REFINEMENT_ROOT_CONFORMING`), `ROOT_AMENDMENT=NO`, `STOP_IMPLICIT_CANCEL=REMOVE`, `DEADLINE_IMPLICIT_CANCEL=REMOVE`, `STICKY_CONTROL_LEAK=FIX`, `TASK_ERROR_CROSS_RUN_LEAK=FIX`. The reviewer held that the first campaign over-claimed implementation soundness with two P1s the blinded reviews missed: (1) the sticky-control leak — host stop called `interrupt_progress_waiters()`, planting an unacknowledged control generation; a stop landing while the run never waits leaves it unobserved, so the next progress owner receives a stale `control_interrupted` from a control it never caused (driver-epoch contamination); (2) the cross-run error leak — the first-task-error accumulator was never reset at run entry, so a failed run made every later all-success run return the stale error. The reviewer further adjudicated that neither cancellation question was a root ambiguity: PROG-04 ("an expired deadline does not cancel I/O") and CANCEL-02 ("operation cancellation is a separate explicit action"; a completed-return deadline bounds initial waiting, not total return latency) already decide them, so the implementation contracts to the root instead of the root expanding. All findings ACCEPTED and fixed at `fad06192`/`6770d4cc` exactly as adjudicated: host stop and deadline expiry no longer cancel accepted requests; request_stop no longer writes the context control plane (stop latency is natural-settlement-bound); the accumulator resets at each successful run entry; H2/H4/H6 rewritten as no-cancel discriminators; the run-reuse and control-plane regressions added; mutants M7–M9 added and killed; ADR-0003 and this record corrected (the nonexistent PROG-05 citation fixed to PROG-04; authority map, census deletions, bounds wording, limitations). The fix is a mechanism contraction: `cancel_suspended_awaited_`, `sweep_expired_deadlines_`, `AwaitLink::cancel_fn`/`cancel_initiated`, and the host progress interrupt are deleted, and the first campaign's deferral of H4 cancel-arm determinism is resolved by the new undispatched-window discriminator |

All ACCEPTED and fixed at `dc6d5c9d`/`f1fc59af`/`4028d85b`: the surface is now `Result<blocking::CompositionOutcome>` with the invocation boundary structurally identical to the direct forms (the shared `precheck_data_op` oracle on file state/access/offset/length, the null-buffer guard, and host-stop admission rejection through `canceled`), so semantic and admission rejections surface as outer errors with nothing accepted while per-primitive failures keep the confirmed prefix in the outcome. During the round-4 matrix re-run, the release leg additionally exposed a pre-existing legal-interleaving violation of the round-1 sticky-control discipline the earlier evidence had passed only probabilistically (~14% of release runs; the round-3 release leg's 6 green runs were luck): `wait_one` runs a progress pass after an `interrupted` wake, and a reaped completion there returns `progress` before the control is ever observed — a host whose last task retires on that wake exits `run()` and releases the owner with unacknowledged control, which the next owner receives as stale `control_interrupted`. Root-caused to that exact interleaving and fixed at the owner per the PROG-02 conceptual loop ("else if token changed or control is pending: repeat"): `run()` performs a zero-duration wait after all tasks retire, observing and retiring any control a progress return consumed (ADR §9); both acknowledgement points share the M12 seam so the mutant still means "acknowledged nowhere". The deterministic discriminator holds the driver at its prepark pause while the worker publishes, forcing the consuming interleaving (5/5 reproduction with the fix removed, green with it). The M9 kill observation moved from next-owner staleness to notification-fd readability at stop time (a direct plant observation, immune to the now-legitimate run-exit retirement of any control found at exit). Disclosed for adjudication: this last fix is one reviewable slice beyond the two adjudicated P1/P2 items, delivered because the release matrix could not be honestly reported green without it and the discipline forbids sleeps/retry-inflation/weakened assertions; error-exit control hygiene stays with the #401 health/shutdown domain. The prior rounds' records are preserved as history, including the round-3 `NO_NEW_LOW_LEVEL_REQUEST` closure (unchanged by this round: the boundary reuses the existing oracle and submission transaction, no new validation authority) and the round-3 result-payload wording that this round corrected |
| Human adjudication round 5 (admission classification and release linearization) | The human reviewer returned `PR_449 = REQUEST_CHANGES` (P0=0, P1=2, P2=0) with `ROUND4_ORDERED_P1 = CLOSED`, `ROUND4_ORDERED_P2 = CLOSED`, `D2_HOST_CORE_PROTOCOL = PASS`, `SEM01_EMPTY_AND_PUBLIC_SHAPE = PASS`, `SEM01_ADMISSION_LAYERING = INCOMPLETE`, `PROG02_OWNER_RELEASE_HYGIENE = INCOMPLETE`, `MERGE_READY = NO`, `#399 = KEEP_OPEN`, `#400 = DO_NOT_START`, at head `9aeee71a` (Draft/open, CI #602 green), accepting the round-4 empty-fix and `Result<CompositionOutcome>` surface, the closed/access/range precedence, the empty no-op admission, M13, the control-leak diagnosis and the M9/M12 re-scoping, the 14% disclosure and the sanitizer matrix. P1-1: a nonempty first primitive rejected by the submission transaction (capacity exhausted, admission rejection) was still folded into the outcome's primitive-error arm as `Result` success with confirmed 0 — ERR-01 requires admission rejection (no accepted request) to surface as the outer `Result` error, and the fix must not be the naive `confirmed_bytes == 0` rule, which would wrongly reject an accepted first primitive failing terminally before any byte; the classification must come from the submit/terminal distinction the await path already knows. P1-2: the round-4 exit fix performed a single `wait_one(0)`, but THREAD-01 lets other threads submit context operations during `run()`, so a completion of such a request can be reaped by the exit wait — returning `progress` before the control check — and the owner then releases with the control unobserved; the release must have a linearization point at which pre-release pending control has been observed/retired even when progress repeatedly precedes control, and not a constant additional count of waits. Both fixed at `b9c1e390`/`f627bfd9`/`bfece304`: the await helper gained a host-internal accepted report (out-parameter, no public payload, no new request kind), and the composition classifies positionally — a rejection before this invocation's first acceptance goes to the outer `Result`; an accepted primitive's terminal error, and any failure after any acceptance, report through the outcome's primitive-error arm with the confirmed prefix. The exit release repeats the zero-duration wait while passes keep reaping completions; the first completion-free pass is the release linearization point (no control pending, or the latest generation observed and acknowledged — control arriving after that observation belongs to the next owner). Evidence as ordered: the paired deterministic discriminators (capacity-rejected nonempty first primitive → outer `would_block`, nothing accepted, no kernel operation; accepted first primitive terminal error → outer success with `primitive_error`, confirmed 0, injection fired once, no syscall) and the external-completion exit-drain discriminator (external zero-length request published and control planted inside the last task's epilogue on the driver thread, forcing the exit pass's progress-first return; next owner observes clean `progress`). Local temporary reverts reproduced each named failure (3/3 admission folding, 3/3 naive rule on the accepted-terminal side, 5/5 single-wait exit) and were restored and re-verified. One test-construction finding during this round, recorded for accuracy: an early choreography draft parked an external thread's context submit at an intra-submit pause gate, which deadlocks by design because the context submit wrapper holds the context access mutex across the backend submit; the final choreography uses a gate-free zero-length submission signalled from the task epilogue instead. Mutation-discipline note: the first drafted drain loop (`continue` after the control observation) made the skip-control-ack mutant hang rather than fail named; the loop was restructured to exit on the control observation itself — observation of the latest generation is itself the linearization point — restoring the named kill before the matrix was recorded |
| Independent final-PR review and corrective (post-round-5) | Final repair and whole-PR adversarial review at frozen heads. Known round-5 defect fixed first at `c1cd5c76`: the composition primitive lambdas had moved from span slicing to raw pointer arithmetic in round 5, so the admitted zero-length request's `primitive(0)` step formed `nullptr + 0` on a legitimately null buffer — repaired by restoring `subspan` slicing (no arithmetic on a potentially-null empty-span pointer; behavior-preserving on every reachable path, so the existing empty-parity rows are the regression; a clang `pointer-overflow` probe confirmed neither toolchain sanitizer observes the form). Frozen-head matrix at `c1cd5c76`: debug liburing=n 28/28; release liburing=n 28/28 ×5; debug liburing=y 29/29; releasedbg liburing=y 29/29 ×5; ASan+UBSan clang 29/29 ×3 zero reports (halt_on_error + LeakSanitizer); TSan clang 29/29 ×3 zero reports; all thirteen mutants killed on named discriminators (M1–M3/M7–M9/M11–M13 exit 1; M4–M6 exit 134; M10 exit 1-or-134 with the identical named discriminator on both sides of `c1cd5c76` — the mode is the cancel/dispatch race inside the mutant run, not the repair); both core-only probes and `uring_public_consumer_probe` pass; `git diff --check` and `scripts/check_cpp_comment_authority.py` clean. Four blinded whole-PR reviews against the frozen head (subagent budget capped at four, so the reviewer set was consolidated: A+D semantic authority + composition parity, B C++/memory/lifetime, C progress/control concurrency, G unprimed open bug hunt; the evidence-adversary mutation campaign and the mechanism-contraction audit were run by the coordinating agent): A+D `AUTHORITY_CLEAN`/`COMPOSITION_REFINEMENT_SOUND` with the full parity matrix re-proved (4 P3 style/evidence notes recorded, none accepted as changes); B `CPP_LIFETIME_SOUND` (fiber/stack/entry-destruction/ring/atomics/empty-span sweep, no findings); C `PROGRESS_CONTROL_FINDINGS` and G `NEW_COUNTEREXAMPLE` each independently found the same exit-release defect the main-agent audit had also found: the release loop treated a `health_failure` outcome and a `wait_one(0)` error as clean release points, returning success on a poisoned wait domain where the main loop fails fast — reachable because the final reap pass hides fresh sticky health behind the progress-first return (production setters: uring fatal submit/overflow-flush poison, wake-fd `poll(2)` failure). Fixed at `c52ccdc2`: the release pass fails fast on the health outcome and propagates the wait error; paired regressions `exit_window_health_failure_fails_fast` (reproduces the silent success without the arm) and `zero_length_convenience_crosses_request_admission` (pins the SEM-03 admission crossing through the context submit counter — evidence-adversary mutant E16, which skips the no-op request while keeping the semantic precheck, passed the entire prior suite and is killed by this discriminator; the raw-arithmetic regression E15 remains without a runtime discriminator on this toolchain and is carried as a disclosed limitation guarded by the source-level review and the `subspan` form). Header `run()` contract now states the release deferral under unbounded external completions and the health fail-fast boundary; ADR §9 states the release pass's failure arms. New-head matrix at `c52ccdc2`: debug liburing=n 30/30; release liburing=n 30/30 ×3; debug liburing=y 31/31; releasedbg liburing=y 31/31 ×3; ASan+UBSan clang 31/31 ×3 zero reports; TSan clang 31/31 ×3 zero reports; all thirteen mutants rebuilt and killed on named discriminators; all three probes pass; the local evidence-adversary mutants re-run at the new head — single-observation release, last-error-wins, admission folding, naive zero-confirmed rule, nested-spawn disable, empty admission skip, and exit-health-arm removal all killed by named discriminators; the raw-arithmetic reintroduction (E15) and the wait-error-swallow reintroduction remain without runtime discriminators on this toolchain (the former is behaviorally identical under gcc/clang sanitizers, the latter unreachable under conforming use) and are disclosed limitations guarded by the source-level reviews, the committed `subspan` form and the ADR §9 documentation. Reviewer-rerun disclosure: the user's four-subagent budget was consumed by the initial blinded reviews, so the post-fix rerun of the affected scopes (B lifetime delta, C exit-loop trace, G fresh attacks on the delta, E mutation campaign) was executed by the coordinating agent against `c52ccdc2` and is recorded here rather than as independent subagent verdicts |
| Known limitations | Plain stacks without guard pages (same as the fiber substrate); x86_64 Linux only (`fiber_ctx::supported` rejects elsewhere); stop convergence requires the accepted operations to reach terminal states and cooperative tasks to observe the token (no operation preemption, no driver wake from stop, no bounded stop promise); a task that never awaits can defer host exit indefinitely (cooperative rule); `_for` forms bound the driver's initial park window only and never cancel — the helper returns the operation's real terminal result (PROG-04/CANCEL-02); a non-positive wait provides no effective bound (documented, spec-silent); health failure during `run()` fails fast rather than returning an error result (the #401 boundary, same as D1); the exception-mapping table is duplicated from `task_result.hpp` by decoupling necessity until #402; the clang sanitizer matrix excludes the D1 `request_scope_test` target for the pre-existing unused-variable diagnostic noted above |

## E1 implementation record — Issue #400, request-path metadata ops, effect currency, cancel refinement

Stage-local status: `E1_IMPLEMENTED_PENDING_REVIEW`. This record claims the
#400 hard-scope slice only: the six required request operations
(read_at, write_at, sync_data, sync_all, file_info, size) on both required
profiles, the shared effect-currency channel, the CANCEL-01 disposition
surface, bounded sticky cancel intent on io_uring, the required-profile
setup gate, and the #400-owned per-request physical retirement (consumption
retires every physical trace; hard-submit recovery retains the borrow of any
kernel-visible entry until its physical proof). It closes no #401
shutdown/poison state machine, no durability/sync-coverage API, no async
open/close/resize, no shared-cursor I/O, and no legacy retirement (#402 owns
that).

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Requirement scope | SEM-01 (operation matrix: file_info/size Required on the request path), SEM-07 (metadata kind/size/identity as first-class request results), ERR-02/V15 (partial effects: unknown remainder, never a fabricated zero), CANCEL-01 (dispositions including the physical_interruption_unsupported mechanism fact; bounded sticky intent), BACKEND-02 (required profiles: successful construction implies the mechanism exists for all six ops; setup failure is explicit, never a constructed-but-dead profile or a runtime not_supported), BACKEND-03 (a cancel CQE is not the original's retirement), INV-02 (submitter nonblocking maintained), REQ-02..05 (acceptance/publication/reclaim preserved through the new ops), VERIFY-04 V13/V14/V15 (V13: the cancel control retires only its own obligation — B1-C mutants still killed; V14: a raced success is never rewritten; V15: a dispatched failure reports unknown), PROD-02 (explicit unavailable outcome at setup). Excludes: async open/close/resize, shared-cursor I/O, shutdown/Destroyable/poison recovery (#401), legacy retirement (#402), await-host conveniences for metadata (they need the #399/#401 host typing work; the context-level Completion/Request surface is the canonical v1 result path) |
| Implementation | Head `9dc80d16` + round-2/round-3 corrections (branch `feat/400-e1-backend-refinement`). `include/sluice/effect.hpp` (shared `EffectReport`/`EffectCertainty`; the blocking and detail aliases now derive from it), `include/sluice/file_resource.hpp` (`FileSize` carrier; LP64 makes `Completion<uint64_t>` and `Completion<size_t>` the same type, so size needs a distinct public carrier), `request_core.hpp/.cpp` (`RequestOp::file_info/size`, metadata on `TerminalCandidate`/`PublicationPayload`/slot, metadata out-params on observe/consume), `async_io_context.hpp/.cpp` (`submit_file_info`/`submit_size` in all three families, `CancelDisposition::physical_interruption_unsupported`, `RequestObservation::effect`), `threadpool_backend.*` (fstat worker path, accounted metadata failures, disposition mapping), `uring_backend.*` (statx ops with `AT_EMPTY_PATH|AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`, one-shot setup probe with injected-failure seam, sticky control-intent service, `terminal_is_kernel_cancel`, ledger base-position rule). Human-review round-1 corrections: the `requested` cancel disposition now publishes the ProgressSource obligation so a parked owner wakes and submits/services the control; hard `io_uring_submit` failures reconcile the transport ledger against the kernel SQ head before poison — head-passed entries leave the ledger as kernel-visible and keep their real completion path, the remainder are provably invisible because no submit runs after poison (dispatch, transport submission and new admission are gated on the fatal flag; the CQ-overflow flush enter submits nothing) and take the Class-A path; construction probes the full required opcode set (`io_uring_get_probe_ring` + READ/WRITE/FSYNC/STATX) before the statx live probe. Human-review round-2 corrections: SQ-head reconciliation computes outstanding cardinality as a difference of the free-running SQ counters (`io_uring_sq_ready`) with a `≤ ring_entries` bound — masking either side before the subtraction aliased a full ring onto an empty one; the ThreadPool destructor joins the workers before its quiescence verdict (a pre-dispatch cancel win leaves a claim-failed dispatch entry only the worker lane drains) while still rejecting genuinely unsettled accepted work before the join. Human-review round-3 correction: the round-2 overflow-poison settlement (settling routes whose CQE was absent from the visible ring on an unknown remainder) was removed — absence does not prove an operation finished, so a read still executing inside the kernel would have had its borrow retired under the kernel's feet; instead the CQ-overflow flush stays live after poison (a zero-submit `io_uring_get_events` cannot consume the SQEs whose invisibility the Class-A proof established) and routes without a delivered CQE keep their borrows and stay pending under the failed health, retiring with their real outcomes once the flush recovers or the operation completes |
| Change | Current: file_info/size absent from the request path; effect certainty dropped at the public boundary; ThreadPool running-cancel reported `requested` although no mechanism can interrupt a claimed worker; io_uring lost a cancel whose control SQE could not be enqueued; io_uring setup degraded silently on ring/probe failure; byte-op failures reported accounted zero on io_uring while ThreadPool reported unknown. Target: the six-op required matrix on both profiles with one shared semantic suite; effect currency observable on every terminal; cancel dispositions report the backend mechanism fact; sticky intent is serviced, never dropped; setup failures throw. Compatibility: the new virtuals are breaking for AsyncBackend implementors (all in-tree and test backends updated); `physical_interruption_unsupported` is a new enum value; no existing legal interleaving changes outcome except the two corrected defects above |

Round-3 discriminator: `overflow_flush_failure_does_not_release_still_inflight_borrow` (R3, mixed state at a flush-failure poison: three completed reads — two ring-visible, one parked in the overflow — plus one still executing with the kernel holding its buffer; the poison publishes nothing for the executing read, which stays pending with its borrow; after the failure clears and its pipe is written, the real four-byte accounted outcome surfaces, proving the buffer was never released — the same test also re-verifies `overflow_flush_failure_becomes_observable_health_event`'s corrected contract: visible completions retire with real outcomes at poison time and the parked one retires through the recovered flush) |

TSan clean on both conformance variants and the focused cutover/public/progress/race set |

The uring conformance variant exercises the real ring for every shared test and the deterministic submit fiction only for the claimed/stuck control windows (running cancel, V14 injection order, sticky intent, hard-submit partial consumption, full-ring zero consumption, wrapped-head partial consumption); the fiction's fake kernel head advances as a free-running consumed counter matching the real kernel's shared `sq.head` protocol; `URING_REAL_KERNEL=yes`, `URING_LIBURING=2.14`, `URING_FILESYSTEM=tmpfs`. Transfer-limit disclosure (SEM-04): a submitted read/write carries one SQE length clamped to `numeric_limits<unsigned>::max()` by `uring_chunk_length`; the kernel may transfer less (its own single-op bound included); whatever the kernel accepted surfaces as the confirmed byte count with accounted certainty, so the supported range restriction is disclosed by the execution itself and documented here — no chunked resubmission exists and none is claimed. Retirement scoping: per-request physical retirement (BACKEND-03 per-operation reference accounting; truthful publication and effect) is #400 scope and is claimed here; global shutdown/poison/Destroyable is #401; legacy runtime retirement is #402. Human review round 1 returned `PR_450 = REQUEST_CHANGES` (P0=0, P1=2, P2=2; ThreadPool/effect-currency/arbitration/metadata accepted; both P1s were the io_uring cancel-progress obligation and the hard-submit batch-visibility assumption); all four findings are corrected with the discriminators above. Human review round 2 accepted the common semantics, ThreadPool refinement, cancel-progress, required-capability gate and scope assignment, and the partial-submit model, with one implementation blocker: the full-ring cardinality alias — corrected with R1/R2 and `e1_conformance_mut_masked_sq_cardinality`. The remote-CI blocker (run #610, `backend_conformance_e1_threadpool_test` dying with a bare `terminate` after 7 of 13 tests) is attributed as a branch defect, not infrastructure or flake: the same signature reproduced locally on two CPUs within one to seven iterations at both the round-1 head and this head, a core backtrace pinned it to `threadpool_non_quiescent_destruction_fail_fast` from the ThreadPoolBackend destructor with the core fully idle (`dispatch=1, accepted_live=0`), and the cause is the destructor sampling the worker lane's claim-failed residue before the join — fixed by joining before the verdict, with evidence of 120 debug and 200 release two-CPU stress iterations clean at this head against 1–7-iteration reproduction before it. The same full-suite run exposed a second branch defect at the round-1 head: a flush-failure poison stranded completions parked in the kernel overflow list behind the gated flush (`uring_progress_race_test` died on its nonterminal reset at 9dc80d16 and at this head before the correction); the round-2 fix for it (an absence-based settlement) is itself removed by the round-3 correction below. Human review round 3 (narrow scope: SQ reconciliation, submit fiction, R1/R2, CI #610/#611 attribution) passed all four requested items and closed the round-2 full-ring blocker and the remote-CI blocker, but rejected the round-2 overflow-poison settlement as a P0 borrow-lifetime violation: absence from the visible CQ ring is not a per-request retirement proof, because an operation still executing inside the kernel is absent for exactly the same reason as a parked completion — settling it publishes a fabricated terminal and retires a borrow the kernel still walks. Corrected by removing the settlement and keeping the CQ-overflow flush live after poison (a zero-submit enter cannot consume the poison-proven-invisible SQEs; dispatch/transport/admission stay gated so no submit ever runs again), so routes without a delivered CQE keep their borrows and stay pending under the failed health and retire with their real outcomes once the flush recovers or the operation completes — the root-authorized unresolved-health behavior, with full poison recovery left to #401. Discriminators: `overflow_flush_failure_does_not_release_still_inflight_borrow` (mixed completed/parked/executing state), with `uring_progress_race_mut_overflow_absence_settles_inflight` (killed only by the mixed-state test, exactly as the reviewer predicted for all-completed worlds) and `uring_progress_race_mut_post_poison_flush_gated` (the gated-flush stranding class) as mutants. TLC not re-run: the correction is backend-local and RequestCore state, transitions, arbitration and accept/reclaim predicates are untouched. Not claimed: shutdown/poison (#401), durability and sync-coverage APIs (#401), await-host metadata conveniences (#399/#401), legacy retirement (#402); nothing is merged |
